- [BUILD-BACKEND-3] The LLVM release path takes the cost out of a cold build without changing any object's identity: `zig build` prebuilds the runtime archive for each backend flavor (`qbe`, `llvm`, `llvm-lto`, `llvm-thinlto`), keyed so a QBE archive can never be linked into an LLVM build nor full-LTO bitcode into a ThinLTO one, and a cold `mylang build` links the matching installed archive instead of recompiling the runtime. The guest units of one LLVM build that missed the cache are translated in process and compiled concurrently (LLVM-ZIG-1), every call site declaring its callees from their own headers, so the order units finish in never changes a declaration. Each unit still lands as its own content-addressed object, a cache hit still starts no tool, and a failed unit stops new work and is reported, with every other failure in unit order, only after every worker has stopped.
- [BUILD-BACKEND-4] An unchanged `mylang build` whose published executable still matches every input keeps it and relinks nothing, so a warm build does not pay the LTO link again. The decision is conservative: a `--provider`, `--link`, `--link-library` or `--library-path` disables it, and the executable, every cached object and the runtime archive must still be on disk. The sidecar beside the executable records a link identity over the object set, runtime archive, artifact kind, mode, target and symbol policy, and reuse requires an exact match.
- [USE-WITHOUT-PYTHON-1] Using the installed compiler needs no Python or any other interpreter: `check`, `build`, the executable it writes, `run`, `test`, `fmt-project --check`, `docs`, `index-project`, `context`, and the LLVM release path — `build --release`, the executable it writes and `run --release` (LLVM-ZIG-1) — all succeed on a two-module project in an environment whose `PATH` names only an empty directory, given nothing but `--zig` and `--qbe` (only `--zig` for the release path).
- [PACKAGES-LOCK-1] `mylang packages lock CONFIG --output LOCK` reads `{"packages":[{"name":...,"root":...}]}`, requires every name to be a unique ASCII identifier outside the reserved `std_` prefix, resolves each root against the configuration's directory, inventories every `.my` source under it (at most 50,000 walked entries and 16 MiB, refusing a symbolic-link source or directory and any source outside `name.my` and `name/`), and writes a `package-lock-v1` file naming each resolved absolute root and each module's SHA-256; an invalid, reserved or repeated namespace, a missing root or a package with no source exits 2 with a `packages:` line and writes nothing.
- [PACKAGES-BUILD-1] `mylang packages build ENTRY --lock LOCK [--command check-project|run-project|index-project|emit-project-qbe] [--receipt FILE]` re-inventories the entry's directory and every locked package and exits 2 before anything compiles on a package whose files changed since locking, a namespace locked twice, a module path the application and a package share, a module named like a standard one (`std_`) or a symbolic-link source; otherwise it copies the re-hashed bytes and the nearest `mylang.json` into a private directory under `TMPDIR`, runs this executable's `project-dependencies` and then the command there (`check-project` by default), forwards the command's exit status (for `run-project`, with `--print-result`, the program's own), writes neither the application tree nor a package root, and removes the staging directory. `--receipt` writes `package-build-v1` (the SHA-256 of the lock's compact JSON, this compiler's identity and the dependency report) once the snapshot checks and before the command runs, and a refused build leaves an earlier receipt untouched.
- [QBE-SYMBOLS-1] A native build links its executable without a symbol table by default and keeps it when `--keep-symbols` is named, so a profile build can attribute `perf report` samples to guest functions while the shipping shape, its bytes and the reuse decision on an existing output are unchanged. The switch is a build control, not a runtime one: it is rejected with a value, is absent from `--run` argument forwarding, and changes no generated code.
- [QBE-RUNTIME-MERGE-1] The fixed runtime translation units compile into the archive's single member through one `zig build-obj` invocation naming every source, so a cold build pays one toolchain start rather than one per unit. The relocatable merge keeps each unit compiled with function and data sections, so the final `--gc-sections` link still drops runtime a program never reaches.
- [QBE-STRIP-1] Native output carries no debug information: every C compile and assembly passes `-g0`, the Zig runtime unit is built with `-fstrip`, and the link strips the symbol table, so a cached object, a runtime archive and a published executable hold only the code and data the program runs. The one exception is a build that asks for `--keep-symbols`, which keeps the symbol table so a profiler can name functions and still emits no debug information. Native traps, their frames and their structured detail come from the runtime's own tables, so a stripped program reports exactly what an unstripped one did.
- [QBE-GENERATION-1] A cache root keeps one live generation per artifact family: objects under `objects/<hash>`, `mylang run` programs under `run/<hash>` and the Zig test suites' guest executables under `exe/<hash>`, each named by the native driver identity and the bundled runtime hash. Tool stamps, mode and target stay in each entry's key but not in the generation, so every process of one compiler build shares one generation whatever path it resolved its tools from or mode it asked for, and never evicts a concurrent sibling's entries. Opening a generation removes every sibling generation in that family that no process still leases, so a changed driver or runtime never leaves its predecessors' artifacts behind; an existing generation is reused without a sweep, the shared staging folder is never swept, and eviction is best effort so a cache that cannot be swept still serves the generation it keeps.
- [NATIVE-GENERATION-LEASE-1] Every process that opens a generation directory for use holds a shared advisory lock on a `.lease` file inside it for the rest of its life, and a sweeper takes a non-blocking exclusive lock on each sibling's lease before evicting it and skips whatever it cannot take. Concurrent builds under one cache root — the compiler and the host applications, whose driver identities differ — therefore never delete a generation another process is still compiling or linking into. Leasing is advisory and best effort: an entry with no lease file is still swept, a filesystem that cannot lock behaves as before, and the kernel releases a dead process's lease, so no abandoned lease pins a generation and no wall clock is consulted.
- [NATIVE-PUBLISH-REPAIR-1] Publication into a generation heals a lost eviction race instead of failing it: a rename that reports the destination missing recreates and re-leases the generation directory and retries once, and a link whose cached input objects are no longer on disk rebuilds them into a restored generation and retries the link once. A link that fails with every input present is the program's own error and is reported unchanged.
- [QBE-DRIVER-IDENTITY-1] The object and executable caches key on a *native driver identity* — a build-time digest over the native build driver and link-policy sources (`src/qbe_build*.zig`, `src/native_link*.zig`) and the frame-map annotator that rewrites every unit's assembly (`src/qbe_frame_map.zig`), the Zig version and the optimize mode — rather than the whole compiler identity. Objects are already content addressed by the QBE IL the frontend emitted, the runtime sources by the bundled content hash, the tools by their stamps and the link flags by the cache contract, so a frontend or checker edit that cannot change how IL becomes an object no longer evicts every cached guest object and executable. The compiler identity is unchanged and still keys frontend/semantic caches and `mylang test` result reuse.
- [QBE-SPAWN-1] A native build starts every tool (QBE, the assembler, the C compiler, the linker, each clang compile of the LLVM arm) with `clone(CLONE_VM | CLONE_VFORK)` instead of `fork`, so a start costs the same whatever the build process holds: the child resets every installed signal handler to the default, points standard input and output at `/dev/null` and standard error at the build's own or at a private pipe, restores the signal mask and executes the tool, named by path or found on its environment's `PATH`, in the environment the request names or, when it names none, this process's own (read back from `/proc/self/environ`), with `ZIG_PROGRESS` removed. A tool that cannot be executed is refused at start (`FileNotFound` for a missing one) with nothing left to reap; a reaped tool reports its exit status or signal and everything it wrote to standard error.
- [HOST-FAILURE-CAUSE-1] A build tool refused by `execve` with `E2BIG` fails as `host failure: ArgumentListTooLong`, not `Unexpected`; any other errno the spawn error set has no name for is written to standard error as `mylang: <call> [tool] failed: errno <NAME>` before the generic failure, so every tool-start failure names its cause.
- [QBE-BATCH-1] The QBE arm compiles guest functions in batches. Units are ordered by module (the qualified name before its last `::`) and then symbol; a batch never spans two modules, starts at every symbol whose hash marks a boundary (one in 8 on average) and holds at most 128 functions (`MYLANG_QBE_BATCH=N` caps it, `1` compiles every function alone), and a unit that defines data compiles alone. One QBE run takes each member's IL as its own input file with every function placed in its own `.text.<symbol>` section, so `--gc-sections` still drops an unreferenced function; the assembly is frame-map annotated and assembled once, by Zig's bundled LLVM assembler invoked directly (`zig clang -cc1as`). The object is content addressed by the ordered keys of its members, so a changed function, driver, runtime, tool, mode or target misses exactly as a per-function object did, and an unchanged batch starts no tool. Missed batches build concurrently, as many at once as the cores the process may use and the machine's free memory at 256 MiB per tool allow; after the first failure no new batch starts, every running tool is still reaped and every scratch directory removed. `--report` still lists every function with its own key, now naming its batch's object, and adds `function_objects` and `compiled_function_objects`.
- [QBE-BATCH-3] A batched QBE build keeps each metadata group's function metadata (QBE-BATCH-6) — every function's `MylQbeFunction` record with its location, message and inline tables — in an object of its own, assembled from generated assembly that spells the same six-, three- and five-word records as the C unit (`qbe_runtime.h` asserts their sizes) and keyed by what that text is rendered from (BUILD-METADATA-KEY-1), so an edit that moves one module's lines reassembles that module's tables and nothing else. Each function's tables share one `.data.rel.ro.<symbol>_meta` section, so the final link keeps or drops them with the function, and every distinct string is stored once in a mergeable string section. The program-wide metadata C unit keeps the runtime descriptors, host bridges and literals and names no function (QBE-BATCH-4); it and the entry adapter compile in the same concurrent pool as the batches.
- [BUILD-MEMORY-1] A batched QBE build stages each missed batch by reading, size-checking and writing one member's sectioned text at a time, removes a staged batch whose QBE never started, and reads the assembly it annotates and the object it fingerprints through a scratch arena freed after that step, so a cold build holds one batch's texts and outputs rather than the whole program's.
- [BUILD-POSITIONS-1] A lean manifest unit's stored metadata record (EMIT-META-1) carries its per-instruction source positions as a `paths` table, one space-separated `path:line:column` entry per instruction in `positions`, and its assertion messages as `[pc, message]` pairs in `assertions`, instead of `locations` and `messages` (a unit with more than 256 distinct paths keeps the spelled-out form); the native build reads either spelling into the same location, message and function tables.
- [QBE-BUILD-READ-1] The native build reads every input file (a checked manifest, a cached object it verifies, a generated source) into one buffer of the length the file states, and reads it to its end the ordinary way only when that length is unknown or the file changed while being read; a missing file or a directory fails exactly as before. The object fingerprint beside every cached object and runtime archive is `mylang-object-v2:<length>:<Wyhash>`, because the pinned Zig's XxHash3 hashes long inputs at about 20 MB/s; a `v1` fingerprint never matches, so such an entry is rebuilt once.
- [BUILD-KEYS-1] A native object key is the BLAKE3 digest of `mylang-object-key-v4` followed by the length-framed build environment, each field of the cache contract, the kind and the input (the key was SHA-256, which the pinned toolchain runs at about 14 MB/s on the build hosts and which a warm build ran twice per unit and batch), and the link identity a published executable's sidecar records is BLAKE3 too; the sidecar names it first, and a later build reads it without parsing the function records after it.
- [BUILD-VERIFY-1] Publishing a cached object records, after its `mylang-object-v2` fingerprint, the published file's inode, size and modification time; a later lookup accepts the object when the file still has exactly that identity, without reading it, and otherwise only when its bytes match the fingerprint, so a replaced, rewritten or truncated object is still verified by content and a mismatch is still a miss.
- [BUILD-LINK-1] The final link of a QBE or non-LTO build runs `zig ld.lld` directly with the command `zig cc` produces for it: the first link of a given shape runs `zig cc -###`, which links and prints that command, and records it beside the objects with the object run and the output replaced by markers, keyed by the build environment, the `PATH`, `ZIG_GLOBAL_CACHE_DIR`, `ZIG_LIBC`, `LIBRARY_PATH` and Nix linker variables and every other argument (the build's scratch directory, which only compile flags name, excepted); a later link of that shape fills the template in and skips `zig cc`'s host probe, unless a path the template names is gone or the direct link fails, and then goes through `zig cc` again. No template is recorded when an argument holds a space or the printed command does not name the objects once, in order, and the output once. `--report` names the route as `link_route` (`direct`, `recorded` or `driver`, null when the link was skipped), and a direct link of the same objects writes the same bytes.
- [BUILD-LINK-RSP-1] Every link command whose arguments grow with the program — the `zig cc` driver link, its `-###` recording run, the direct `zig ld.lld` link from a template, and the full-LTO frame-map link's two commands — passes everything after the tool and subcommand through a response file (`@path`, beside the link's output), which `zig cc` and `ld.lld` expand. A million-line program names 19,007 objects, 4.66 MB of arguments, past the kernel's `execve` limit; its link now runs with a command line of a few hundred bytes. `zig cc` still starts the `ld.lld` it prints with every object named, which the kernel refuses past about 6 MB, so when that recording run fails having printed its linker command, the build links directly from the printed command through a response file and keeps it as the template; a program's own link error fails that link too and is reported from `zig cc` as before. Arguments containing whitespace or a leading `#` are double-quoted; an argument with a quote or backslash, which Zig's and LLVM's response-file readers spell differently, keeps the command on the command line.
- [BUILD-METADATA-KEY-1] A batch's assembled metadata object is keyed by the BLAKE3 digest of everything its tables are rendered from — each member's symbol, name, positions in the spelling it carries, assertion messages and inline frames, in batch order — and its assembly is rendered only when that object is missing, so a warm build renders no unchanged batch's tables.
- [QBE-BATCH-4] The program-wide metadata C unit of a batched QBE build names no function, frame map or not (QBE-FRAMEMAP-ROOTS-1), so adding or removing a function leaves that unit, and its compile, unchanged.
- [QBE-LITERAL-ORDER-1] The shared metadata unit's frozen-literal pool is written in symbol order, each literal once, so the unit is a function of the set of literals and not of the order the manifest lists its units: a program linked from per-module manifests (MODULE-COMPILE-3) gets the whole-program build's unit byte for byte.
- [QBE-FRAMEMAP-ROOTS-1] The `used` array that roots every function's metadata for a frame-map program is emitted only in the full-table metadata unit of the LLVM arm, the one a full-LTO link optimizes; a batched QBE build is never LTO and its frame-map sections name each `_meta` their entries reach, so a default build keeps the shared unit's cache key when a function is added (`metadata_reused: true`) and a trap in it still names every frame.
- [QBE-BATCH-5] A batch boundary falls on one symbol in 8 on average, so a module of 1,000 functions forms about 125 batches and a body edit recompiles the handful of functions around the one it changed, not the 32 or more a boundary of one in 32 grouped.
- [QBE-BATCH-6] Function metadata is assembled per group rather than per code batch: groups are planned like batches (within a module, at most 128 functions) with a boundary on one symbol in 32, so a cold build starts about a quarter as many metadata assemblies as code batches, and an edit that moves one module's lines reassembles only that module's groups.
- [QBE-BATCH-7] A generic specialization (a unit whose name ends in `]`) is planned in a group of its own and compiled alone, for code and for metadata: every module that uses it emits it, so its object is keyed by its own text alone and is the same in each module's build and the whole program's, and it never sits between two units of its template's module, whose batches are therefore the same whichever specializations a program has.

- [EXPORT-PLACEMENT-1] A re-export `export module.Name;` naming an imported declaration may appear anywhere among top-level declarations after the module/import header. Discovery collects directives in source order with original locations; a duplicate or unknown re-export and private interface leaks still reject. The bare `export Name;` directive is removed and rejects with `E_EXPORT_REMOVED` (see `PUBLIC-DECL-3`). Directives inside function or type bodies reject, and formatting preserves directive order and comments.
- [PUBLIC-DECL-2] `list-declarations` reports a `public` declaration's `exported` flag as true while its `exports` array stays empty, and the declaration's byte range begins at the `public` modifier so a rename round-trips exactly.
- [PUBLIC-DECL-3] The bare `export NAME` directive is removed: it rejects with `E_EXPORT_REMOVED` at the directive, both in `list-declarations` and when a project is checked. Only the dotted `export module.Name` re-export, which publishes an imported declaration, remains an export directive.
- [REEXPORT-1] An export directive may name an imported declaration (`export shared.units.Distance`) as well as a local one. The facade module publishes that declaration under its own local name; a reference through the facade resolves to the defining module's identity with the target's own export privacy enforced, and interfaces, contracts and the project fingerprint report the facade name while the schema uses the defining declaration.
- [PUBLIC-DECL-1] A top-level declaration is published with a leading `public` modifier (`public def`, `public host def`, `public extern def`, `public record`, `public class`, `public enum`, `public union`, `public result`, `public domain`, `public resource`, `public type`, `public const`, `public constraint`). The modifier replaces the bare `export NAME` directive, so a module's published surface is written on the declarations themselves and `list-declarations` reports each declaration's export flag with an empty directive list.
- [DIRECTORY-CASES-1] An entry's cases file sits beside it as `<stem>.cases.json` (`driver_tests.my` owns `driver_tests.cases.json`). `mylang test ENTRY` without `--cases` applies that file when it exists, so a directory run applies each entry's own file to that entry and nothing else; an explicit `--cases FILE` still wins for a single entry and is still refused for a directory.
- [TEST-PRIVATE-ACCESS-1] A module's test file sees its private declarations: a module named `<name>_test` that no other module imports may reference the non-`public` functions, types and constants of module `<name>` (so `scanner_test.my` calls `scanner.helper()` without making it `public`). Every other importer, including a `<name>_test` that some module imports and a test file of a different module, still gets `E_PRIVATE_SYMBOL`, and domain-private members keep `E_PRIVATE_FUNCTION`.
- [REEXPORT-2] An export directive naming a qualified path whose alias is not imported, or whose target is not exported in its defining module, rejects with the existing located E_UNKNOWN_EXPORT/E_PRIVATE_SYMBOL family. list-declarations reports the facade directive with its alias and leaves local declaration flags unchanged.

- [PROJ-1] Loads shared module dependencies once in dependency order and releases state on allocation failure.
- [PROJ-2] Reports module cycles with both the importing location and the related source location.
- [PROJ-MODULE-NAME-FIX-1] `E_MODULE_NAME` states the rule and the fix: it names the declared header, the file, and the module name that file must declare (its path from the project root without `.my`), and ends with the exact header to write — for `sexpr/parser.my` declaring `module parser`, the message ends `write module sexpr.parser` with the header quoted.
- [PROJ-3] Rejects duplicate exports, missing exported symbols, private types exposed through public interfaces, and a re-export whose qualifier names no import or whose target is not exported in its defining module.
- [PROJ-4] Reports related declarations in their original source files after project source mapping.
- [PROJ-5] `mylang run FILE` loads the reachable project graph when the entry file opens with a `module` header or a module-only `import`/`export` directive, so a single file that imports a standard module needs no separate command; a file with no module header stays one standalone unit, and `run-project` remains the explicit project-entry alias.

- [PROJ-6] Project loading defaults to 16,384 reachable modules and 262,144 import edges (PROJ-SCALE-1); a `mylang.json` `budgets` object overrides `max_modules`, `max_imports`, `max_file_bytes` and `max_total_bytes`. Each present value must be a positive integer, unknown and duplicate budget keys are rejected like every other configuration field, and an absent field keeps its default.
- [PROJ-SCALE-1] The default project budgets admit a million-line project with no `mylang.json`: 64 MiB of total source (a generated million-line project is 35.1 MB), 4 MiB per file, 16,384 modules and 262,144 import edges. `mylang test` bounds its source snapshot by the same configured `max_total_bytes`, so one `budgets` entry governs both.
- [PROJ-BUDGET-MESSAGE-1] An `E_PROJECT_BYTES` refusal names the budget that ran out — the project's `max_total_bytes` or one file's `max_file_bytes` — with its size in bytes and the `budgets` field in `mylang.json` that raises it, instead of the reader's `TooLarge` error name.
- [PROJ-7] `mylang build FILE` detects a module-project entry the same way `run` does and links the reachable graph without requiring `--project`; a plain single-file program keeps the one-unit path, and `--project` remains an explicit override.
- [QBE-ARRAYWRITE-1] A `replace` on a fixed array writes one element of the local it read instead of copying the whole array twice: when the array operand is that local's own storage — a `load_local` or `move_local` of it that ran with an empty operand stack and is reached by falling through, so the borrow rule already named the slot — and the very next instruction stores the replaced array back into that same local, the element is written straight into the local's slot after the same unsigned bounds check against the same length, so the copy into the per-pc aggregate scratch, the copy back out of it and the now-dead scratch declaration all disappear and an element write costs one check and one store instead of two whole-array copies. The destination is claimed only when the local owns nothing, is this frame's own nonzero-sized value of the array's own type rather than a parameter slot holding a caller's address, and nothing between the read and the replace is entered by a branch or writes the local — so a copy taken before the write, an array inside a record, an array a callee was handed and a table read through another live operand all still see the value they held, and an out-of-range index still traps `R_LIST_BOUNDS` with the same index, length and source position.
- [QBE-ARRAYWRITE-2] An element write through a record field, `r.cells[i] = v` on a value record local and `c.cells[i] = v` on a `class` reference (and their augmented forms), writes the one element where the field already lives instead of copying the array and the record: when the statement is exactly the checker's `load r; load r; get_field k; <index>; <value>; replace_index; update_record {k}; store r` over one local, entered by no branch, with nothing between the field read and the `replace` but scalar pushes and computations that cannot call, allocate or write the local, the field is a fixed array of scalar elements, and a value record is this frame's own storage owning nothing, the field read names the field inside the record (the frame slot or the heap cell), the `replace` stores the element there after the same unsigned bounds check with the same trap and position, and the update and the store copy nothing. Any other shape — a table or an array of aggregates, a record reached through a parameter slot holding a caller's address, a write below a list slot, a value computed by a call — keeps the rebuilding lowering and its copies.
- [QBE-FIELDARRAY-READ-1] Reading one element of a `class` field that is a fixed array of scalars, `c.cells[i]`, loads the element from the heap cell instead of first snapshotting the whole array: the field read lends the field's address when its only consumer is the element read that follows it and every instruction between them is a scalar push or computation that cannot call, allocate or write memory, so the cell still holds what the snapshot would have copied. A field read with any other consumer, or across a call, keeps its snapshot.
- [QBE-ARRAYREF-1] A `mut` fixed-array argument crosses the call as the address of the caller's own storage: the front end emits `ref_local` for it instead of a load, which pushes the local's slot address (or, for a `mut` array parameter passed on, the caller's address that slot already holds). The callee's `mut` array parameter is an address-holding slot that is never copied at entry; its element writes go through that address, forwarded in place by `QBE-ARRAYWRITE-1` when the write is a statement, and any other store to it copies into the caller's storage. A read of it that the borrow rule cannot name — because the window before its consumer writes the parameter or hands it to a `ref_local` — is a snapshot in the per-pc scratch, and so is a read of any local whose `ref_local` is already on the operand stack, so `f(mut a, a)` hands the read-only parameter a copy. `ref_local` counts as a write of the local wherever the backend proves a local unwritten (read-only parameter addressing, payload projection, element and call-result borrows, constructor and replace forwarding), the bytecode validator accepts it only on a fixed-array local, and the inliner never splices a callee with a `mut` array parameter. Roots are unchanged: the caller's slot stays a traced root of its own frame while the callee writes managed values into it, and a callee's address-holding parameter is traced through the address.
- [QBE-AGGREF-9] A parameter that crosses by address (`QBE-AGGREF-1`) and a match payload binding that names its payload in place (`QBE-MATCHPROJ-1`) each occupy one word of the frame — the slot that holds the address — rather than the value's whole size, in the slot's declaration and in the frame estimate alike, and a caller charges one word of outgoing argument space for every argument that crosses as a scalar or as an address, the value's own size only for a move-only aggregate that still crosses by value. The slot holds the same address, is read through it exactly as before and is never zero-filled, copied into or released, so every value, trap, trace and exit status is unchanged.
- [QBE-CTORREAD-1] A record or variant constructor ends the life of each field operand it reads, so a local whose value one of those operands is — and whose type releases nothing — is named by its own slot instead of snapshotted into per-pc aggregate scratch, exactly as `QBE-AGGREF-1`'s borrow rule already did for a field, tag, element or argument read. The constructor writes each field once, into this pc's own scratch or into a destination no reachable instruction reads, so the region a named operand points at is never the region being written; a local the window writes, a branch entering the window, and an owning or zero-sized type all keep the copy, and every value, trap and trace is unchanged.
- [QBE-RETFWD-1] A list or byte-string element read whose only consumer is the `return` that hands it back is returned from the receiver's own backing storage: the result leaves through the one copy the caller owns — `%out` for a result over 4 KiB, the frame's return storage for a heap-backed frame, QBE's return pad otherwise — so the copy into per-pc aggregate scratch and the reload out of it disappear and the element crosses the boundary once. Only the epilogue runs in between, so the address is kept only where the epilogue can neither release nor free what it names: a frame that drops a local or closes an arena scope keeps the snapshot, as does an element the caller would have to release, one of zero size, a read a branch can enter directly, and a result type that is not the element's own. Bounds checking, negative-index normalization, the trap's code, message, position and structured detail, and the value the caller receives are unchanged.
- [QBE-CALLREF-1] A local whose only write is the store of a record-returning call's result — `x = f(...)`, and `x = f(...).field` through an uninterrupted chain of field reads — holds the address of that result instead of a copy of it, so the whole-record copy into the local and the per-pc scratch it came from both disappear and every read of `x` reaches the result where the callee left it. The storage is the frame's own: one result buffer per call site, reserved in the entry block, which lives as long as the frame and is rewritten only by that same call site — which re-runs the store first — so no live-range proof is needed and no instruction between the store and the last read is restricted. The local's slot becomes one word; a local that traces starts out naming an all-zero value of its own type, so a root push before the store still finds a traceable record. A local that is written anywhere else, is a parameter, owns something, is zero-sized, or whose type is not the callee's result type keeps its copy, and every value, trap, trace, arena check and exit status is unchanged.
- [QBE-ELEMREF-2] `x = xs[i]` names its element where it lives on the same terms `QBE-ELEMENT-ADDRESS-1` gave `for x in xs`, and the run that must be inert is now the borrow's real live range rather than the branch-free window after the store: every instruction reachable from the store without the store running again, from which a read of the local is reachable, together with the run after each read over which the operand that read pushed is still on the stack — which is where a `return x` and a call handed `x` sit. Inside that range only instructions that can neither call, allocate, release nor mutate are admitted; control flow, another element read of a list, byte string or fixed array, and a length read join the set, while any call, append, element write or unknown opcode ends the claim. A `return x` inside the range additionally needs an epilogue that releases nothing. An element type that holds managed references is borrowed too, and its slot is never read by the root protocol: no collection can run inside an inert range, and outside it the local is dead, so a root push can never follow an address into storage the receiver has since reallocated or the sweep has freed — while everything the element names stays reachable through the receiver itself. A frame that opens an arena scope keeps the copy, because closing the scope scans every local declared before it through its slot. A local written more than once is claimed only when every write stores an address of the same type and the same stability — a compiler temporary the front end reuses for two element reads of the same receiver is exactly that shape — and each write then carries its own live-range proof over a range the other writes end, so the slot holds an address for the whole function and never an address and then a value. Value-snapshot semantics are unchanged: a copy taken before a write to the same element still holds what it held, because the write is not inert and ends the claim.
- [QBE-LISTSET-1] Writing one element of a managed list or byte string — `xs[i] = v` and `replace(xs, i, v)` on a list receiver — is emitted as the same unsigned bounds test against the same receiver length followed by one store of the element's own size through `storage + i * stride`, in place of the `myl_native_list_set` call that performed exactly those two steps. An out-of-range or unrepresentable index still traps `R_LIST_BOUNDS` with the same index, length, message and source position, negative-index normalization is unchanged, the arena store barrier still runs before the bounds test so an escaping value still reports `E_ARENA_ESCAPE` first, and the list's length, capacity, generation and identity are untouched, so no iteration guard can observe the write. Because the write now names no allocating callee, it is no longer a safepoint: the root record this frame used to push for every live traced local and operand across the call, and its restore, are not emitted, and a value that names storage inside the same receiver copies one stride-aligned region onto an identical or disjoint one, exactly as the runtime helper's `memcpy` did.
- [QBE-FRAMEFIT-1] Whether a function's frame lives on the native stack or in a per-call heap block is decided from the per-pc aggregate scratch its lowered body actually declares, not from every scratch buffer the lowering might have declared: a function whose conservative estimate exceeds the 32 KiB native limit is lowered onto the native stack first, its frame is measured again from the `%agg`/`%aux` declarations that survived constructor forwarding, the borrow rule, the in-place array write and dead-slot removal, and only a frame that still exceeds the limit is lowered a second time, heap-backed, at the conservative size. A callee that only reads a large fixed-array parameter — `kernel(values: [1024]int)` returning `values[0]`, or the same over `[4096]int` — therefore calls neither `myl_qbe_frame_allocate` nor `myl_qbe_frame_release`, and neither does a function that only passes such a parameter on; a function whose surviving scratch or own locals exceed the limit still gets a heap block large enough for every slot its prologue derives from it, the stack-floor guard stays on every non-leaf and on every leaf whose frame could reach the headroom, a lowering that fails for any reason fails the same way once, and every value, trap, trace and exit status is unchanged.
- [QBE-NORETURN-1] In a build that dropped both step and frame accounting — the fast protocol of `QBE-UNWIND-1`, which a ReleaseFast executable and `mylang run` use when no ceiling was asked for — the cold path of every check whose last call is a `myl_qbe_fail_*` helper — or the `myl_qbe_int_neg`/`myl_qbe_int_binary` call in the cold block a checked negation or shift opens for the operands that always trap — ends in `hlt` (LLVM's `unreachable`) instead of `jmp @failure` into the shared exit that unlinks the frame and returns. The trampoline armed the non-local exit before any guest instruction ran, and every such helper records the trap, builds the trace and leaves through it, so the jump was dead; without it a check is no longer an edge into one common exit, which had kept checked functions from inlining and hidden their no-overflow facts. Every check is still performed, and a trap's code, message, `in f at file:line` trace, `MYLANG-TRAP` record and exit status are byte-identical. A build with step or frame accounting — a library, the guest-test runner, a Debug executable, a run with `--max-steps` or `--max-frames` — keeps the sticky return path unchanged. The LLVM translator accepts `hlt`, declares every `myl_qbe_fail_*` helper `cold`, and declares one `noreturn` only in a unit where every call to it is followed by `hlt`.
- [QBE-FRAMEMAP-1] In the fast protocol — a ReleaseFast executable or `mylang run` with no `--max-steps`/`--max-frames` ceiling — a guest function links no frame record: no `ctx->active_frame` load or store, no metadata store and no position store runs on any call path. Each unit opens with `dbgfile "SYMBOL"`, every call is preceded by `dbgloc PC+1` naming the bytecode instruction it belongs to (pc 0 for the prologue's own calls), and the prologue keeps only the execution-stack floor test, whose `myl_qbe_fail_frame` call carries column 1 to mark it as an entry guard. The build turns those positions into a table of return addresses, and when a trap fires the runtime walks the guest stack — by call-frame information in an LLVM program, whose guest functions carry unwind tables and no frame pointer and which links libunwind, and by the frame-pointer chain in a QBE program — and reports every return address found in the table — skipping an entry guard's frame, which was never entered — expanding bytecode-inliner splices exactly as before. A trap's code, message, `in f at file:line` trace, `MYLANG-TRAP` record, `assert` message and exit status are byte-identical to the linked-record protocol, including a callee LLVM inlined, a trap inside an inlined runtime helper, recursion and a stack-floor failure. A build with step or frame accounting, a library, the guest-test runner and a Debug executable are unchanged. For the QBE walk, the generated C and every runtime unit that can reach `fail` (the `qbe_*` units, text methods and value-copy unit) keep a frame pointer in every function that calls; the heap, collector and container units, which only return a status, keep every register.
- [LLVM-OVERFLOW-INTRINSIC-1] The LLVM translator lowers a checked 64-bit `+`, `-` or `*` to `llvm.{s,u}{add,sub,mul}.with.overflow.i64`, so it reaches the machine as the operation and one flag branch (`add; jo`, `sub; jb`, `imul; jo`) instead of the frontend's xor/and sign test or four-limb high product. It evaluates each block symbolically — a pure 64-bit operation is its opcode over its operands' values, a literal is its bit pattern, anything else is a value equal only to itself — and rewrites a flag only when its value is exactly one of the frontend's formulas over an `add`, `sub` or `mul` earlier in the same block: `((a ^ r) & (b ^ r)) < 0` for a signed add, `((a ^ b) & (a ^ r)) < 0` for a signed subtract, `r <u a` and `a <u b` for the unsigned ones, the four-limb high product `!= 0` for an unsigned multiply, and the signed high product against `r >> 63` for a signed one. Each formula is the intrinsic's overflow bit for every pair of 64-bit inputs, so the rewrite assumes nothing about the operands; the intrinsic's value replaces the wrapped result bit for bit. The branch, the cold block and its `myl_qbe_fail_overflow_op` call with the same operands, width and position are untouched, so a trap's code, message, trace, `MYLANG-TRAP` record and exit status are byte-identical. A narrower width's range test and literal bounds outside LLVM-CONSTANT-OVERFLOW-1 are left as they are, the QBE backend's IL and assembly do not change, and `wrapping_*`/`saturating_*` are unaffected.
- [QBE-LOOP-REMAINDER-BIAS-1] Under LLVM scalar policy, an already admitted loop copy may replace signed i64 flooring remainder by a positive literal with unsigned remainder of its dividend plus a multiple-of-divisor bias. The old final magnitude bound, old bounded private scalar leaves and checked u128 arithmetic must prove the whole signed interval maps into u64. No new guard, narrowed leaf bound or extra loop copy is added; the original checked fallback and other actions remain unchanged. Unsupported, escaping, exhausted or infeasible optional proofs keep the previous signed specialization. QBE policy refuses before allocating any new temporary and retains its previous signed recipe, as does truncating remainder.
- [QBE-LOOP-FACTS-1] With no step accounting, the native emitter plans each innermost loop of the shape `for i in range(...)`, `for x in xs`, `enumerate(...)` and `while i < n` / `while i <= n` with one `i = i + step` as the loop's last statement (a positive literal step, or a step held in a local the loop never writes), whose every instruction is a local load or store, a literal, a comparison, a jump, integer or float arithmetic, a cast, a field or fixed-array operation, `float`/`int`/`sqrt`-style float conversions, or a list or `bytes` length, element read, element write or iteration check — no call, no allocation, no pop, insert, slice or other resizing operation, no `str` or dictionary access, and at most one append — to a list of integers, `bool` or floats held in a local, in a straight-line body with a literal step, which is then the loop's only access to lists of that type — and whose header is its only entry. Such a loop cannot change the length of any collection it reads or move any element storage. The plan names the value of every operand and local symbolically (literals, locals the loop never writes, the counter between its entry value and its bound, an accumulator whose one write adds a bounded amount, sums, differences and products of those, lengths, a division of a bounded value by a nonzero literal, and elements read at an invariant position from a list whose element slots the loop never writes) and proves: an index `alpha * i + beta` with `alpha` in -1, 0, 1 and an invariant `beta` is in bounds on every iteration when its smallest value is at least zero and its largest is below the length, which also makes the frontend's `< 0` normalization branch for that index dead; a checked 64-bit `+`, `-` or `*` whose operands are bounded cannot overflow once every leaf it is computed from lies in `[-2^b, 2^b]`, where `b` (at most 60, at least 20) is the widest bound at which every such expression, counted as coefficient times leaf-bound to its degree, fits 64 bits — an operation that fits only below 2^20 keeps its check; an accumulator is bounded by its entry value plus three times the leaf bound times its increment's bound; a `for x in xs` length check holds on every iteration when it holds on entry; an invariant row `c[i]` and a stable list's element storage address are the same on every iteration; and an append runs once per counter value, so the list's spare capacity holds every iteration's element when it is at least the number of counter values. A loop the plan proves nothing about, and every loop of a step-accounted build, is lowered once, exactly as before.
- [QBE-LOOP-VERSION-1] A planned loop (`QBE-LOOP-FACTS-1`) is lowered twice behind a guard that every entry to the loop reaches first: it checks that the counter has at least 16 values to run through before its bound (a shorter loop saves fewer checks than the guard costs, and runs the original), each leaf's magnitude bound, a non-negative (or, for an accumulator, positive) step held in a local, each proven index's extremes against its length, each iteration check's length against its expected value, and reads each hoisted row and element storage address once, in an order in which no element is read before its position was checked. When every condition holds, control runs a check-free copy of the loop: the proven index, overflow and iteration checks and the dead normalization branches are omitted, an append whose room the guard checked (`capacity - length` at least the number of counter values) is an element store with the length and generation written back exactly as the inline append's fast path writes them, without its capacity test or growth call,, a hoisted row is the guard's value, and an element address is computed from the guard's storage address; every check the plan did not prove is still emitted, at the same instruction, with the same trap, operands and position. When any condition fails, control runs the original loop, lowered exactly as an unversioned build lowers it, so a trap the guard could not rule out is raised by the original at the same iteration with the same code, message, trace and `MYLANG-TRAP` record. Except for the separately certified ordinary growing append path of `RC-APPEND-RANGE-1`, the copy is discarded if its lowering would name an allocating callee. Results, traps and trace text are byte-identical to the unversioned lowering; only the instructions executed differ.
- [LLVM-ELEMENT-CLASS-1] Emitted QBE IL marks the storage word of a managed container — the start of its element storage block — with `# myl-elem %temp KIND` instead of `# myl-obj %temp`, where KIND is the element's byte size followed by `r` when elements are GC references and `v` otherwise: the storage word an element read or write loads, the backing pointer of the inline list append, and the destination and source storage of the inline byte-buffer appends. It is a comment line, so QBE's assembly is byte-identical. The LLVM translator reads it as everything `# myl-obj` says and more: an access derived by `copy` and offsetting `add` from an element storage word is placed in an element scope of its kind, one derived from any other marked object (a header, an allocation's payload) in an object scope, and the scoped alias metadata says objects and element storage never alias, and neither do element blocks of different kinds — each storage block is an allocation of its own and only ever holds elements of its one kind. A plain foreign access (a parameter, a global, the context) still aliases all of them, an access that combines two refined classes drops the refinement for the whole unit, and a unit with more than 29 element kinds leaves the rest plainly foreign. It is what lets LLVM keep a list's length, storage pointer and row pointers in registers across a loop that stores into element storage.
- [LLVM-ELEMENT-GEP-1] The LLVM translator gives a load or store whose address is an element address — a singly-defined `add` computed earlier in the same block from a marked storage word or object plus at least one non-constant term, every temporary on the way singly defined and none redefined in between — the address as a plain (not `inbounds`) `getelementptr i8` from the marked base instead of an `inttoptr` of the integer sum. The bits are the same; LLVM's scalar evolution can now see the variable part, so a loop over `data[i]` loads whole vectors instead of gathering one element at a time, and dependence distances can be computed. The integer `add` is still emitted for every other reader, a constant-offset header field and every `blit` keep the integer form, and `MYLANG_QBE_LLVM_ELEMENT_GEP=0` turns it off.
- [LLVM-RC-PREFIX-1] RC lowering marks the address of every word of the counted prefix below a managed payload that its inline fast paths read or write — the owner and hold counts and the common-header mark, `sub PAYLOAD, 32|24|16` of a payload it has already tested non-null — with `# myl-rc %temp`, a comment line, so QBE's assembly is byte-identical. The LLVM translator places such an access in an RC prefix scope that the scoped alias metadata keeps apart from the frame, from every object and from every element storage block: in an RC build no object or element access reaches below a payload, the frame never holds a managed object, and the runtime's own (inlined) count updates carry no metadata and so still alias everything. When the marked address is a singly-defined constant offset from a singly-defined payload, the access addresses it as a plain `getelementptr i8` from that payload instead of an `inttoptr` of the integer difference, so LLVM tells the owner, hold and mark words of one payload apart, forwards a count it just stored, and folds a loan and unloan of one payload into nothing. An unmarked header access keeps the integer form and no metadata; `MYLANG_QBE_LLVM_ALIAS=0` drops the scope and `MYLANG_QBE_LLVM_ELEMENT_GEP=0` the `getelementptr`.
- [LLVM-STICKY-REDUCTION-1] Before translation, the LLVM translator versions an innermost loop whose overflow checks do not decide control or an address: the loop's entry edges go to a fast copy in which the check of a reduction — a 64-bit signed frame cell the loop changes only by storing `v + x`, `x + v` or `v - x` where `v` is a value the cell held — is dropped while the copy sums the magnitudes of the added values, and the check of any other value is sticky (its flag is OR-ed into one word and the copy continues with the wrapped result). Every other check stays a branch, to a fallback. At each normal exit the copy verifies that the sticky word is zero and that `|entry value| + sum |x| <= 2^63 - 1` for every reduction (with the per-site iteration limits that keep the sums exact); then its results are the checked loop's. Otherwise, and on every would-be trap, the fallback restores every frame cell the loop writes to its entry value and runs the original loop, whose checks trap exactly as before: same code, message, operands, trace, record and exit status. The rewrite applies only to loops with no call, `blit` or phi, whose stores all write this activation's own `alloc` cells, whose temporaries are not live across the loop boundary, and in which no value that may have wrapped reaches a non-check branch, a load or store address, a shift amount, a divisor or a float-to-integer conversion; a check whose wrapped value would is kept as a branch. The QBE backend is unaffected, and `MYLANG_QBE_LLVM_STICKY=0` turns it off.
- [LLVM-SELF-ADDRESS-1] An LLVM unit may take its own function address and pass it as a callback without emitting a second declaration for the function defined by that unit. Other addressed functions retain their ABI-correct declarations.
- [LLVM-ZIG-1] The LLVM backend translates each emitted QBE unit into a self-contained textual LLVM module inside the compiler, with no Python and no per-unit translator process: loop versioning (LLVM-STICKY-REDUCTION-1), memory classes (LLVM-ELEMENT-CLASS-1), element GEPs (LLVM-ELEMENT-GEP-1), overflow intrinsics (LLVM-OVERFLOW-INTRINSIC-1), frame-map positions (QBE-FRAMEMAP-1) and the AMD64 SysV aggregate ABI produce the same bytes the retired Python adapter produced for the same unit, closure signatures and target policy. A call site declares a callee of the build's closure from the callee's own header, and a runtime helper from the curated table, the generated renderer and host-boundary shapes, or the declaration read from the bundled runtime headers and Zig `@export`s; a `myl_native_*`/`myl_qbe_*` helper found in none of them refuses translation with a diagnostic naming the unit. The build translates only the units that missed the object cache, on worker threads as many as the cores and free memory allow, and compiles each module with the bundled clang's `-cc1` command, taken once per build from `zig cc -###` for the same flags, so a unit costs one clang start. Its object is keyed by the unit text, the driver identity (which covers the translator's source) and the translator identity `mylang-llvm-translator-v1` naming every switch that is off (`MYLANG_QBE_LLVM_ALIAS`, `MYLANG_QBE_LLVM_ELEMENT_GEP`, `MYLANG_QBE_LLVM_STICKY` set to `0`), so a warm build translates nothing. `MYLANG_LLVM_DUMP=DIR` writes each translated unit's IL, IR and the closure signatures it read, for an equivalence check against the retired adapter.
- [QBE-FRAMEMAP-ASM-1] A frame-map unit's assembly is rewritten before it is assembled, and a full-LTO frame-map executable is optimized by LLD to one assembly file that is rewritten, assembled and linked natively: every positioned call that may enter the managed runtime is followed by a local label; direct libc `memcpy` and `memmove` calls are excluded because they cannot collect or trap in the managed runtime, and a retained `mylang_frame_map` section records, per call site, one entry per logical frame innermost first — a probe's inline stack adds each guest caller the call was inlined through at its own call-site position — each naming the unit's metadata and the position, with bits marking an entry guard, an inlined caller, and an entry from assembly that carries call-frame information (LLVM's), whose program the runtime unwinds by that information. The `.file`, `.loc` and `.pseudoprobe` directives and the probe descriptors are removed, so no DWARF reaches the object. Global symbol linkage directives, including weak declarations placed after the final probe descriptor, survive this rewrite so absent optional runtime symbols retain their null/fallback behavior at the native link. Assembly with no positions is left byte-identical, a position that names an unknown unit or a call positioned in another unit's function without an inlining record fails the build, and the compiler and the native build share the guard column.
- [LLVM-THINLTO-1] `--lto=thin` (with `--backend llvm`) builds every guest unit and the runtime as ThinLTO bitcode, keyed apart from full-LTO bitcode, and links a frame-map executable by letting LLD optimize and compile each module on its own thread (`--thinlto-jobs` as many as the cores and free memory allow) to one assembly file per module, written beside a private alias of each input in the link's own scratch directory and never beside a cached object. Each file is annotated for the frame map and assembled on worker threads with the bundled clang's `-cc1as` command, and the objects are linked natively. A callee inlined from another module is named through every guest unit's probe GUID (LLVM's `Function::getGUID`, the first eight bytes of the name's MD5), since only its own module holds its descriptor. The generated entry and metadata units stay machine code, so no module without probe descriptors imports a guest function and every unit's `_meta` record stays global for the other modules' tables, and the link turns off sinking common instructions and merging common block tails, which would fold copies of a callee inlined at different call sites into one whose inline site is lost. Traces, trap records and exit statuses are those of the full-LTO build. `--lto` and `--lto=full` keep the single merged module.
- [QBE-ROOTLIVE-1] A safepoint publishes a root record only for the traced locals that are live at it — read by the safepoint itself or by an instruction reachable from it before the local is written again — instead of for every traced local the frame declares. Liveness is a backward dataflow over the bytecode's own successor relation (branches, `?` error edges and match arms, where an arm's payload binding is written on the edge into it), so a hidden receiver temporary the front end made for one `xs[...]` expression stops being a root after its last read, a named local stops being one after its last read, and a value read at a loop's head stays one across the whole body. Taking a local address with `ref_local` is a use: a fixed array or record passed only to a later `mut` call stays rooted across earlier allocations, before its address reaches the operand stack. Operand-stack values are published exactly as before. The lowering's implicit reads count as reads: closing an arena scope scans every local declared before it, the fused `src[a:b].decode()` hands the slice's receiver to the decode two instructions later, and a local whose type owns something is released through its slot by the epilogue and by an overwriting store, so it stays published at every safepoint; a function with an arena scope and no root push keeps its locals' zero-fill for that scan. Collection only ever frees what no later instruction reads, so every value, trap, trace and exit status is unchanged, under `--gc-stress` too. The liveness solution is solved once per function and shared with the ownership analysis (`OWNERSHIP-ANALYSIS-1`), which reads the same rows, so the rows root planning publishes from are identical to the ones it computed alone; `QBE-BORROW-ROOT-1` then drops a live local whose value a published owner already reaches.
- [OWNERSHIP-ANALYSIS-1] One runtime-independent ownership analysis runs per function over the bytecode the native emitter lowers, reading the liveness solution root planning uses (`QBE-ROOTLIVE-1`) rather than solving a second one. For every transfer of a non-scalar value — an assignment, a loop or match binding, an argument to a parameter the callee only reads, a list element, set member or dictionary key or value, a field an update writes or a constructor fills, the record an update starts from, and a return — it answers `move` (the source local is an owned local read for the last time, or no local held the value), `borrow` (the source stays readable but neither it nor the destination is written while both are live, or the value is a string, byte string or scalar-only record no one can write in place) or `copy`; an operand it cannot trace to one local through a join is a `copy`. Every local gets a role: `owned`, `param`, `mut_param`, `borrowed` (filled once from another local or a part of one that stays live and unwritten while it is) or `path` (a compiler temporary filled that way and written through). Every in-place write of a managed payload gets a copy-on-write site — the write of an owned collection, the load handing an owned local to a `mut` parameter or beginning a write-through path, and the element read beginning a write through a temporary (`elements`) — with `needed` false under a `mut` parameter, and a site a loop cannot invalidate (the loop never rebinds the root, never stores, returns or crosses a branch with a value sharing its payload, never lends it to a call, and for element sites never restructures it or copies element references out) is hoisted to the widest such loop's header. The classification and hoisting are ported from the E2b prototype (`exp/e2b-rc-runtime`) without its refusals: `class`, arenas, callbacks and resources are answered, conservatively where unproved, and never rejected. Nothing the analysis answers changes a program's meaning.
- [RETURN-BORROW-1] Every checked function infers a physical `borrow_origin` separately from its logical `return_origin`: it includes managed backing such as immutable `bytes` inside a returned record even when value semantics make the result logically fresh, and transits direct calls, specializations and returned aggregates to a fixed point. Unknown or indirect-call provenance remains unknown. The fact survives typed-to-bytecode lowering, cached check summaries and native fragment dependency keys. A direct call result may borrow a single source parameter's backing only when the caller's ownership/liveness proof keeps that source live and unwritten for the borrow's whole use; a mutating source, escaping result, ambiguous source set or unknown fact requires an owned transfer. The explain surface reports both facts without changing the checker's mutation-overlap verdict.
- [QBE-MOVE-ELIDE-1] Two aggregate copies the ownership analysis proves unobserved are not emitted, in a frame that opens no arena scope. An *alias*: a local of a copied aggregate type that owns nothing, every write of which is `store_local` fed by `load_local` of one other local holding its own value in its own slot, and whose target no instruction writes while the alias is live, names that slot — its stores copy nothing, its reads read the target, a root published for it traces the target, and a constructor forwarded into the target (`QBE-CTORFWD-1`) counts a read of the alias as a read of the target. An *in-place update*: an `update_record` of a plain record whose written fields are all one word and whose base is the last read (a move) of a local holding its own value — no alias of it live afterwards; outside value-semantics builds no `ref_local` of it anywhere, while a value-semantics build permits prior completed `mut` calls but no pending reference at the read or write within the read-to-update window; its slot named by the read (`QBE-AGGREF-2`) and not written before the result is consumed, and no operand still on the stack under the base naming it or an alias of it or lost to a join (`f(p, replace(p, x=2))` keeps its copy) — writes the fields into that slot, and a store of the result back into the same local copies nothing. Under value semantics, a compiler temporary filled only from a `mut` record parameter may name the caller's storage when MUT-EXCLUSIVE-1 guarantees disjoint mutable arguments and no COW write disturbs the storage while the temporary is live; an immediate scalar-field update returned to the same parameter then writes directly into caller storage. Under COW, the unused physical slot of a proven move alias is not zero-initialized: every read and GC root uses the target slot, and alias stores emit nothing. An update whose base is a borrowed list element written straight back (`xs[i].f = v`) keeps its copies. Every value, trap, trace and exit status is the one the copies produced, under `--gc-stress` too.
- [RC-PLAIN-PROJECTION-1] RC projection preserves every physical byte of a non-owning inline result before consuming its owning parent. Plain record fields and enum tags remain valid even when parent cleanup clears the old operand storage.
- [RC-CONTRACT-RESULT-1] Contract-generated variant inspection borrows its hidden owner and payload until the clause finishes. Inspecting a Result for postconditions never consumes or clears the value subsequently returned, including managed and unique payloads; ordinary source match bindings retain their existing transfer rules.
- [RC-PLAIN-OPERAND-1] RC operand cleanup emits no runtime drop for an input whose physical type contains neither counted edges nor unique resources. Every instruction still retires consumed owning inputs before publishing a plain result, so reuse of an operand position cannot suppress an earlier value's release.
- [RC-MATCH-SCALAR-1] An RC match binding of a scalar payload publishes one canonical machine word into its local slot: the payload is loaded with its own zero or sign extension and stored whole, as the GC lowering does. A packed payload narrower than a word (`bool`, `u8`, `i16`, a `u32`-backed domain, …) never leaves stale upper slot bytes that a later whole-word read, branch or comparison could observe; aggregate payloads keep their byte-exact copy.
- [RC-AGGREGATE-1] RC aggregate construction journals zero-initialized inline values and fresh managed bodies before any fallible child transfer. Consumed ordinary owning operands transfer their tokens after a successful pin-aware take; borrowed or held inputs acquire independent values, and unique children transfer their tokens. A completed copyable constructor may publish directly into its vacated stable operand slot before disarming its partial guard; this handoff cannot call out or fail. Empty variants still publish an initialized typed operand. Updates prepare replacement ownership before releasing old fields. Variant payload bindings own their active values before the consumed scrutinee is released. Managed bodies with unique resources install their finalizer, and all partial ownership is cleaned on a trap.
- [RC-FRAME-1] RC frames keep no runtime journal and link nothing into the execution context. Each physical slot that can hold an obligation has a frame-private guard word and role word; guards start clear, and a local's role starts as its type's ordinary owner. Normal exits restore the frame's open receiver scopes before guarded releases, and every guard clears before a drop can reenter host code. The C entry's argv owner is one guarded C-side slot with a distinct construction role that immediately drops arena child edges. Borrowed local uniqueness adopts only a fresh clone, preserving the caller's owner; an owned field replacement releases its former edge after cloning. Box write-back copies its prepared inline body atomically, publishes the replacement owner before releasing the former owned edge, and preserves borrowed owners and all input guards on refusal. A noncopyable managed body's unique adapter closes raw resource fields before counted child release. Traps are handled by heap teardown (RC-TRAP-TEARDOWN-1).
- [RC-STARTUP-1] An RC native context initializes its counted heap and registers the generated ownership module before host initialization or guest entry. Missing RC state or missing generated metadata cannot continue as GC. Registration is idempotent, and status adapters preserve count-limit and region errors while distinguishing managed-budget refusal from host allocation failure through the ordinary located trap entry. GC startup is unchanged.
- [RC-STATIC-DROPS-1] Every RC drop — a local's release, an operand's consumption, an arena end and a return's exit drops — names the slot's static type (the local's declared type or the operand stack's type at that instruction) and its fixed storage, and tests only its frame-private guard word. A copyable counted value also reads its role word to route a loan or held pointer to its release of holds; any other value is released as an ordinary owner directly. A type with generated glue (RC-GLUE-1) releases either way through that glue, inline for a one-word value, so a static drop calls no runtime lookup. A local initialized on only some paths is dropped exactly once on every exit.
- [RC-EXIT-SCOPES-1] A return from a frame with receiver scope records tests each record's active word; only when one is open does the frame pass its records to the runtime, which closes exactly those open scopes at the head of the chain, restoring all their pins before any hold is released. `?` inside a receiver statement therefore returns with no scope left linked.
- [RC-EXIT-SKIP-1] The one function that runs last — the program's entry, or the function the synthesized `<entry>` wrapper calls, when no other instruction calls it or takes it as a value — asks the runtime at its return, after closing its receiver scopes, whether the process is about to exit, and releases none of its operands or locals when the answer is yes. The runtime answers yes only for a production RC executable that is the whole program (not `--rc-runtime=stats` or `sites`, which balance every allocation, and not the checked runtime or a `--gc-stress` run), and only while no registered file, socket or host handle is open: releasing an owner is where such a handle is flushed and closed, so a live handle keeps every drop, in today's order, and the registry closes anything left. After the registry and stand-ins have run, a clean exit of such a process also skips the arena and cache release. Output, stream flush order, written files and the exit status are unchanged; every other build and every other function keeps all of its drops.
- [RC-FLAG-PROMOTE-1] Every RC guard and role word lives in its own frame slot and is loaded and stored through that slot directly, so the backend promotes each word no call can observe to a register and folds the drops a path has already decided.
- [RC-DEAD-DROPS-1] A guard word that nothing ever sets — no nonzero store and no address handed to a call — reads zero everywhere: its drop sequences are removed, its other loads become the constant zero, its stores and slot disappear, and role words that nothing reads lose their stores.
- [RC-UNUSED-ENTRY-READS-1] After dead RC drops are removed, an RC function whose prologue reads the heap pointer or the entry arena depth and never uses that value no longer makes the call; both calls only read the execution context, so values, traps and exit status are unchanged.
- [RC-LAYOUT-1] ABI5 RC heap allocations carry a 32-byte prefix with unsigned 64-bit ordinary owners at payload minus 32, nonsharing holds at minus 24, common mark/count/flags at minus 16 and native descriptor at minus 8. Payloads remain 16-byte aligned: slab metadata and slots retain 16-byte alignment and strides, and dedicated extents preserve their aligned allocator metadata. A full-width retired/death/free link reuses the active count pair only after the object becomes DYING or RETIRED. GC, arena and immortal allocations retain their 16-byte prefix, and generated GC/arena heap offsets remain unchanged.
- [RC-STORAGE-1] RC heap storage reuses retired slots immediately, initializes owner and hold words on reuse, charges allocator reservations and metadata to the heap budget, and permits relocating large backing only with one owner and no holds or pins. Checked extent overflow and allocation refusal preserve existing storage. A production small-object cache is per heap and admits a type only after successful metadata registration; it stores a size class rather than a reclaimable slab address. Generic counts, arenas and custom allocator hooks retain their allocation contracts. Retire advances a saturating reuse epoch; dictionary weak absence hints are disabled at saturation.
- [RC-PLAIN-REPLACE-1] Physical RC replacement of registered native layouts with no ownership leaves preserves overlap and region errors without staging allocation; pending finalizers retain the staged-source publication order, and raw unique resources or held tokens retain their full ownership protocol.

- [RC-DICTIONARY-WORD-1] An existing RC dictionary entry with an eight-byte native plain value or a zero-width unit may publish captured bytes directly only with a registered hook-free zero-edge binding, an empty death queue and a valid actual dictionary region. Same-address replacement remains an early no-op; all refused admissions preserve the original replacement, registration, callback, overlap, failure and ownership behavior.
- [RC-DICT-SCALAR-INSERT-1] An RC dictionary insertion with registered hook-free zero-edge key and value bindings, no queued deaths and a valid destination region copies its staged entry bytes without a generic ownership transaction, including the zero-width payload of a set. Inputs are staged before backing growth or compaction. A recorded absent scalar probe may supply its hash and empty slot only after reservation and callbacks leave it valid for the staged key; successful insertion records its retained key as found. Table growth, compaction, deletion, reindexing and failed index allocation retain their existing invalidation. Publication failure, pending callback order, region refusal, owned keys and values, insertion order and allocation accounting are unchanged.
- [RC-FLAT-LOAN-1] Physical loans of admitted small flat ordinary layouts preserve byte padding and acquire one H token per leaf, including duplicate aliases. Native unique obligations and finalized terminal payloads remain excluded; refusal rolls back acquired holds and empties output. Unloan clears all siblings and queues their deaths before callbacks, preserving finalizer order and reentrant cleanup.
- [RC-PLAIN-RETAIN-1] Physical RC retain of a registered native layout with no ownership leaves may copy disjoint nonzero bytes directly. Invalid destination regions still clear output and report the same failure; cold metadata, aliasing, zero-size layouts, queued finalizers and every counted, held or unique obligation retain their existing path.
- [RC-COLLECTION-1] RC list headers own their live logical elements and raw backing exactly once. Copy, replace, insertion and removal use pin-aware publication; backing growth and in-place ordering relocate existing edges. Constructors and clones undo initialized ownership on failure, and held retired backing has no duplicate element ownership.
- [RC-DICTIONARY-1] RC dictionaries own live keys and values, while private entry bookkeeping owns only stable raw backing. Removal immediately releases deleted logical edges; compaction uses stored non-owning hashes instead of released keys. Destruction enumerates live index slots without hashing, and held bookkeeping can outlive dictionary-embedded descriptors safely.
- [RC-VIEWS-1] RC byte views own their canonical backing allocation rather than interior addresses; text views own their canonical text. Published byte-buffer snapshots acquire one backing owner, while the buffer's duplicate snapshot marker owns none. Runtime text reuse returns one owned success result, and failed construction releases initialized view and split-list ownership.
- [RC-CODEC-OWNERSHIP-1] Copied codecs own decoded arguments, callback results and initialized temporary values until transfer or cleanup. List and dictionary scratch values are released after stores; partial values and rejected conversions are rolled back. Guest traps retire tracked host buffers and managed owners before the execution context is destroyed; GC callback results remain rooted across further conversions. Decoded live dictionary views convert their freshly decoded mapping owner into a held edge before publishing the view, including nested and failing decodes.
- [RC-HOST-OWNERSHIP-1] Copied host bytes publish one owned independent guest payload only after callback and output validation succeed. Host node scratch is execution-tracked so a guest trap cannot bypass its release; scalar and inline wire values introduce no managed ownership.
- [RC-STATISTICS-1] Counted RC artifacts report physical successful owner acquisitions and releases separately from lifetime holds, allocations, retirement, cloned shells and uniqueness checks. Initial ownership is an allocation; null, immortal and arena owner no-ops do not add traffic, and refused increments have distinct counters. Counters are per heap and saturate diagnostically without changing ownership behavior. Ordinary GC and RC artifacts contain no counter storage or event calls. Execution reports one versioned JSON record after cleanup and before heap destruction, including the standalone fast-exit path.
- [RC-COUNTS-1] RC-v1 owners represent physical value edges and holds preserve lifetime without increasing snapshot sharing. Destruction starts exactly once at zero owners and zero holds; nested pins restore their prior state before their holds are released. Common immortal and arena flags are checked before accessing any RC count prefix. Both counts stop at UINT64_MAX. Runtime and generated IL/C glue preflight acquisitions, including aliased leaves of an aggregate; count overflow changes no count or output and reports the located `R_REFERENCE_COUNT_LIMIT`; underflow and resurrection of a dying object are internal invariant failures.
- [RC-REGISTER-1] RC-v1 ownership shapes are independent of GC reachability metadata. Static module registration and dynamic metadata import validate checked native sizes, copy durable sidecar metadata, compute inline depth iteratively while stopping at managed pointers, and reserve budget-charged teardown frames before any owning value exists. Reservation includes unique-resource nesting even when count-free shapes collapse internally, and preserves that depth when imported shapes become children of new shapes. Registration and reservation publish atomically; refusal preserves existing metadata and release capacity. Imported shape source addresses have no retained identity or lifetime requirement. There is no fixed descriptor-depth limit. A budget-charged heap index resolves native types, registered shapes and repeated static modules without scanning prior registrations; index growth commits with the metadata and reserved frames.
- [RC-SCOPES-1] An RC receiver scope links into dynamic cleanup before fallible preparation, owns nonsharing holds and prior pin states in a charged journal, and closes when empty or partially prepared. Native typed scope bridges hold captured list backing and dictionary entry/index backing independently of their headers, preserving an addressed block after growth retires it. Closing first unlinks the scope, restores all recorded pin bits in reverse acquisition order, then releases any hold or journal storage. Nested cleanup preserves outer pins and holds; a last hold may finalize after every pin has been restored. Failed path or backing preparation unwinds only newly acquired journal entries, preserving the source owners and older scope state.
- [RC-ARENA-1] Closing an RC arena detaches its chunks, restores its receiver scopes, runs all finalizers before releasing any logical edge, and only then releases the chunks in bulk. Closing backing storage and descriptors remain valid through both passes, including an earlier-allocated arena child inspected by a parent finalizer. Raw backing owns no logical elements. Finalizers run once and may allocate in a reentrant arena. Aborted construction is marked dead so later close cannot finalize it twice. Retained chunks and the per-depth table remain charged; retained chunks can be reused without another reservation at the exact budget ceiling.
- [RC-CAPABILITY-1] Native environment-name, directory-listing and file-readlines producers release fresh element tokens after the list acquires its logical edges. Failed list construction releases initialized edges and restores temporary roots before raising a trap. File decoding frees its raw read buffer before reporting invalid text or allocation refusal. Successful capability results carry exactly one caller-owned token.
- [RC-HOLD-VIEW-1] A live dictionary view carries a nonsharing lifetime hold H, independent of ordinary owners O. Copies and hidden receiver projections preserve dictionary identity without increasing O or snapshotting pinned referents; take transfers H and drop releases it. O=0 with H>0 keeps the dictionary and its current logical edges alive. Mutating an exposed held mapping preserves the H token: uniqueness reuses an unshared or ownerless referent and converts a detached snapshot from fresh O to H before replacing the old hold. Refusal preserves the original slot. Escape of a younger arena view memoizes a dictionary copy and converts its fresh owner to H; same/older referents retain identity. Hold-count refusal and later aggregate or escaped-child failure roll back all acquired tokens. Ownership metadata ABI 2 distinguishes held edges from owned edges; stale modules are rejected before registration.
- [RC-ESCAPE-1] RC escape returns an owned source-preserving copy in the enclosing region, cloning younger arena objects and retaining eligible older objects.
