Its iterative operation stack and source-plus-destination-region map preserve duplicate DAG edges without repeated subtree cloning or native recursion. The same map bounds pinned diamond copies. Failed escape leaves output empty and source usable, immediately releases initialized heap edges, and aborts fresh ancestor-arena clones before either arena closes. Sparse dictionary copies preserve live logical values and sharing without tracing raw entry bytes as extra owners.
- [RC-TRANSFER-1] RC semantic copy establishes independent owned edges, while semantic take transfers existing tokens only where pins permit it and copies pinned paths otherwise. Failed aggregate transfers undo initialized temporary tokens, preserve source ownership, and leave the destination empty. Replace publishes prepared ownership before releasing the previous value, including a child borrowed from that previous value. Owned uniqueness replaces one owned payload token; borrowed uniqueness returns either unchanged borrowed reuse or a fresh conditional owner, with abort and commit balancing that token exactly once. Pinned heap paths are cloned with an explicit operation stack and staged collection adapters rather than native recursion; failed unpublished arena clones release their acquired heap edges immediately, before region close. Ordinary unpinned pointer transfers may bypass the aggregate walker; small inline traversals use bounded automatic frames with charged, unbounded-depth heap fallback. Both paths preserve the same count-limit, region and publication rules. A private registered plan may flatten at most eight unconditional ordinary edges after matching the complete native field kinds to the RC shape; raw unique resources, holds and variants retain the generic path. Flat copy preflights repeated aliases before incrementing any count, and typed take retains transactional output staging.
- [RC-RELEASE-1] RC-v1 release walks direct ownership edges with reserved iterative frames, ignoring inactive variants and pointer-free subtrees, and enqueues newly dead heap objects through their dying count words. It never allocates or recursively descends through heap pointers, including at the memory ceiling. Unique raw-resource scheduling also traverses inline shapes iteratively, disarming initialized resource fields before their close callbacks; a failed partial constructor closes only resources already transferred into its body. Collection adapters enumerate only initialized logical edges while their header remains valid. Explicit unique finalizers run at idle walker depth before allocator retirement; reentrant release only enqueues, and workspace is reacquired after callbacks.
- [COW-SHARED-1] The default value model (also selected explicitly by `--value-semantics`) enables copy-on-write checks and routes each real `value_copy` through one rooted shallow-transfer hook: a list, dictionary, set or byte buffer payload is marked shared in the existing two-word object header, while a move or borrow emits no transfer work. The internal copy-on-write policy also supports tests without the language switch; its ownership-transfer stand-in is disabled when the real hook runs, so a transfer is never marked twice. A fixed record or array with at most eight directly held collection leaves may mark those leaves inline after copying into private scratch, with a rooted runtime transfer if any leaf is pinned or immutable; the transfer passes its private copy to the rooted fallback so the operand stack slot does not escape; boxes, variants, deeper shapes and child-copy operations use the rooted entry. The rooted entry handles the copy hook's output scratch and fresh-children contract; `extend`, dictionary `update` and keyed `sorted` share the source collection's elements that the destination will take, while an ordinary element store shares the element's outer payload. The bulk source keeps its outer payload if no child is pinned; if a pinned child needs replacement, it copies the outer payload first and patches only that private copy. A pinned payload is copied for the transfer instead. Unpinned immutable recursive box pointers transfer without walking their bodies; descriptor reachability and shallow sharing stop at boxes, whose mutable children are protected when their bodies are projected. A temporary receiver pin makes a snapshot clone only the pinned box path. Reusing a descriptor's reachability result across transfers must give the same share and depth behavior as walking it anew. An acyclic type descriptor walk accepts up to 1,024 descriptor edges. A runtime share walk accepts up to 1,024 inline record, array and variant descriptor edges from the transferred value; immutable box hops do not consume that budget. Exceeding either bound traps `R_COW_DEPTH` rather than leaving a mutable descendant unmarked. Under `--legacy-sharing`, ordinary builds emit no copy-on-write code.
- [COW-UNIQUE-1] A write tests the shared bit inline and copies only on the shared branch, replacing the owning slot with a fresh unshared shallow copy in the payload's region. The sticky bit makes either holder copy on its first write. The copy marks directly held collection payloads shared, and collection stress preserves both values and reclaims unrooted copies.
- [COW-HOIST-1] A uniqueness check proven stable by ownership analysis moves to loop entry edges; an element loop can make the list's held payloads unique once there. An in-loop check prevents loop versioning from using storage that the check might replace.
- [COW-NESTED-1] A nested write makes each collection along its path unique and writes a copied element, dictionary value or field back into its owning place, including a record body reached through fields. Repeated and alternating list, dictionary, fixed-array and field steps retain every intermediate owner; each receiver and key evaluates once in source order, including dynamic negative array indices. A receiver whose path overlaps an evaluated `mut` argument in the same statement is rejected by MUT-OVERLAP-RECEIVER-1; a call evaluated in its own preceding statement finishes before receiver preparation begins. A recursive box remains immutable: copying its pointer does not walk its descendants; projecting its body into an independent value shallow-shares directly held collections before exposing them and copies pinned children into that private value. A dictionary reached through record fields is made unique before a nested value is read from it.
- [COW-PIN-1] A `mut` call pins collection arguments until it returns; sharing one during the call makes an eager shallow copy so the callee's inout slot remains stable. A nested call restores the prior pin state. A pending fixed-array receiver pins the collection and box path it selected until its later index callbacks finish; snapshots copy that pinned path, and preparation never traverses unrelated recursive siblings. Completion, early propagation and failure restore pins and temporary roots in reverse nesting order.
- [COW-PIN-ELIDE-1] Under the RC runtime a direct call whose `mut` list argument is a whole local skips the `COW-PIN-1` scope begin, pin and end when the callee's parameter is in the pin summary; every other `mut` argument (a field or element place, an indirect or function-value call, a collection the summary does not cover) is pinned as before, and the argument is still made unique and written back to its local before the call. The summary is a whole-program greatest fixed point over the parameters that are `mut` lists of plain elements (element shape neither counted nor a unique-drop resource, not a reference `mut`, written by the body and never rebound, moved, dropped or referenced), computed once before the second emission pass; it is empty under the tracing runtime. A parameter stays in the set only while the body's uses of the parameter, and of every compiler temporary filled from it, are all of: a buffer `length`, `read` or `write` on it, or a straight-line pass of its value (no branch target between the load and the use) into a compiler temporary or as argument `q` of a direct call to a function whose parameter `q` is in the set. Anything else removes it: growth, shrink, iteration, slicing or any other buffer operation, a store into a named local (`copy = xs`), a field, list or dictionary store, a return, a read-only parameter, an indirect call, an `invoke` or a branch reached while the value is on the operand stack. The elision is sound because the pin is observable only through a semantic share of the payload during the call (which the pin turns into an eager copy), a growth of its storage (which the pin refuses) and the backing hold that keeps the block alive; the summary excludes each: no instruction reachable from the callee, directly or through a callee that receives the parameter, shares, grows, replaces or releases the payload, so nothing during the call is affected by pin state. Element writes never depend on the pin (a hold does not block a write and the caller has already made the payload unique), the callee's own code is unchanged, MUT-EXCLUSIVE-1 keeps two `mut` arguments of one call from overlapping and VALUE-SEMANTICS-5 gives a read-only argument that could name the same root a snapshot, so no other operand of the call can reach the payload. A recursive or mutually recursive callee is accepted only if the whole cycle is, since the fixed point starts from every candidate and removes one whose body breaks the rule until none changes: were a violation reachable by any call chain, its first step would be an instruction in a function of the set that the local test rejects. A trap ends the program, so a call that elided its scope has nothing to unwind. The final emission key of a caller includes the callee's summary flags.
- [COW-STATS-1] An internal counting build reports mark, uniqueness-check, hoisted-check, pin and copy counts plus copied bytes on one `cow-stats` JSON line at exit. `MYLANG_COW_COUNTING=1` selects this policy in the default value model or with explicit `--value-semantics`, but not under `--legacy-sharing`; build and test children inherit it, and compiler, fragment, manifest and test-result cache identities differ from ordinary value-semantics builds. The normal emission policy adds no counter calls. The native test runner treats a sole `cow-stats` line as a successful measurement rather than a trap.
- [QBE-BORROW-ROOT-1] A safepoint publishes no root record for a traced one-word local, other than a parameter, that a published owner already reaches: every write of the local is a store fed by a read of one owner — its whole value, a list element, a dictionary key or value, a `class` or value-record field, or a fixed-array element — and, on every path from such a store through the safepoint itself, the owner is not written (nor written in place the instruction before its store, nor updated in place) and, for an object read from the heap, no instruction overwrites or removes an element or field of an object of that object's type (an element write, `pop`, `remove`, `clear`, `take`, `truncate`, `resize`, a dictionary `update` or `pop`, `set.discard`, a keyed sort, a `replace` or a `class` field update), no callee or invoked function value is handed an argument whose type can reach that type, and no resource, file, stream or provider operation runs. The owner must itself be live and traced there, and published or covered the same way through a chain of at most eight owners. Frames that open an arena scope keep every root. The collector never moves an object and its generations rescan or remember every old object that references a young one, so the value is marked through its owner exactly as its own root would have marked it; values, traps, collection timing and results are unchanged, under `--gc-stress` too.
- [QBE-INVOKE-SAFEPOINT-1] Invoking a function value is a safepoint: the callee is whichever top-level function the value names, known only at run time, so the invoking instruction publishes its live roots around the indirect call and a function that invokes one counts as able to allocate, exactly like a direct call to a function that can. A list the caller still reads after invoking a callback that allocates therefore survives the collection that allocation starts.
- [QBE-ELEMREF-3] A cast to an integer type is inert inside a borrow's live range (`QBE-ELEMREF-2`): it only range-checks and traps, like the checked arithmetic already admitted, and it is how a runtime index's negative-index normalization converts the receiver length — so a second element read with a runtime index, such as `pins[row.index]` while `row = rows[i]` is live, no longer cancels the borrow. A cast to text builds a string and still ends the claim.
- [QBE-ELEMREF-4] A direct call inside a borrow's live range (`QBE-ELEMREF-2`) keeps the borrow when the callee provably cannot disturb the receiver's backing storage: the allocation analysis proved it cannot allocate — directly, transitively or through a function value — so it can neither collect nor grow a list; none of its parameter types traces a reference, so, with no mutable globals, no closures and no allocation, it can name no heap object at all; and it has no mutation footprint and writes no aggregate parameter, so no `mut` parameter reaches storage the caller names. `for k, s in enumerate(spheres): t = intersect(origin, direction, s)` with a plain-data `Sphere` therefore reads each sphere in place instead of copying it per iteration. A callee that can allocate, takes a list, class, string or other traced parameter, or declares or infers a `mut` parameter keeps the copy, and every value, trap and trace is unchanged.
- [QBE-NICHE-1] An `Option` whose `Some` payload is a managed reference — a class instance, list, dict, `str`, `bytes` or `Cell`, every type whose value is a non-null heap pointer — is stored as that reference word alone: 8 bytes, 8-aligned, null for `None`, and the case is whether the word is null. Every other optional keeps the `{tag, payload}` layout: a scalar, record, union or domain payload, and an optional optional, whose inner `None` already owns the null word, so `Some(None)` and `None` stay distinct. The rule reads the schema's `some`/`none` cases, so a user union of exactly that shape is laid out the same way. A `class` with two optional children is 16 bytes of payload rather than 32. The runtime needs no kind of its own for it: the optional's descriptor is its payload's pointer descriptor under the optional's name, because the collector, the arena region check and escape copier, and structural equality already treat a null pointer as no reference, so they trace nothing for `None`, copy it as null and compare it equal only to `None`. The native layout ABI version is `mylang-native-amd64-layout-v3`.
- [QBE-NICHE-2] Nothing a program can observe changes with `QBE-NICHE-1`: construction, `is None`/`is not None` and the narrowing they give, `match` with and without a binding, `?` on an optional, `is_some`/`is_none`/`unwrap_or`, `==`/`!=`, printing and f-string rendering (`Some(...)`/`None`), elements of lists, dict values, record fields, fixed-array elements and class fields, arguments and results of direct calls and callbacks, and `escape` out of an arena all answer exactly what the tagged layout answered, and the collector keeps every `Some` reference alive and follows no `None`, including under `--gc-stress`.
- [QBE-SIZE-LIMIT-1] Before QBE compiles a guest function, the build estimates QBE's memory for it as 1.6 bytes per block x temporary pair (its liveness and register allocation keep one temporary bit set per block) and, when the estimate exceeds three quarters of the address-space ceiling QBE inherits from the compiler, refuses the build with a message naming the function, its blocks and temporaries, the estimate, the ceiling and the two ways out (split the function or build with `--backend llvm`), exit status 2, instead of letting QBE die in `emalloc` after minutes. An unbounded ceiling refuses nothing, and a function that fits builds exactly as before.
- [QBE-NICHE-3] The copied-value boundary keeps its external form for a niche optional: the codec manifest describes it with its own kind, `niche`, and the adapter reads its case from whether the reference word is null and writes `None` as the null word, so the wire node still carries the case index in `bits` and the payload as its one child, exactly as for a tagged optional. The host bridge never carries a traced value, so its schema is unchanged.

## Discovery and formatting

- [TOOL-1] Groups exact duplicate bodies after binding renaming while preserving resolved binding identity.
- [TOOL-2] Reports literal differences once per exact-group representative when discovering near duplicates.
- [TOOL-3] Reports incomplete near-duplicate discovery when work budgets are exhausted and rejects invalid budgets.
- [TOOL-5] `fmt` prints the one canonical layout to stdout, permits semantically incomplete drafts but rejects syntactically invalid input, and `--check` is nonzero when the input bytes differ from canonical output. The fixed layout uses four-space suites; one space around binary/assignment operators and after commas and annotation colons; no padding inside ordinary delimiters; no trailing whitespace; one final newline for nonempty source; two blank lines between top-level declarations and one after the header directive group. Comment attachment, literal values, directive order and expression evaluation order are preserved, and there is no configurable wrapping.
- [FMT-MODULE-DETECT-1] `fmt` formats a module file instead of rejecting it: a source whose first line that is neither blank nor a comment opens with a `module` header or an `import` directive is formatted exactly as `fmt-module` formats it, so `fmt FILE --check` works on every file the compiler accepts.
- [CHECK-LIBRARY-1] `mylang check-module FILE.my` checks a library module and its reachable imports, including ordinary and generic bodies, effects, public type boundaries and configured policy, warning and interface-file gates, without requiring or synthesizing an executable entry point. It accepts `--value-semantics` with the same checker rules as executable checks. It leaves no executable check verdict; `check-project`, build and run retain their main requirements. Libraries containing only type or generic declarations are valid.
- [CHECK-LIBRARY-2] `mylang check FILE.my` checks a file that declares no free function `main` as a library, exactly as `check-module` does (bodies, effects, public type boundaries, configured warning and interface-file gates) and succeeds silently when it is well-typed, so the module an agent just edited needs no second command. This holds for a headerless single file and for a module or import entry, an empty file included; a type error in the library is reported as itself, never as `E_MAIN`. A file that declares `main` of any signature keeps the executable check, so a wrong signature, capability parameter list or generic `main` is still rejected with `E_MAIN` or `E_TYPE`. Commands that need an entry point (`run`, `build`, `check-project`, and `check --explain-ownership`, which analyzes a lowered program) keep `E_MAIN`, whose message names `mylang check FILE`. The library verdict is never cached as an executable one. `--diagnostics=json` reports the same diagnostics as text. `check` takes a file, not a directory. `check-module` is unchanged: it always checks as a library, even when `main` is declared, and requires a module header.
- [FMT-CHECK-LOCATION-1] A failed `fmt --check`, `fmt-module --check` or `fmt-project --check` reports the file path and one-based line and column of its first difference from canonical formatting, including a missing final newline at the original end of file. Clean files remain silent and unchanged; syntax errors retain their existing diagnostics.
- [FMT-TEST-MODULE-1] Formatting a headerless `*_test.my` file permits imports of sibling project modules, matching the test runner's accepted source shape. `fmt` and `fmt-module` preserve the absent header instead of reporting `E_MODULE_HEADER` or inserting a synthetic declaration; ordinary project modules keep their header requirement.
- [TEST-SIBLING-HINT-1] When `mylang test FILE.my` selects no tests or benchmarks and a sibling `FILE_test.my` exists outside the loaded graph, stderr names that sibling and explains how to run it explicitly or through a directory invocation. The existing exit status 3 and JSON report remain unchanged; a missing or already-loaded sibling produces no hint.
- [FMT-CLAUSE-SPACE-1] `fmt` and `fmt-module` keep one space between an identifier and a `]` that closes a type application or subscript, so a signature whose return type ends in brackets keeps its clause a separate word (`-> Option[int] effects():`, `-> list[T] noalloc:`, including inside a callback type) instead of printing `Option[int]effects()`; the `]` of an array length prefix still binds an identifier element type (`[2]u.Value`), and the output is idempotent.
- [TOOL-6] Includes calls in untaken paths and cycles when calculating static function reachability.
## Evaluation and wire boundaries

- [WIRE-2] Rejects duplicate input names, keywords, unsupported input types, and noncanonical or out-of-range values.
- [WIRE-3] Encodes independently constructed typed values without consuming them and enforces byte, value, and depth limits.
- [WIRE-4] Rejects duplicate, missing, unknown, and private function entries in domain codec declarations.
## Command line

- [DECLARATION-LIST-1] `list-declarations` prints one parse-only `declarations-v1` JSON document with the declared module name or null for a single-file program, its import names and explicit aliases, its export directives (a re-export carries its qualifier as `alias`), and every top-level declaration's kind, name, export flag, byte range and attached comment start. The kinds are exactly the top-level constructs the parser accepts: alias, const, struct, enum, union, result, domain, resource, fn and host_fn. An export directive sets the named local declaration's flag, a re-export marks no local declaration, and unknown and duplicate export directives reject with the existing located diagnostics. Listing requires only parsing, so modules with type or ownership errors still list, and no name resolution, checking or execution is claimed.
- [INTERFACE-SIGNATURE-1] `list-interfaces` prints each exported callable's whole signature by default: parameters in their Python type spelling (capabilities as `Filesystem`, `Network`, `Clock`, `Entropy`, `Environment`, never the removed `*_cap` internal names), the inferred `effects(...)` set when the body checks, `mutates(...)` by parameter name, and the declared ceiling when the source wrote one. A body that fails to check leaves the declaration listed and reports no body diagnostic, so the interface gate stays declaration-only. `--contracts` renders the same Python capability spellings in its canonical signature text.
- [INTERFACE-SIGNATURE-CLI-1] The `list-interfaces` command renders a capability entry parameter as its Python spelling (`Filesystem`, `Clock`) rather than the removed internal tag, including for a single-file program that exports `main`.
- [BUILD-TIMINGS-1] A native build's `--timings=json` request is carried to the linker step as an argument rather than as a new build-option field, the usage text names it, and only that spelling is a build control: a bare `--timings`, a value attached to it, and the switch written after `--` (where it belongs to the guest program) are rejected or ignored exactly as an ordinary build control is.

- [DECLARATION-LIST-2] Listed byte ranges are zero-based, half-open, disjoint and ordered by start. A declaration runs from its first token through its terminating semicolon or final token before an implicit newline/EOF terminator, and its comment start covers contiguous leading `#` lines with no blank line before the declaration. The module header, import statements, export directives and comment-and-declaration ranges account for every byte of the file except whitespace and line comments between them.

- [DEPENDENCIES-1] `project-dependencies` emits a versioned checked module graph with resolved imports, source byte hashes, implementation identity and exact consumed generated-input manifest hashes. It does not execute guest code; invalid projects emit no complete receipt.

- [DISK-PROJECT-1] Interpreter application hosts load `main.my` and imported modules from an explicit disk directory, with bounded reads and no embedded-source fallback. Failed loads and compilation release owned storage and preserve located source errors; a later invocation reads repaired source. Existing typed host registrations remain mandatory. The standalone acceptance driver also changes guest source and project roots using the same executable without Zig or Guardian on PATH.

- [CLI-1] Distinguishes source errors, runtime traps, and invalid command usage through actual process results.
- [RC-ARGV-OWNERSHIP-1] Native RC entry startup owns its argument list across capability admission and the borrowed call to `main`, including nonzero returns and traps. Each argument text constructor transfers exactly one edge to the list; conversion or append refusal releases all partial startup owners before reporting failure.
- [ARGV-1] A guest entry point may take `args: list[str]` as its first parameter; `mylang run` and `build --run` reserve their own options before `--` and pass every value after it as guest arguments, installed executables pass OS arguments after `argv[0]`, and generated native entry points build the same managed list with validated UTF-8 text.
- [NATIVE-ARGV-1] A native executable from `mylang build` hands `main(args)` every argument after `argv[0]` unchanged — `--`, `--gc-stress`, `--print-result`, `--max-steps=N`, `--restrict` and `--allow-*` included — and reads its runtime options only from the `MYLANG_OPTIONS` environment variable, before `main` runs: the `--restrict`, `--allow-*`, `--max-steps=N`, `--max-frames=N`, `--timeout=SECONDS` and `--print-result` spellings `mylang run` takes, separated by whitespace, where a double-quoted stretch keeps its whitespace and a backslash inside it escapes the next character. An unknown option, a budget that is not a positive decimal or is repeated, or an unterminated quote exits 2 (`mylang: MYLANG_OPTIONS names --engine=vm, which is not a runtime option`); `--timeout` arms the wall-clock guard; `--max-steps` and `--max-frames` are refused as QBE-STEP-ACCOUNTING-1 and QBE-FRAME-ACCOUNTING-2 say. `mylang run`, `run-project` and `build --run` keep their own command lines: the grants and `--print-result` given before `--` travel to the program in `MYLANG_OPTIONS`, replacing any inherited value, the budgets are compiled in, and only the values after `--` reach its argv.
- [CHECK-PROJECT-ENTRY-1] `mylang check ENTRY` where ENTRY opens as a module project checks the reachable project graph exactly like `check-project`, executing nothing; a plain single-file program keeps the one-unit path unchanged.
- [EPRINT-1] `eprint` is the standard-error spelling of `print`: it accepts the same values and `sep=`/`end=` keywords, writes only stderr, contributes the same `output` effect, and shares the buffered one-line native output path without mixing bytes between streams.
- [STREAM-CAPABILITY-1] `Stream` is a capability passed like `Filesystem`. The entry point may declare `out: Stream` and `err: Stream`; the entry rule matches Stream parameters by name, admits at most one of each, and rejects any other name with `E_MAIN`. `stream.print(values..., sep=, end=)` renders like the old printing builtin and `stream.write(text) -> int` writes the text and returns its character count; both contribute the `output` effect and take the destination from the receiver value, so a helper declared `def show(log: Stream)` may be handed either `out` or `err`. Streams are granted unconditionally, including under `--restrict`, and the per-type native renderers write through the stream's own sentinel. A bare `print`/`eprint` reports `E_OUTPUT_CAPABILITY` with the fix to add `out: Stream` and pass one from main; a local binding or declared function named `print` still shadows the spelling.
- [STREAM-BYTES-1] `stream.write(data: bytes) -> int` writes the raw octets, including NUL and invalid UTF-8, without quoting or an added newline, flushes the selected stream, and returns the byte count. The existing `str` overload still returns its Unicode scalar count. Both preserve destination capability and the `output` effect, reject other argument types, and trap `R_OUTPUT` on a refused write or flush; byte output performs no intermediate text conversion or managed allocation.
- [STREAM-BYTES-2] `stream.write(buffer)` accepts the mutable `byte_buffer` and writes the octets it holds at that moment straight out of the buffer's own storage: no snapshot is built, no copy is made, and the write contributes the same `output` effect and returns the same byte count as writing a byte string, so an `effects(output)` function may call it and `noio` withholds it with `E_EFFECT_BOUND`. Appending to the buffer afterwards cannot change what was already written, the lowered form is one raw byte-sink call naming the receiver, and a program that only writes a buffer links no copy, slice or snapshot helper.
- [DEBUG-LINE-1] `debug(values...)` renders like `print` to standard error, takes no capability, contributes no effect, returns unit, and is emitted only by Debug builds and by `run`/`test` — a ReleaseFast `build --project` drops it as a single-file one does; a debug line therefore never changes a signature or a checked effect bound.
- [RESULT-MAIN-1] A guest `main` may return `Result[int, str]`: `Ok(code)` becomes the process status `code & 0xFF`, while `Err(message)` writes one `error: message` line to stderr, followed by the error return trace (ERROR-TRACE-2), and exits 1; runtime traps and source/usage failures keep their existing distinct statuses. The synthesized entry wrapper that renders the error is the runtime's exit protocol and is not held to `main`'s signature-derived effect ceiling, so a `main` with no `out`/`err` stream parameter may still return `Err`.
- [RESULT-MAIN-2] `main` may return `Result[int, E]` or `Result[None, E]` for any error type `E` that `print` can render — an enum, a union, a record, `str` or an integer. `Ok` keeps the status rules of RESULT-MAIN-1; `Err(value)` writes `error: value` and the error return trace to stderr (ERROR-TRACE-2) and exits with `value & 0xFF` when `E` is `int` and 1 otherwise. An error type `print` cannot render (a capability, a file, a callback) still rejects with `E_TYPE`.
- [OPTION-TRY-1] `?` propagates `None` only within a function returning a compatible `Option[T]`; a `Some(value)` unwraps to `value`, and mixing `Option` propagation with a result-returning function or vice versa is a located type error.
- [OPTION-TRY-3] Option propagation does not require the operand's element to match the enclosing function's: `?` on an `Option[T]` inside a function returning `Option[U]` unwraps `Some(value)` to a `T` and returns a fresh `None` of `Option[U]`, for any `T` and `U` (scalar, managed or class, concrete or generic), because `None` carries no payload. Propagating an option into a function that does not return an `Option` still rejects with `E_TRY_RETURN`.
- [BOUNDARY-CLI-1] `examples/boundary-cli` is a two-module acceptance program that combines `args: list[str]`, `Filesystem`, explicit `Environment`, `print`, `eprint`, source-file reads, and `main -> Result[int, str]`, and the same cases pass through `run`, `build --run`, and an installed executable.
- [CAPABILITY-METHOD-NARROWING-1] `fs.read_only() -> Filesystem` narrows a `Filesystem` capability to read authority by intersecting its permission mask. The derived capability is an ordinary typed value: it can be passed, returned, stored in records/classes, and cannot be stored in a list or `Cell`. It performs no I/O and contributes no effect.
- [CAPABILITY-METHOD-NARROWING-2] `restrict_resource[...]` is removed and reports `E_REMOVED_SPELLING` naming `fs.read_only()`; `acquire_resource`, `complete_resource`, `read_resource` and `write_resource` are unchanged; `environment_get[...]` is now a trusted standard-library spelling only.
- [FILESYSTEM-DIR-1] `fs.dir(path) -> Filesystem` derives a filesystem rooted at the string-literal `path`, resolved under the receiver's root. It records the scope lazily in a bounded host-side table, performs no I/O, and contributes no effect. Every acquisition through the derived capability joins the prefix and rejects a request that could leave the root (`..`, `.`, empty components, backslashes or NUL); `restrict_resource` preserves the scope. A dynamic path or a literal that escapes the root is `E_FILESYSTEM_DIR` at check time, and a non-`Filesystem` receiver is rejected.
- [FILE-METHOD-1] Every whole-path file helper also accepts a qualified method spelling on a `Filesystem` value: `fs.open(path, mode)`, `fs.read_text(path)`, `fs.write_text(path, text)`, `fs.read_file(path)`, `fs.write_file(path, data)`, `fs.exists(path)`, `fs.listdir(path)`, `fs.mkdir(path)` and `fs.remove(path)`. The receiver is the capability operand, so the method form has one fewer argument than the free spelling, and both share the same arities, result types, `E_ARITY`, the literal-mode `E_FILE_ARGUMENT` rule and the trapping `with fs.open(...) as f:` head. The method belongs to the receiver, so a declared function or local named `read_text` still claims the free `read_text(fs, path)` spelling, and generic bodies keep rejecting the file builtins under either spelling.
- [NETWORK-HOST-1] `net.host(name) -> Network` narrows a `Network` capability to the single string-literal endpoint `name`. It records the scope lazily in the same bounded host-side table as `fs.dir`, performs no I/O, and contributes no effect. Every acquisition through the derived capability requests exactly the recorded endpoint, so a narrowed `Network` composes with a `connect` wrapper that takes no request string; an endpoint outside the active `--allow-endpoint` grant is still denied. Re-narrowing to a different endpoint traps `R_CAPABILITY`. A dynamic, empty or NUL-bearing name is `E_NETWORK_HOST` at check time, and the receiver must be a local `Network`.
- [NETWORK-ENDPOINT-1] `mylang run`, `run-project` and `build --run` accept a repeatable `--allow-endpoint=IP:PORT` grant for a `Network` entry point under `--restrict`. The endpoint is a literal numeric IPv4 address with a nonzero port; a DNS name, an IPv6 literal, an empty value and a repeat are usage errors. The grant provisions the native scoped network provider, whose allowlist matches the request verbatim (no DNS resolution or redirect following) and dials it only on an allowed acquisition; `--allow-all` grants every literal endpoint. Without the grant, `--restrict` refuses the entry point before execution with `--allow-endpoint=IP:PORT` in the message, and an endpoint outside the grant fails as `Connected.err(2)` without opening a socket. A default invocation with no grant keeps the previous behavior: the network provider stays absent and network access is denied.
- [NETWORK-SURFACE-1] The `network` standard module wraps provider acquisition and I/O so a guest does not spell the raw builtins: `connect(net)` acquires a `Connection`, `read[const N](connection)` and `write[const N](connection, data)` transfer bytes, and `close(connection)` completes the handle. The wrappers return declared `result` types and contribute the `network` effect; because resources are linear, each wrapper consumes the connection it receives.
- [ENVIRONMENT-GET-1] On an `Environment` capability value, `env.get("NAME")` returns `Option[str]`: `Some(value)` decoded as UTF-8 for a value the provider exposes and `None` when the name is absent. The name must be a literal `str`, and a dynamic name is `E_ENVIRONMENT_NAME`. A name outside the active `--allow-env` allow-list is a denied authority and traps `R_CAPABILITY` rather than answering `None`. `environment_get[...]` is accepted only in embedded standard-library sources, like the `__float_sqrt` intrinsic.
- [ENVIRONMENT-ONLY-1] `env.only("HOME", "PATH") -> Environment` narrows an `Environment` to a literal tuple of names by intersecting it with the runtime `--allow-env` allow-list. It performs no I/O and contributes no effect, and the derived capability is an ordinary typed value. Only positional string literals are accepted; a dynamic, empty or NUL-bearing name is `E_ENVIRONMENT_NAME`.
- [ENVIRONMENT-NAMES-1] `env.names() -> list[str]` returns the visible environment names as a fresh sorted managed list, honouring both the `--allow-env` allow-list and any `env.only(...)` narrowing. It takes no arguments and contributes the `environment` effect. A native provider's opaque callback context never contributes names.
- [VALUE-EQUALITY-1] Values of the same non-callback, non-Cell aggregate type support `==` and `!=`: plain records, fixed arrays, unions, `Result` and `Option` compare structurally, and a managed `class` compares by reference identity. Reference cycles compare by identity, so a self-referential graph never diverges. A type whose fields reach a callback, `Cell` or resource still rejects with `E_TYPE`, and equality keeps left-to-right evaluation and the existing execution limits.
- [EQUALITY-CONTEXT-1] The two operands of `==` and `!=` share one aggregate type, and an operand reached through a field access over a known value supplies that type to a contextual constructor on the other side exactly as a plain binding does, so `hold.opt == Some("x")`, `hold.opt == None` and `hold.kind == Kind.atom("x")` type-check without an intermediate local. A field access whose base type or field is not statically known supplies nothing, so `Some("x") == Some("x")` still reports `E_OPTION_INFERENCE`.
- [EQUALITY-CONTEXT-2] Equality context follows mixed field and list, dictionary or fixed-array index paths from a known binding, in either operand position, so `rows[i].items != []` and `[] == table[key]` infer the empty list's element type. Recovering the type does not evaluate an index or change left-to-right evaluation; two empty lists without a typed operand still report `E_LIST_INFERENCE`.
- [OPTION-TRY-2] Generic body checking propagates `Some(value)` and `None` through `?` when the enclosing function returns a compatible `Option[T]`, and rejects propagation into a result-returning function with a located diagnostic.
- [CAPABILITY-DIAG-1] When an entry point requests several ungranted capabilities under `--restrict`, `run`, `build --run`, and installed executables stop at the first failed grant and emit exactly one identical diagnostic.
- [ARGV-LIMIT-1] Guest arguments accepted after `--` use dynamically sized storage, so `run` does not impose a lower fixed argument limit than `build --run` or an installed executable.
- [INCREMENTAL-ID-1] Module, declaration, and body identities are derived from canonical names and declaration/body content rather than source offsets; private body and comment edits preserve declaration identities and the project exported-interface fingerprint, while exported signature changes alter the interface fingerprint. The checked `project-dependencies-v1` receipt exposes these identities and fingerprints under `incremental`.
- [INCREMENTAL-INTERFACE-1] The project interface fingerprint is composed from the sorted semantic interface fingerprints of exported interface items only, so exports follow exported constants, declared effect ceilings and parameter defaults while comments, formatting, source paths, spans and private declarations are excluded.
- [EFFECT-CEILING-1] An explicit `noalloc`, `noio` or `effects(...)` on any function, exported generic template, public domain operation or exported test narrows the ceiling the signature itself implies, and is enforced at the declaration through direct, recursive, generic and callback calls; exceeding it rejects with a located witness path. The declared mask, the `mut` parameter positions and the exported return-origin bound remain part of the public contract and interface fingerprint: a body edit that stays within the ceiling preserves the interface fingerprint while the body hash and runtime-test receipts still change, a declared `mut` position change alters the function and project interface hashes, and an omitted clause leaves the ceiling the parameters imply ([DERIVED-EFFECTS-1]). Parameter defaults, keyword parameter names, nominal type identities and generic requirements remain part of the public contract.

- [EFFECT-CEILING-INFERRED-1] Exported declarations do not require an explicit effect ceiling: the published ceiling is the set the parameter list implies, narrowed by a clause when the source wrote one, so a plain `public def` is accepted and its contract is still a real bound. Pure interface pre-validation without checked bodies publishes that same derived ceiling instead of the unbounded `all` mask an omitted clause used to mean, and the published `effects` fact stays the checked body fact ([DERIVED-EFFECTS-4]).

- [EFFECT-LITERAL-1] A compile-time `str` literal contributes no `managed_allocation` in the typed effect walk or the bytecode validator, so `print("hi")` under `effects(output)` and `len("abc")` under `noalloc` are accepted; concatenation, slicing, conversion and `list`/`dict`/`bytes` literals still allocate.
- [EFFECT-INDEX-1] An element read contributes `managed_allocation` only when its receiver is a `str`, whose element is a fresh one-character string; reading a `str` or `bytes` element out of a static array or dictionary hands back the stored reference and allocates nothing. The typed effect walk and the bytecode validator charge the read by the same receiver rule, so `names[0]` on a `[3]str` is accepted under `noalloc`, and a whole program that reads one lowers under `run`, `build` and `emit-project-qbe` instead of failing validation with an internal error.

- [MUTATION-OVERLAP-1] A read-only argument receives an independent value even when a same-call `mut` argument reaches its source, so it never causes `E_MUTATION_OVERLAP`. Under `--legacy-sharing`, a declared write footprint retains the writable/read-only overlap obligation; an unannotated read-only helper has no such obligation.
- [MUTATION-METADATA-1] `effects(mutation)` names externally observable managed-state writes, and a signature may carry the optional `mutates(a, b)` clause after its effect ceiling; names normalize to sorted duplicate-free parameter positions, unknown names reject at the declaration, and the clause requires the mutation bit only when an effect ceiling is declared. Callable types may bind parameter names in the same clause without changing signature identity. The compiler records inferred write footprints, mutation write facts and a sound return-origin bound, preserves them through copying, relocation, generic substitution, callable identity, interface schemas and bytecode validation, rejects forged or missing IR summaries, and versions the serialized effect contract as `effects-v3` so older entries are never admitted. M1 records these facts only; alias, overlap and ordinary call-permission enforcement is the separate M2 guarantee.
- [UNUSED-IMPORT-1] A direct `import` whose alias or dotted name no reference in its module resolves through is diagnosed as `W_UNUSED_IMPORT` at the imported name, beside `W_UNUSED_BINDING`. The evidence is the interface index's resolved-import flags, not an identifier scan: a qualified reference the checker accepted marks the import it resolved through, a binding that reused the first segment of a dotted path stays an ordinary value, and the alias inside a string literal or comment is not a reference. A cached body that was replayed rather than re-checked recovers its usage from the qualified names in its module's own source, so a warm run reports the same imports as a cold one. The warning is `W_*` and therefore rejecting under a configured `lint.warnings: "deny"` exactly like the other codes, and `import_policy` continues to evaluate every resolved direct import, used or not.
- [MUTATION-ENFORCE-1] A function cannot write caller-owned state through a read-only argument. Value transfers through records, arrays, options, unions, collections, helper results and wrappers are independent; local copies can be changed without changing their source. A `mut` argument writes through the caller's slot. Under `--legacy-sharing`, reachable mutable origins propagate through those containers, and a declared writable input graph overlapping a read-only argument is rejected unless proven disjoint. Callee footprints substitute into actual arguments to a fixed point through recursion, unused generics, specializations and indirect callbacks; analysis-budget exhaustion widens conservatively with a located limitation. The guarantee is call-duration only and makes no total/trap-free/allocation-free/concurrency claim.
- [INCREMENTAL-SPEC-1] A generic specialization key mixes the template identity, canonical concrete arguments, the concrete provider type shape (fields, variants and layout flags), the target ABI and the compiler build identity; a provider-shape edit invalidates the key even when the template is unchanged.
- [INVALIDATION-EXECUTION-1] An invalidation report records actual query work: a stage a command did not run is reported `skipped`, never as reuse; lowering, emission and linking consume body and emitted-fragment inputs, so a body edit invalidates them; and a rejected check emits a best-effort explanation against the previous baseline without publishing a successful verdict.
- [WORK-REPORT-1] Ordinary check, build, run and session commands emit a versioned `work-report-v1` that separates an unchanged fingerprint from actual result reuse and reports each of parsed modules, checked bodies, instantiated specializations, lowered functions, emitted fragments, links and executed tests as `hit | miss | skipped` with fresh-performed and reused counts. A stage a command did not run is `skipped` and is never counted as reuse; bypass reasons and cumulative-versus-request session deltas are included. Existing `frontend-cache-v1` and `invalidation-report-v1` shapes remain comparison-only and are documented for migration.
- [INVALIDATION-BASELINE-1] A baseline is compared only after its version, implementation identity, inputs array and incremental module table validate; a structurally invalid baseline makes every executed stage miss with `corrupt-baseline` instead of comparing missing fields into hits.
- [CLI-2] Reports missing arguments and missing source files as usage or host failures.
- [EXIT-STATUS-1] The entry point's `int` result is the process exit status, the way a Python or Go program returns an exit code, truncated to the low 8 bits (POSIX `waitpid`: `status = value & 0xFF`), for `mylang run`, `run-project` and a native executable from `mylang build`. So an entry returning 7 exits 7 and one returning 256 exits 0. `--print-result` (in `MYLANG_OPTIONS` for a built binary) still writes the full value to standard output and is orthogonal to the status, so a program can print its result and exit with it at once. A runtime trap outranks the return and exits 1, because the program never reached it; source and check errors exit 1 and usage, capability and host failures exit 2. Unit and Result entries adapt first ([PYTHON-CORE-2]): a `unit` or `Ok(unit)` entry exits 0, an `Ok(n)` or `int` entry exits `n & 0xFF`, and an `Err(code)` entry exits `code & 0xFF`.
- [CLI-3] Executes the plan acceptance programs and recursive functions through the command-line executable.
- [CLI-4] Schema generation reads declared input manifests into generated modules on every invocation and rejects malformed or duplicate inputs. A generated module is ordinary source — a `#` provenance comment, a `module` line, `export` directives and one `def` per value — and spells a boolean `True`/`False` even though its manifest spells it the way JSON does.

- [CACHE-1] Reusing unchanged module syntax preserves fresh semantic checks and changed results, with independent returned storage and bounded cache retention.
- [FRONTEND-CACHE-1] Ordinary one-shot `check`, `build`, `run` and `test` commands reuse parsed module syntax from a persistent content-addressed cache under `--cache-dir` (default `.mylang-cache/frontend`), keyed by the compiler build identity, the exact source path and bytes and the module body start. Declaration collection, symbol/export resolution, interface visibility, ownership, effects and every typed body still run fresh, and a changed byte yields a miss. Damaged, truncated or foreign entries fall back to a full parse, and `--no-cache` disables reuse.
- [CLI-5] Persistent checking sessions reuse unchanged parses and allow clearing retained cache storage between requests.
- [CLI-6] The removed C-backend command names `emit-c` and `emit-project-c` fail with a migration that names `emit-qbe` and `emit-project-qbe`; no live C backend or VM command remains in the CLI surface.
- [FRONTEND-CACHE-2] An ordinary one-shot `check-project` may reuse a successful whole-program verdict from the persistent cache only after loading every module fresh and comparing each module name, path and source byte against the content-addressed key; reused warnings are replayed and interface/semantic work is skipped. Failed checks are never stored, a compiler or diagnostic-format change misses, and `--report`/`--timings` expose reuse and miss counts.
- [CACHE-CHECKSUM-1] Persisted frontend syntax and verdict entries and checked-body store entries carry a 64-bit payload checksum taken from the leading bytes of the payload's BLAKE3 digest; an entry whose payload no longer matches it is damaged and treated as a miss.
- [FRONTEND-CACHE-3] Cached module syntax round-trips every field of the parsed program, including expression lambdas, so a warm lookup checks the same program a cold parse would.
- [FRONTEND-CACHE-RETAIN-1] The persistent frontend cache is trimmed once per run that stored an entry, after the command and before its receipt, to the larger of its 128 MiB floor and twice the entry bytes the run read back or stored (its working set), evicting least recently used entries first: a successful read renews an entry's modification time. A project whose working set outgrows the floor (about 440k generated lines) therefore keeps every entry it uses, and an unchanged rerun evicts nothing, instead of every run evicting every entry.
- [FRONTEND-CACHE-ENTRY-1] Once a project is loaded, the frontend cache's per-entry ceiling (and retention floor) becomes the larger of 128 MiB and eight bytes per byte of the project's source. A project's check summaries are about one byte per source byte (39 MB at a million lines) and a module's syntax about eight per byte of that module, so no entry a project produces is bypassed at any size, while a damaged length still cannot make a read allocate more than the project could have produced.
- [CHECK-SCALE-LEX-1] Reserved words and removed spellings are recognised through compile-time maps, layout lowering finds line starts in one forward pass over the source, header discovery lexes each module into a scratch arena it resets before the next module (keeping only its diagnostics and import facts) and records which imports a dotted reference qualifies from the tokens it already holds (so the unused-import warning never lexes a module twice), and a reference spelling made only of identifier bytes and dots is normalized without lexing it. Every spelling lexes to the same tokens and every reference resolves exactly as through the full lexer: a keyword segment is still not a name, and a removed spelling is still left to the lexer, which reports `E_REMOVED_SPELLING`.
- [CHECK-SUMMARY-1] A successful one-shot `check` of a project records one summary per ordinary function — its stable declaration key, dependency context, the BLAKE3 digest of its declaration region, its propagated effect mask and mutation summary, the diagnostics it contributed with region-relative spans, and its direct callees — in one slot per entry of the frontend cache. The next `check` of that entry stands a summary in for every body whose context and region are unchanged: the body is neither checked nor copied, the effect and mutation passes seed it with its stored facts and report nothing of their own for it, and its stored diagnostics are replayed at the region's current offset. The entry point, constants, conversions, host and foreign declarations, and bodies whose checked tree names a specialization, a lambda or a type interned while checking are always checked. After the passes every checked body that had a summary must still have exactly its stored effect mask and mutation summary, and no call cycle may pass through both a checked and a stood-in body; a changed fact, such a cycle, or any error the passes report makes the check repeat in full with no stand-ins, so a check's diagnostics are always those of a check without summaries. `work-report-v1` counts a stood-in body as a reused `checked_bodies` body; `--explain-invalidation`, `--no-cache` and every command other than `check` use no summaries.
- [CHECK-SUMMARY-2] A command that lowers the whole checked project (`build`'s frontend `emit-project-qbe`, `run`, `run-project`) reads the same summaries but checks every body, because lowering needs its tree; a summary stands in only for an unchanged body's effect and mutation facts and its pass diagnostics, so the effect and mutation passes walk only changed bodies. The same fact and cycle conditions as `check` apply, and a violated one repeats the passes in full. Such a command publishes summaries only when it had none to stand in or repeated in full, and a request served by summaries neither reads nor publishes retained checked trees.
- [SEMANTIC-KEY-IDENTITY-1] A declaration's dependency fingerprint names its defining module and, for a function, its `@deprecated` reason. Retargeting an import alias to another module's byte-identical declaration therefore invalidates every body that references it (the new target has its own types and inferred facts), and deprecating a callee invalidates its cross-module callers, which then report `W_DEPRECATED` exactly as a cold check does.
- [SEMANTIC-KEY-TRANSITIVE-1] A declaration's dependency fingerprint is transitive and independent of the order declarations are keyed in: it covers its own digest and the own digest of every declaration it reaches, so a cycle of recursive or mutually recursive types folds every member into every member. A composite type spelling (`list[T]`, `dict[K, V]`, `Option[T]`, tuples, arrays, callback types) references each of its component types, a type declaration's own digest frames its source text (field types, defaults, `record`/`class`), and a name a facade re-exports binds the declaration it names. A field renamed, retyped or reshaped in a record reached only through a list element, a dictionary value, an Option payload, a return type, a type cycle or a facade therefore invalidates every body that reaches it, and an incremental check reports what a cold check reports.
- [CHECK-SHAPES-1] A check summary, a retained checked tree and a retained specialization also record the transitive shape digest (schema of the record and of every record its fields, cases, elements and callback slots reach) of every catalog record the checked tree touched. A summary is not stood in, and a retained tree is not reused (`record-shape-changed`), unless every such record still exists under its name with the same shape in the fresh catalog, however the body reached it. The checked-body store format is `semantic-cache-v2` and the summary format `check-summaries-v3`.
- [CHECK-SCALE-KEYS-1] Dependency-key preparation records, per function, the BLAKE3 digest of its declaration region and the names its syntax references; check summaries keep both. While a function's region digest is unchanged, the next preparation resolves the recorded names instead of walking the function's syntax again, which binds exactly the declarations and negative lookups the walk would, so every dependency digest is unchanged; a digest that no longer matches is ignored and the syntax is walked.
- [MODULE-INTERFACE-1] `mylang interfaces emit ENTRY.my [--out=DIR]` checks the project as one program and writes one `module-interface-v2` artifact per module (`<module>.myi` in `DIR`, by default `<cache-dir>/interfaces` with cache-dir `.mylang-cache`) and an `index.json` of each module's interface, implementation and positions hashes; `mylang interfaces show FILE.myi` renders one artifact canonically. An artifact holds the module's declaration text: its `module` line, the import lines that text uses, every exported declaration and, transitively, every declaration of the same module they name (a domain with all its members), copied verbatim, with each function body replaced by `pass` except those a dependent reads: generic templates, constants, conversions, host and foreign declarations, and functions the bytecode inliner may splice into a caller (in a lowering with or without `debug` statements). Each kept item is followed by the gap to the source's next top-level item, blanked except for its newlines, so a position past the item's last token maps back exactly. Beside the text it carries each replaced function's whole-program facts (effect mask, mutation summary, return origin, write facts), an interface fingerprint per declaration, the facade names it re-exports with their targets' fingerprints, and the CHECK-SHAPES-1 digest of every catalog record its declarations reach, its own or another module's. Where each verbatim stretch came from (offset, line, column) is a separate positions section, and an `emission` section, empty in an artifact built from a check alone, holds the facts a module compile adds (MODULE-COMPILE-2).
- [MODULE-INTERFACE-2] A module's interface hash covers its artifact except the positions: the declaration text without its blanked gaps, the imports, each function's stable key, role, export flag, facts and interface fingerprint (its declaration digest, role, facts and kept body, folded over every declaration those name in any module, in an order-independent way), each type's and re-export's fingerprint, and the shapes. An edit to a body the interface replaces that changes none of the module's exported facts leaves it unchanged, as do comments and blank lines between declarations, moved declarations and edits to private declarations nothing exported reaches; a signature, visibility, deprecation, type declaration, record shape (including a record reached only through `list[T]`, `Option[T]`, a dictionary or a union payload of another module), fact, template, constant or inlined body changes it. The implementation hash is the module's name and source bytes and the interface hash of each direct import, so a body edit changes only its own module's. Neither hash contains a path or a time.
- [MODULE-INTERFACE-3] `mylang interfaces check ENTRY.my --module=NAME --from=DIR` checks one module against the interfaces in `DIR`: the module's own source, and for every other module only its interface text, each declared function standing in by stable key with its interface facts, as a check summary stands in for a body, and the abstract template check taking their effect masks instead of walking bodies it does not have; the standard library is read from the compiler's own source; a module other than the entry is imported by a synthetic entry, and the check reads only the artifacts of the modules it loads. `mylang interfaces oracle ENTRY.my [--module=NAME] [--from=DIR]` checks every module that way, from its artifacts after an encode and decode (or those in `--from`), and compares each with the whole-program check: every function the module's check produced has the same checked contract (effects, mutation footprint, return origin, write facts) and signature types, every declaration of the module has the same dependency context, every body it lowered and inlined (a stood-in function keeps only its signature) is the whole program's instruction for instruction, with types resolved in the lowered catalog, callees by name and positions in their own module's source, every diagnostic it reports (positions in interface text mapped back to their source) is one the whole program reports, and together the modules report every whole-program diagnostic. A difference, a check that falls back to reading the bodies it stood in, or a project that does not check exits 1.
- [MODULE-INTERFACE-4] `mylang --help` lists `mylang interfaces emit|check|oracle <entry.my> | show <module.myi>`; `mylang interfaces` with no action, an unknown action or an unknown option prints its usage on stderr and exits 2.
- [MODULE-COMPILE-1] `mylang compile-module ENTRY.my --module=NAME --interfaces=DIR --out=DIR --arenas=on|off [--cache-dir=DIR] [--drop-debug] [--drop-step-checks] [--drop-frame-checks] [--drop-safety-checks] [--objects]` compiles one module from its own source and the interfaces in `DIR` (MODULE-INTERFACE-3), which its imports' compiles wrote: every function a declared interface function stands in for is lowered signature only (so the inliner never splices its `pass` body) and its units read, in place of that function's body, the emission facts its interface carries (whether a call to it may allocate, transitively, and which parameters it writes); an imported signature without a real physical body remains explicitly unknown, may allocate and write every parameter, and cannot prove uniqueness, readonly access, a borrow or a pin; physical function IDs must resolve to their archive names, and every signature-only dependency must agree with the named declaration, including declared mutation qualifiers, explicit effect ceilings and host or C-ABI declared effects, regardless of interface order before supplying facts; ordinary inferred body effects remain separate physical facts; physical record layouts omit source-local field-default node indexes, which remain the checked source's semantic authority; every other function the check produced (the module's own, the bodies interfaces keep, the standard library, every specialization) is lowered and analysed as the whole program analyses it, and a position inside an interface's text is reported at its place in the module's real source. It emits the module's functions, its lambdas, the entry adapter when it is the entry, and every specialization those reach; writes `DIR/NAME.myi` with its emission facts and `OUT/NAME.units.json` (its lean executable units, their fragment keys, its runtime-data blocks and the hashes of every interface it read); and with `--objects` compiles its units' batch and metadata objects into the build cache (`build --module-objects`). `--arenas` is the program-wide arena switch, which no one module can derive. For every module of a program compiled this way in import order, the units, their fragment keys, text and metadata digests and the emission facts equal the whole-program emission's, function by function; `emit-project-qbe --emission-report=FILE` writes the whole-program reference (every function's facts and keys, and every module's imports).
- [MODULE-COMPILE-2] A module compile's interface carries an `emission` section: the configuration its facts hold for (`debug=… steps=… frames=… checks=… arenas=…`), each declared function's may-allocate fact and parameter writes by checked name, the functions its units take as values, and whether its units open an arena or need the text runtime. The section is outside the interface hash; `emissionHash` covers the configuration and the functions' facts. A compile whose stood-in import carries no emission facts, or facts for another configuration, fails with `compile its module first`, exit 1.
- [MODULE-COMPILE-3] External `*.units.json` inputs require the current `module-units-v1` envelope with a named module, units array and manifest object; missing/unknown versions or malformed envelopes refuse linking before artifact use. `mylang link-modules ENTRY.my --units=DIR -o OUT [build options]` merges every `DIR/*.units.json` into one executable manifest — each unit once (two modules' copies of one specialization must be the same unit), the union of the modules' descriptor and renderer blocks rendered in symbol order as a whole emission renders them, the union of their host bridges and canonical wire/codec/invariant tables with conflicts rejected, the entry module's entry and the text runtime when any module needs it — and builds it with the ordinary driver, which finds every guest object in the cache the module compiles filled (`compiled_function_objects` is 0). The linked executable is the whole-program build's, byte for byte.
- [MODULE-PROJECT-1] A project with a `mylang.json` builds and runs module by module like any other: the per-module path loads the configuration and its import policy, checks the discovered graph against the source budgets the whole-program loader enforces (the configured ones, else the defaults: module count, import count, each file's bytes and all files' bytes) and every import edge against the policy (`import_policy.denies`, the evaluator's first matching rule or default), and a `run` of a project whose `lint.warnings` is `deny` stops with exit 1 after printing a warning, as the whole-program run does. None of these settings changes compiled output, so none keys a compile. A configuration or policy that does not load, a graph over a budget and an edge the policy denies go to the whole-program path, which reports them exactly as before (exit 2, 1 and 3).
- [MODULE-SYMBOL-CUTOFF-1] A module compile records, beside the hashes of every interface it read, what its output can depend on of those interfaces' emission sections: its reach (`hashes.reach`), the sorted names of every function its emitted functions, every record's codec and every invariant callback reach, transitively, through the function references the restored physical bodies spell (calls, function values, inline origins); and per input a `symbols` digest over that interface's emission configuration, whether its physical inputs count, every physical record row whose record the compile's own catalog lacks (its catalog's record names are part of the reach), the modules its signature-only dependency rows name, its invariant callbacks and, for each reached function, its emission facts (may-allocate, parameter writes, stable parameters, physical identity), its physical body row and its dependency row, each or its absence. A compile that reaches a body invoking a function value (which may call any function taken as a value), whose restored program names a function it does not hold or holds one name twice, or that only checks, records no reach.
- [MODULE-SYMBOL-CUTOFF-2] A module none of whose inputs changed but in an interface's emission hash (the interface hash, and the positions hash where its compile read positions, held) is not compiled again when its record has a reach, each such input has a `symbols` digest, that module's interface file is the one its current interface and emission hashes name, and the digest recomputed over it for the record's reach is the recorded one; the record then keeps the input's new emission hash. No reach, no digest, a missing or mismatched interface file or a different digest compiles the module as before. The state file is `module-build-state-v3`, so an earlier build's records are stale.
- [MODULE-SYMBOL-CUTOFF-5] A module compile whose emitted units report positions inside an import's interface text (an inlined or instantiated body) and that renders no diagnostic records, beside that interface's positions hash, the sorted indices of the stretches those positions are reported through and a digest of where every stretch lies in the text and, for each of those stretches, the source line and column it starts at and the text it spans (its blanked gap included). When that positions hash is all that changed of the input's positions, the scheduler keeps the module if the digest recomputed over the interface file there now (the one the module's current interface, emission and positions hashes name) is the recorded one, and the kept record states the new positions hash. A stretch's byte offset in its source is read by no unit, so a body edit that keeps the line count moves no dependent, and one that adds lines moves only dependents that took positions from stretches below it.
- [MODULE-ARCHIVE-TRIM-1] A module's physical archive holds the bodies of the functions it emits and every body their inline-origin chains name, a signature-only dependency row for every other function those bodies or the kept records' codecs name, and a row for every record the kept rows name, transitively; a function or record no kept row names is left out (a reference outside the catalog keeps every row). Inline origins must resolve to real instructions through bounded acyclic chains; malformed provenance refuses the archive. Restoring provenance bodies emits no additional guest function, but preserves logical frame admission and Result creation, propagation and return sites after separate compilation. A dependent's restore reads only names its rows spell, so its emitted units, and the linked executable, are those of an untrimmed archive, byte for byte.
- [MODULE-INTERFACE-CUTOFF-1] A module compile records, per interface its check read, the declarations of that module it used: those its own declarations and bodies name (resolved through facades), those its facade re-exports, and every function its emitted units call, take as a value or splice (a specialization by its template), as identities (`f` and a stable key, `t` and a type name); the digest of the fingerprints that interface states for them; and an outline digest of everything a lookup into it can find or miss: the imports its text names, every domain member and every function whose body the text keeps (inlined, template, constant, evaluated, conversion) by key with whether it is exported, and each kept body's fingerprint, since every reader checks that body and takes the types it instantiates into its record catalog (a free function whose body is `pass` is found only by a qualified lookup that spells it, since there is no `from` import, so only a reader that used it depends on it); every re-export with its target's fingerprint, every type declaration with its fingerprint and every record shape its declarations reach. It records none for an interface that does not state one of the used declarations (a lambda, a compiler-made function), and none at all when it renders a diagnostic; an interface it read only physical rows of is recorded as `unread`.
- [MODULE-INTERFACE-CUTOFF-2] A module whose input changed its interface hash is not compiled again when that input's outline and used-declaration digests, recomputed over the interface file there now (the one that module's current hashes name), are the recorded ones, and the input's positions and emission changes hold by MODULE-SYMBOL-CUTOFF-5 and MODULE-SYMBOL-CUTOFF-2; an `unread` interface always holds, and a test run never cuts an interface change off. The kept record states the new hashes. The state file is `module-build-state-v4`.
- [MODULE-CHECK-1] `mylang check` of a project entry that declares `main`, given no option but `--cache-dir`, checks it module by module: the scheduler (MODULE-SCHEDULE-1) runs `compile-module --check-only --ready` for every stale module under a check variant of the cache (`<cache-dir>/modules/`, default `.mylang-cache` or `MYLANG_FRONTEND_CACHE`), which checks the module against its imports' interfaces, reads no import's emission facts, writes its interface with an empty emission section and `NAME.result.json` with the warnings its check reported, and emits nothing. It prints every module's own warnings in the order the modules were found, as the whole-program check prints them, and exits 0, or 1 when the project's `mylang.json` denies warnings and one was reported. The whole-program check still answers a library entry, any other option, a friction log, a configuration, budget or policy failure, a module that does not check (the whole-program check reports it), and a foreign warning its owner's check did not report; the modules that did check keep their records, so the next check re-checks only what changed. Under the held program and standard variant locks, unchanged graph rows and state headers keep their existing state files without serialization or publication; changed rows, graph order, shared state headers and partial repairs retain atomic publication.
- [MODULE-TEST-1] `mylang test` of a project entry runs module by module. The scheduler compiles every stale module with `compile-module --tests`, which also writes `NAME.tests.json`: its share of the test facts (every function it emits, described as the whole program's facts describe it, but with no `indirect` or codec-runtime edge, the two program-wide sets), the schema and export descriptions of its tests and benchmarks, and its wire and codec tables. The run merges the shares into the facts of the tests' closure (`native_test_facts.mergeShares`), adding each invoking body's `indirect` edges to the union of the modules' value sets and, when any module declares a codec, every body's `codec` edges to the union of their codec sets; plans and replays receipts from those facts as before; and links the library of the tests it runs from the modules' objects with a wrapper exporting those tests. Its report is the whole-program run's. `--whole-program` keeps the whole-program session, which `--no-cache`, `--release`, explicit link inputs, `--affected`, benchmarks, JSON diagnostics, a case configuration that names provider fixtures (for those cases) and a project the modules cannot build (which the session then reports) also take.
- [MODULE-TEST-3] Module by module, the tests a run executes are linked into one test executable with the harness (compiled once per cache as an object that calls the adapter directly, `MYLANG_TEST_LINKED`), from the modules' objects, the program-wide metadata unit and a wrapper exporting those tests, so no test process loads and relocates a library of the whole program. A test's report — its value, failure text, error return trace, trap and captured output — is the whole-program run's, including explicit entropy seeds.
- [MODULE-TEST-5] A module's contract entry is rendered as `list-interfaces --contracts` renders it (no null field), so a test's receipt identity is the same module by module and on the whole-program path, and a verdict recorded on one replays on the other. The first test run of a project of at least 64 modules (`MYLANG_TEST_SEED_MODULES` sets the count), when its test variant holds no records yet, is the whole-program run's, which compiles only the tests' closure in one pass; the run then starts a detached seeder (`mylang test` with the same arguments and `MYLANG_TEST_SEED=1`, no standard streams, never waited for; `MYLANG_TEST_SEED=off` starts none), which waits until the run has released the project's stable snapshot path, claims it, compiles every module into the test variant and exits, running no test and printing nothing.
- [MODULE-TEST-6] A test run never waits for a detached seeder. A run that cannot claim the project's stable snapshot path (a seeder or another run holds it) stages at a private path and is the whole-program run's, which replays the verdicts the receipts hold; it starts no seeder, and its trace names how it was staged (`stage stable`, `stage private`, or `stage seeding` when the holder marked the path as a seeder's). A run waits for no module variant either: while a seeder or another run holds its program's variant or the standard modules' shared one, the whole-program run answers. Only a seeder waits, for the path and the variants. Both seeders (MODULE-CHECK-2 and this one) run at nice 19, and so does every compile they start. A test seeder's mark names its process, which leads its own process group; a run staged beside it stops that group (SIGSTOP) for its length and lets it go on (SIGCONT) when it ends, so the run has the cores. A mark whose process leads no group or does not carry `MYLANG_TEST_SEED=1` stops nothing, and a private watcher resumes the same live seeder group after normal cleanup or foreground death, including SIGKILL of the foreground group. The watcher keeps a kernel process identity, closes every inherited descriptor, and exits when the seeder exits; failed setup stops nothing.
- [MODULE-TEST-2] A library's wire table and codec metadata are canonical: wire types sorted by name, codec types sorted by name with their descriptors, codecs by domain, and callbacks by symbol with every method numbered by its callback's position (`qbe_codecs.canonical`), so the tables a per-module test library merges from each module's own are the whole-program library's.
- [MODULE-SCHEDULE-5] A module compile builds its interface, with its emission section and hashes, once its allocation analysis is done and before it emits any unit, and hands it to the compile's `ready` callback. `compile-module --ready` writes `DIR/NAME.myi` there and prints `\x1emylang-module-ready INTERFACE EMISSION POSITIONS` on stderr, then emits its units, builds its objects (with the build options after `--`) and writes `OUT/NAME.result.json` and `OUT/NAME.shared.json` in place of `NAME.units.json`, so the scheduler starts the module's dependents while its code is still being generated.
- [INVALIDATION-REPORT-1] A checked project can be explained stage by stage against the previous `project-dependencies-v1` receipt: `--explain-invalidation` emits one `invalidation-report-v1` JSON line naming load, parse, declaration, interface, body, specialization, lowering, fragment and link hits and misses with the first changed dependency, a missing baseline is all-miss, a corrupt baseline is all-miss rather than a failure, and `--report` adds the same per-stage counts to `frontend-cache-v1`. The baseline lives under `<cache-dir>/invalidation/` and is not partitioned by compiler identity, so a compiler change can be explained.
- [INCREMENTAL-KEYS-1] Every incremental cache entry includes the compiler build identity, bootstrap-language version, target, runtime ABI, effects/capabilities contract and relevant flags through one contract fingerprint. Changing any dimension invalidates parsed syntax, whole-program verdicts, checked function bodies, generic-specialization keys and native object fragments even when the source bytes are identical.
- [INVALIDATION-MATRIX-1] For whitespace, comments, private bodies, exported signatures, effects, constants, record/union shape, imports, generic templates, specialization arguments, runtime ABI and compiler version, a warm check equals a clean rebuild in accepted status, diagnostic JSON, checked interface manifest, normalized QBE and reuse counts; missing or damaged cache data recomputes instead of trusting a partial entry.








- [NOMINAL-TYPE-SYNTAX-1] Nominal declarations retain explicit type parameters and requirements; nested named applications retain base and argument spans through parsing, formatting, syntax caching and module relocation. Lists are bounded at 16 and nesting at 256; module interfaces check exported type privacy, constructor kind, application arity and parameter shadowing.
