Malformed nominal declarations follow NOMINAL-TEMPLATE-CHECK-1; concrete specialization follows NOMINAL-INSTANCE-1.
- [GENERIC-SYNTAX-1] Generic syntax retains named type parameters, nonempty explicit requirement lists, and explicit `[...]` call type arguments through owned AST copying and formatting; duplicate parameters/requirements and lists beyond 16 are rejected. A specialized instance is named `f[i64]` after the arguments it was given, so a diagnostic and a disassembly name it the way its call site is written. Executable specialization must preserve these annotations through contract validation before lowering.

- [NOMINAL-INSTANCE-1] Concrete nominal records, unions, results and enums specialize by declaration and ordered semantic argument identities into complete runtime schemas. Construction, assignment, matching, result handling and owned-field flow use the ordinary checked IR/VM rules; concrete types compose with existing generic functions and numeric arrays. Unused templates and unexpanded phantom arguments do not become empty runtime records. Public type lookup requires exported bases and visible arguments, including phantom arguments. Symbolic nominal uses in generic function bodies follow NOMINAL-BODY-1. Canonical type display names have a compilation budget of 1 MiB or one byte per source byte, whichever is larger (NOMINAL-NAME-BUDGET-1), and schema export of a generic type constructor remains explicitly unsupported pending generic tooling.
- [NOMINAL-NAME-BUDGET-1] The display names of a compilation's nominal types may total 1 MiB or one byte per source byte, whichever is larger, and each name 1 MiB. A program's ordinary names grow with its declarations, so a large program is never refused for their total, while nested generic instances, whose names grow with the square of their depth, still stop a small program at 1 MiB with `E_GENERIC_LIMIT`.
- [CATALOG-INTERN-1] Materializing a type the catalog already holds adds no record and does not revalidate the catalog's layouts, and each identity is indexed once, when it is resolved, with the type index keeping the highest graph node per type. An instantiation whose type is already held, with no graph node added since the catalog last completed, returns that type without another pass over the graph. Checking cost per type use therefore does not grow with the number of declared records, and every distinct type is still interned exactly once.
- [NOMINAL-LAYOUT-1] Nominal declarations, including unused templates, have bounded finite storage graphs before executable specialization. Canonical symbolic identities preserve declaration, parameter scope and ordered arguments. Only stored fields/payloads and array elements expand; phantom arguments do not create storage edges, and zero-length arrays keep their element edge. A cycle through a type with no finite value rejects with E_RECURSIVE_RECORD and original closing-edge/related locations, and any other cycle is a recursive value type (RECURSIVE-VALUES-1); growing families reject with E_GENERIC_LIMIT at 4096 nominal instances, 4096 array shapes or 100000 expansion operations per declaration or materialized type (NOMINAL-LAYOUT-BUDGET-1). Graph IDs are not runtime type IDs and do not authorize execution.
- [NOMINAL-LAYOUT-BUDGET-1] The layout graph charges its instance, array-shape and operation limits to the request whose expansion first discovered a node: a declaration's symbolic root, or one concrete type the checker materializes. Each request gets the full bound, so a program's total may exceed it and the work stays proportional to the source; an exceeding request is reported at its declaration name or type spelling, with the type its expansion had reached as the related location.
- [NOMINAL-TEMPLATE-CHECK-1] Nominal declaration metadata resolves field and payload expressions to declaration identities and local parameters even in unused templates. It rejects unknown types, shadowing, duplicate members, malformed result schemas, application arity/kind errors and insufficient argument requirements. Copy guarantees propagate through ordinary and generic records/unions without copying owners or pending results; scalar domains and enums retain equality without integer operations. Expression checking is bounded at 256 levels and 100000 visits per top-level expression (NOMINAL-TEMPLATE-BUDGET-1). Metadata is not an executable layout: finite storage dependencies follow NOMINAL-LAYOUT-1; concrete instances follow NOMINAL-INSTANCE-1; symbolic nominal operations in generic function bodies follow NOMINAL-BODY-1.
- [NOMINAL-TEMPLATE-BUDGET-1] Each top-level nominal type expression — a field, payload, alias target, requirement or type spelled in a signature or body — starts a fresh budget of 256 levels and 100000 visits, so the bound is per spelling rather than per program; an expression whose alias expansion exceeds it is rejected with E_GENERIC_LIMIT at that spelling, with the type resolution had reached as the related location.
- [GENERIC-REQUIREMENTS-1] Builtin generic requirements are copy, equality, and integer, with integer implying equality and equality implying copy. Matching uses concrete equality rules and forbids implicit copying of owners or pending results; nominal integer-backed domains are not integers. Unknown requirement names and builtin type-variable shadowing are diagnosed even in uncalled templates before specialization.

- [GENERIC-BODY-1] The initial abstract generic body checker keeps type parameters distinct and requires declared integer/equality support for the corresponding operations. It checks pure builtin/type-parameter signatures, locals, returns, branches, loops, and casts without substituting representative concrete types. Unsupported forms and 256-depth/100000-work bounds reject explicitly; concrete specialization must retain these universal checks.

- [GENERIC-BODY-2] The abstract generic body checker applies the same wrapping conversion rule to `?` as the concrete path: when a propagated result's error type is not already the enclosing function's error type but is the payload of a case of that enclosing union, the propagation is accepted and the error is wrapped in that case; a symbolic union case payload compares structurally, so distinct type parameters still stay distinct.

- [GENERIC-ARRAY-1] Fixed numeric arrays may contain generic type parameters in signatures, constructors, annotations, nested arrays and explicit type arguments. Universal checking retains element requirements, exact lengths, nominal identities, copy/result obligations and source-located traps before specialization. Concrete shapes are interned by element identity and length, with bounded specialization; symbolic enum-keyed tables and parameterized lengths remain explicitly unsupported.
- [GENERIC-CALL-1] Abstract calls between pure generic templates resolve declaration identities and domain/module privacy, require complete explicit or uniquely inferred type arguments and value-argument arity, enforce requirement implication, and substitute parameter/result identities without merging distinct type variables. Recursive calls check signatures without expanding bodies; reachable concrete helpers must also pass the abstract pure-body rules.

- [GENERIC-HELPER-1] Abstract generic checking validates every transitively called ordinary helper against the supported pure builtin body rules. A bounded declaration worklist checks each reachable body once, including recursive cycles; uncalled ordinary bodies are left to concrete checking. Helper signatures alone cannot authorize hidden effects. Broader effect and ownership contracts remain unfinished.

- [GENERIC-INSTANCE-1] The compilation-local specialization registry interns resolved declaration identities and ordered concrete type identities, owns argument storage, preserves IDs across growth and allocation failure, and reuses recursive requests. It bounds discovery at 4096 instances per generic declaration (GENERIC-INSTANCE-BUDGET-1) and each argument list at 16. Nominal types remain distinct regardless of representation. Executable checking uses these identities without conflating them with source declaration ordinals.
- [GENERIC-INSTANCE-BUDGET-1] The specialization registry counts instances per generic declaration: each may have 4,096, so a program's total grows with its generic declarations (4,097 declarations instantiated once each check), while polymorphic recursion, which multiplies one declaration's instances, still stops with `E_GENERIC_LIMIT`.
- [GENERIC-SITE-BUDGET-1] Distinct array shapes and callback signatures are counted per source spelling that specializes them: one spelling may make 4,096 of each, and the 4,097th is refused with `E_GENERIC_LIMIT` at that spelling ("generic array specialization at this spelling exceeds 4096 distinct shapes", "callback specialization at this spelling exceeds 4096 distinct signatures"). A spelling makes a new one only when a generic body is instantiated at new types, so recursion through it still stops, while a program's total grows with its spellings: byte literals of thousands of different lengths are thousands of spellings with one shape each.

- [GENERIC-EXECUTION-1] Pure generic templates specialize on explicit or uniquely inferred concrete type arguments after universal body validation. Repeated and recursive calls reuse instances; per-instance bindings, expression resolution, widths and nominal types remain distinct. Ordinary entry/export wrappers execute instances through the checked native backend. Internal instances are excluded from direct module export/schema roots. Main cannot be generic; unsupported abstract forms still reject.





- [GENERIC-DIAGNOSTIC-1] Checked specializations retain explicit ordered concrete type arguments. Human-readable function names include those types, preserve qualified nominal names, and do not depend on discovery ordinals. Traces and reports use these names, while public-root selection uses specialization metadata rather than spelling.

## Named constraints

- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SYNTAX-1] A top-level `constraint NAME[SUBJECT] = REQUIREMENTS` names one subject binder whose right-hand side reuses the `+` requirement grammar. The parser rejects non-bare subjects, applied bounds such as `Prices[int]`, named or self references on the declaration's right-hand side, and requirement lists beyond the existing limit; the declaration and its bound references survive owned AST copying, relocation, formatting, declaration listing, and module-interface checks.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-1] Constraint declarations participate in the module declaration namespace and explicit exports with a distinct kind, resolve before signatures are checked, and accept bare and `alias.Name` function type-parameter bounds; an exported generic signature may use an exported constraint.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-2] Named bounds only apply to function type parameters. Records, unions, domains and constant-length parameters reject them, and a constraint name is not a value type: `policy: Prices` and `Prices[int]` report a declaration-kind error.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-3] Importing or naming a private constraint from another module rejects with the existing privacy diagnostic, an exported generic whose named bound is not exported rejects with `E_PRIVATE_INTERFACE`, and an undefined bound name reports `E_GENERIC_REQUIREMENT` at the bound.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-4] Constraint declarations obey existing namespace rules: a collision with another declaration or an import alias reports `E_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL` or `E_IMPORT_ALIAS`.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-5] Resolving a constraint identity outside the mapped project source is an internal error rather than a silently wrong declaration.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-6] A constraint subject may not shadow a module declaration, a direct import alias, or a builtin type, and an exported constraint's nested requirement signature may not expose private types.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-7] Same-module and imported exported constraints resolve to the same declaration identity through the ordinary direct-import and alias rules, so interface and semantic checking cannot disagree.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-CACHE-1] Constraint declarations and their named bound references round trip through the persistent parsed-syntax cache under the bumped frontend format version; the in-memory syntax cache copies them with the rest of the declaration tables.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-1] A named bound expands to the same builtin/field/operation contract as the equivalent inline bound, so generic calls, concrete conformance and forwarding accept and reject the same operations in both spellings, and independently declared names with equivalent requirements satisfy the same obligations.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-2] The declaration's subject substitutes by type-parameter identity recursively through supported composite signature forms, so an operation requiring `list[P]` accepts the provider's `list[Provider]` without rewriting source text or capturing a consumer's same-spelled name.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-3] Required operation effect ceilings compare as before: a concrete method whose declared effects are a subset is accepted, one that exceeds the ceiling rejects with `E_GENERIC_ARGUMENT`, and callers are checked against the full required ceiling.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-4] Forwarding uses the existing implication rules: a stronger named bound may forward to a weaker one, and a weaker bound cannot forward to a stronger one even when the forwarding function is never called. Named and inline forms behave identically.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-5] Expansion counts unique explicitly required members and builtins, including implied guarantees, before applying the existing 16-requirement limit, and reports `E_GENERIC_LIMIT` when the expanded contract exceeds it.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-6] Identical members from distinct bundles or from a bundle and an inline list coalesce; a repeated same-name field or operation with a different resolved type or effect ceiling rejects with `E_GENERIC_REQUIREMENT` and `E_PRIVATE_INTERFACE`-style related locations, and repeating one resolved declaration through two aliases rejects as a duplicate.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-7] Unused constraint declarations are still checked: an invalid subject, an unknown or shadowed name, a nested named reference, a structural field without `copy`, or an unknown requirement signature type rejects before any provider or call exists.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-FIXTURE-1] The acceptance fixture checks and tests its fake-only `fake_tests.my` entry successfully with `production.my` absent and with an invalid unimported `production.my`, and the production entry still rejects the invalid imported body once `main.my` imports it.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-NAMESPACE-1] Standalone programs reject duplicate `constraint` declarations with a located duplicate-symbol diagnostic, whether or not either declaration is used, so adding a module header does not change declaration-namespace rules.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-NAMESPACE-2] Standalone constraint names collide with records, variants, results, enums, domains, resources, type aliases and top-level functions in either declaration order, while non-constraint duplicate collisions keep their existing diagnostics.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-NAMESPACE-3] Standalone constraint lookup reports the duplicate-symbol diagnostic instead of silently selecting the first of several same-named declarations.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-NAMESPACE-4] Standalone valid unused and multi-constraint programs remain accepted.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SUBJECT-1] Standalone constraint subjects obey the same declaration-shadowing rules as module subjects, including unused declarations and collisions with types, aliases, functions and constraint names in either source order. Builtin type names remain invalid subjects; independent constraints may reuse a subject binder.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-CALLBACK-1] Structural requirement normalization resolves generic callback parameters and results recursively, preserving nested type identities and callback effect ceilings. Equivalent named and inline callback requirements coalesce and forward; mismatched parameter, result or effect contracts reject, and concrete callback aliases keep the same identity as their expanded signature.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-REVIEW-1] A permanent regression suite drives the review repros through the compiler: imported exported-constraint identity reads the defining declaration's symbol table; concrete names inside a named declaration resolve in that declaration's scope and cannot be captured by a same-spelled consumer parameter; merging compares resolved member identities and subject positions rather than source spelling; implied and named `copy` guarantees reach module preflight; the expanded requirement limit counts unique resolved members; checked schemas publish exported constraints, named bindings and expanded promises; and standalone declarations enforce namespace and full structural validity even when unused.








- [SOURCE-CODEC-1] Domain codec declarations remain specialist metadata, while the removed `codec_encode` and `codec_decode` source spellings report `E_REMOVED_SPELLING` and direct callers to the documented domain codec API.

- [CHECK-CACHE-1] A persistent checking session may reuse a successful verdict only after fresh project and manifest loading and exact comparison of every loaded module name, path and source byte. Hits replay owned warnings and skip interface/semantic work; failures are never retained. Retention is bounded and clear drops syntax and verdict caches while preserving cumulative observations.

- [GENERIC-CONCRETE-1] Abstract generic checking resolves concrete copyable nominal and fixed-array schemas without conflating them with type parameters. Concrete record/array construction and projection obey declared field, element and index types; malformed uncalled templates reject. Concrete owned flow follows GENERIC-OWNED-1; individual runtime operations require their own abstract rules. Concrete result obligations follow GENERIC-RESULT-1.

- [GENERIC-UPDATE-1] Generic bodies may update concrete copyable records and replace fixed-array/table elements. Field uniqueness and field/element/index types are checked before instantiation; normal specialization preserves immutable snapshots, evaluation order and runtime bounds. These operations do not infer structural capabilities for symbolic parameters.

- [GENERIC-VARIANT-1] Abstract generic checking supports construction and exhaustive matching of concrete copyable enums/unions. Cases retain nominal identities and exact payload types; each arm scopes its payload independently, duplicate/missing cases reject, and a match terminates only when every arm terminates. Named enum-table construction requires every key exactly once. Result-bearing variants follow GENERIC-RESULT-1; runtime-owned value flow follows GENERIC-OWNED-1.

- [GENERIC-RESULT-1] Abstract checking of concrete result-bearing generic values enforces single transfer, explicit handling, scope/return obligations, and agreement across continuing branches, match arms, loops and short-circuit paths before instantiation. Nested payloads retain obligations and aggregate operations cannot erase siblings or replaced results. Propagation requires compatible concrete result errors and rejects live result bindings or previously evaluated result temporaries on its early-return path. An explicit `_ =` release evaluates its operand. Symbolic result type constructors remain pending.



- [GENERIC-CLEANUP-1] Concrete resource ownership passed through generic functions retains its provider lifecycle. Explicit partial extraction/drop and implicit scope cleanup release each live resource exactly once, including allocation failures and traps; forwarding does not complete or duplicate an external resource.


- [GENERIC-PROVIDER-1] Abstract generic provider operations enforce concrete or symbolic nominal result contracts, exact capability kinds and fixed byte-array inputs before instantiation. Resource I/O requires a mutable unconsumed owner after argument evaluation. Acquisition/completion preserve resource ownership and permission attenuation cannot create authority. Direct clock/random/environment/filesystem/network effects propagate through helper calls and enforce bounds even in uncalled or unreachable source paths.







- [DIAGNOSTIC-JSON-1] CLI --diagnostics=json selects diagnostic-v1 JSON Lines on stderr without changing command results or exit statuses. Source and VM diagnostics preserve stable codes, severity, original-file byte ranges and byte-based line/columns, related/origin/requirement notes, bounded frames and omitted-frame counts. Project-loader related sites retain their own module coordinates. Auxiliary CLI messages are separate message records. Successful check-session warning replay uses the selected format, and cache identity prevents cross-format replay. Default text remains available; invalid command-line syntax and emergency output failures may use text.
- [DIAGNOSTIC-FIX-1] A diagnostic may publish a machine-applicable `fix` with one or more exact source edits. Each edit names an original file, a UTF-8 byte range and replacement text. An edit that cannot be mapped wholly into one original file suppresses the entire fix, preserving the diagnostic; text rendering and diagnostics without fixes keep their existing form.
- [DIAGNOSTIC-FIX-2] The diagnostics that carry a fix: `E_MUTATION_MARKER` for a missing call-site marker (insert `mut ` before the argument), a superfluous one (delete the `mut` and its blank), a written parameter not declared `mut` when the write is attributed to named parameters (insert `mut ` before each one's type; an unattributed write that lists every position is advice only), and a `mut` parameter the body never writes or whose type has nothing to write (delete the marker); `E_MODULE_NAME` (replace the declared name with the path-derived one); `E_REMOVED_SPELLING` where the migration is a rename — `push`→`append`, `List`→`list`, the `*_cap` names→their capability types, `f64_sqrt`/`floor`/`ceil`/`trunc`/`abs`→the float builtin — plus `&` where an argument begins (→`mut `) and a function declaration's `mutates(...)` clause (`mut ` on each named parameter, clause deleted); `E_EXPLICIT_DISCARD_REQUIRED` for a value that is not a result (insert `_ = `; discarding a result would drop its error, so that is left to the reader); `E_KEYWORD_ONLY` (insert `name=` before each positional operand the printed call names); and `W_UNUSED_IMPORT` (delete the import's whole line when the line holds only that import and an optional comment).
- [FIX-MIGRATION-1] `mylang fix ENTRY.my|DIR` runs this executable's `check --diagnostics=json`, applies every published fix whose edits collide with no edit already chosen — an identical edit reported twice applies once, and a colliding fix waits for the next round, which sees the updated source — and repeats until a check publishes none. It prints `fixed PATH: N edits (CODES)` per changed file and a total line on stdout, then the entry's remaining diagnostics on stderr in the `--diagnostics=text|json` format, each diagnostic once across entries. It exits 0 when every entry checks, 1 when errors remain, and 2 on a usage or host failure; `--check` writes nothing, prints `would fix` lines and exits 1 when a fix would apply. A directory argument fixes every `.my` file beneath it — files declaring `main` first, then `*_test.my` files, then the other modules, skipping hidden and `zig-out` directories and a module an already-fixed entry imports. `--cache-dir=DIR` and `--no-cache` pass through to the checks. `mylang --help` lists it among the everyday commands, and `mylang docs fix` prints its guide from any working directory. Fix application never guesses from diagnostic prose.
- [FIX-MIGRATION-2] `mylang fix` migrates a project across the call-site `mut` marker change in one command. The gate holds a pinned corpus from before that change — nine modules and tests of the value-semantics Lox port with every call-site `mut` marker stripped (`test/fixtures/fix-migration/lox/`) — and asserts that `mylang fix` on the directory restores all 91 markers, leaving a project whose every entry checks clean and on which `fix --check` finds nothing.






- [HOST-SOURCE-1] Contextual host fn declarations have no guest body and require an explicit effects(host, ...) contract. Only ordinary top-level, non-generic copied data signatures are supported; capabilities, managed collections and runtime-owned graphs reject. Main retains a guest body. Host and additional declared effects propagate through ordinary and abstract generic calls, including uncalled templates. Effect-free evaluation/invocation cannot execute these calls. Modules, formatting and disassembly preserve declarations; duplicate discovery excludes external implementations.


- [BOARD-TRIAL-1] The bounded board-validation experiment runs its guest as a native executable with a trusted geometry provider linked into it, compares deterministic reports and callback counts with an independent fixed oracle and standalone Zig implementation, and releases snapshot and callback graphs on allocation failure.

- [NOMINAL-BODY-1] Generic bodies retain nominal declaration identity and ordered symbolic arguments, check fields and payloads before calls, and preserve construction, assignment, exhaustive matches, named result propagation, partial ownership and cleanup effects. Substitution and late VM catalog materialization retain stable type IDs and reuse existing numeric array shapes. Phantom arguments retain identity without contributing stored-value contracts. Shape traversal uses an explicit stack and the generic checking work budget; argument-growing runtime specialization remains bounded by the nominal graph/name limits. Ordinary unconstrained type parameters still require structural constraints for member operations. Provider-specific symbolic shapes follow NOMINAL-PROVIDER-1; public generic schema tooling remains pending.

- [NOMINAL-PROVIDER-1] Generic resource/environment operations accept symbolic nominal result and payload records only when their exact named fields satisfy the ordinary provider contract for every permitted substitution. Declaration order does not affect meaning. Byte inputs and output arrays must have known u8 elements and numeric lengths; a general integer parameter is insufficient. Concrete and symbolic checking share named contract definitions. Resource identity, authority, mutable access, handling, cleanup and effect checks remain enforced before calls. VM specialization preserves bounded bytes, absent environment values, partial I/O, failed-completion ownership and cleanup under allocation failure or instruction traps.

- [GENERIC-CODEC-1] Generic domain codec metadata preserves exact value types, result obligations, transitive effects, declaration privacy and module relocation for tooling. The former `codec_encode`/`codec_decode` source entry points are removed; `wrap`/`unwrap` still require the exact declaring domain and private methods remain inaccessible outside it.

- [BOARD-ABSTRACTION-1] The board trial uses a shared Slot<T:copy> for optional IDs, regions, features and first violations, and one equality-constrained membership helper for component/net IDs. Typed snapshots preserve each instantiated slot's exact payload identity; a NetId payload cannot enter a component slot. Consolidation preserves the fixed board contract, oracle results, callback counts and repair fixture failure/restoration behavior.

- [GENERIC-BODY-3] A generic body, and every non-generic function it calls, may sort a concrete list in place with `values.sort()` when the element is an integer, a float or `str`, the same set the concrete checker accepts: the abstract checker types the call as unit and records it as a write to its receiver, as it does `append`, but seeds no allocation, because a sort allocates nothing managed. A list whose element is a type parameter or any other type still rejects with `E_COLLECTION_METHOD`, and every other unsupported collection operation keeps `E_GENERIC_BODY_UNSUPPORTED`.
- [GENERIC-BODY-4] A generic body may sort any list in place with `values.sort(key=key, reverse=flag)`, on a named receiver or any expression: the abstract checker checks the operands in source order, `reverse` as a `bool`, records a write to the receiver and seeds `managed_allocation` for the key list, and leaves the key to each specialization, which checks it against its concrete element type under LIST-SORT-KEY-2. Without `key=` the element still needs a native order, as for GENERIC-BODY-3, and a positional or unknown operand is `E_CALL_ARGUMENT`. A generic body may also call the prelude `sorted(values, key=..., reverse=...)` when no binding or declaration claims the name, typed as its list argument, with the same operand rules and `managed_allocation`, which is how `sort.sort_by_key` forwards; and it may take `values.copy()` of a list, typed as the list and seeding `managed_allocation`.
- [GENERIC-LAMBDA-1] A lambda written inside a generic function body is `E_GENERIC_BODY_UNSUPPORTED` at the lambda, reported once, with a message that names the fix (declare a top-level function and pass it by name): a lambda is one synthesized function with one callback type, so it cannot follow the template's type parameters into each specialization. This holds for a sort key (`sorted(values, key=lambda v: ...)`, `values.sort(key=...)`) and for any other callback argument, including one whose type mentions no type parameter; it replaces the earlier pair of an unknown-name error and a missing-callback-context error. A named function key in a generic body still sorts under GENERIC-BODY-4.
- [LAMBDA-NAME-1] A lambda of a project module is named `lambda@<module>::<offset>`, the offset in its own module's source, so its symbol is the same whatever else the program merges around it — the whole program or one module compiled on its own — and it is batched with its module's other lambdas; a single-file program's lambda keeps `lambda@<offset>`.

## Interpreted schematic pilot

- [SCHEMATIC-1] The guest S-expression parser, circuit model, interface expansion and SVG renderer execute in the bytecode VM. A typed scalar host boundary exposes only bounded UTF-8 source bytes and unpublished output. Invalid inputs, unsupported forms, incomplete or duplicate pads and invalid ports reject without publishing partial SVG. The pilot supports one parameterless module with one 41-pad IC and supported two-terminal primitives; its documented limits and metadata exclusions are not a general Netlisp evaluator or electrical sign-off.


- [NAVIGATION-1] Compiler navigation indexes module-qualified declaration identities, original source ranges, signatures, effects, concrete call/read/write/field/type relationships and bounded source excerpts without VM execution. Every result carries compiler and loaded-source identities. Static relationships do not establish runtime coverage. Failed body checking emits declarations without checked relationships; lexical recovery after body syntax errors emits explicitly unchecked candidates.
- [GENERIC-API-1] Checked public schemas expose generic templates separately from concrete callable functions and data types, preserving parameter requirements, symbolic signatures, fields/cases, module import scope, effect contracts and source identity. Templates require explicit specialization and cannot be invoked as concrete values through the JSON API. Exported constraint declarations are published as non-callable `constraint` templates, and an exported generic parameter exposes each named bound's spelling and declaring module together with the expanded builtin/field/operation promises it contributes, under the bumped `generic-templates-v2` version.
- [GENERIC-API-DEFAULT-COPY-1] Public generic schemas retain the implicit `copy` requirement of unbounded function and nominal type parameters, while explicit `storable` or `codec` bounds do not acquire `copy`.
- [GENERIC-API-SUBJECT-1] Expanded named requirements in public schemas substitute only parsed references to the declaration's subject, recursively through nominal applications, arrays and callback signatures. Qualified concrete names with the subject's spelling retain their identity in both the combined contract and the per-bound expansion.
- [STRUCTURAL-FIELD-1] Copy-constrained function parameters may require named fields with exact named types, including another declared type parameter. Concrete satisfaction and symbolic forwarding preserve nominal identity and checked field reads/updates. Structural fields do not expose opaque domain backing or permit incomplete construction of an unknown record.
- [STRUCTURAL-OP-1] Function parameters may require public nongeneric domain operations with explicit parameter/result types and an effect ceiling. Type-owned generic calls check declared contracts before specialization; concrete operations must satisfy signature, visibility and effects.
- [STRUCTURAL-LIMIT-1] Structural requirements currently apply to function templates with copy guarantees and named type expressions. Nominal type declarations and unknown complete constructors reject explicitly.
- [INCREMENTAL-CAPACITY-1] The syntax cache retains at most 256 modules within its unchanged byte budget; edits can reuse exact unchanged module syntax while complete semantic checking and diagnostic equivalence remain required.


### Disk application raw byte host

- [BYTE-IO-1] The raw byte host preserves arbitrary input, enforces bounds and publishes output only on successful guest exit. Its operations are a trusted native provider compiled into the guest's own executable, which buffers output where only the driver can publish it.

The `mylang-io --project DIRECTORY` host compiles `main.my` and its imports from
disk into bytecode. It supplies only `io::length() -> i64`,
`io::byte(index: i64) -> u8`, and `io::put(value: u8) -> i64`, each with host effects.
Input bytes are passed untouched, including invalid UTF-8 and NUL. The guest owns
parsing, validation, algorithms, and serialization. Input is bounded at 2 MiB;
output at 128 MiB; a put returns zero on success. Negative/out-of-range reads and
output exhaustion fail the host call. Output is published only after guest main
returns zero, never after traps or nonzero exits. The host supplies no external
filesystem, network, environment or process authority to the guest. Host calls/work
are bounded at 300 million each and guest execution at 2 billion steps; the coordinator
also enforces wall/CPU/memory limits. The executable is built outside application
sessions; editing guest files requires no Zig rebuild or language Guardian run.

- [STANDARD-MODULES-1] Reserved standard sources load identically through all project providers and participate in source budgets, dependency graphs and digests. They are ordinary Python-syntax guest modules over managed types and may import one another.
- [STANDARD-BYTE-BUILDER-1] The standard `byte_builder` module assembles an immutable `bytes` snapshot over one mutable `byte_buffer` ([PY-BYTES-6]), so `append`, `append_range`, `append_byte`, `append_spaces`, `append_text`, `append_int` and `append_float` write each fragment's octets straight into that buffer's capacity and no fragment allocates a value for itself, with `append_float` writing the canonical spelling `str(value)` prints. The checked appends reject before writing anything, so a refused call leaves the builder byte-for-byte unchanged: `append_range` rejects a negative `start`, an `end` below `start` or an `end` past `len(data)` and accepts an empty range that changes nothing, `append_byte` rejects values outside `0..255`, and `append_spaces` and `reserve` reject negative counts. `reserve` grows capacity for a further `extra` octets without changing the finished length, and `finish` publishes the buffer as an immutable snapshot in append order without copying its octets ([PY-BYTES-SNAPSHOT-1]): it contributes `managed_allocation` but no mutation, the builder stays usable, and later appends, including an append of a snapshot back into its own builder, cannot change an earlier result. Every operation keeps the module's published names, argument types, effects and results, carries `mutation` and `managed_allocation` where it writes, mutates only its builder argument, and leaves the buffer traced, so a builder survives collection at every managed allocation.

- [PLAIN-JSON-1] The strict document parser produces a checked i64 tape for a JSON text. It rejects duplicate decoded keys, invalid Unicode, integers outside i64, nonfinite numbers, trailing data and container depth over 64. Its byte budget is at most 1 MiB; scratch and output allocation remain bounded.
- [PLAIN-JSON-2] Ordinary JSON validation applies to every subtree, including unused fields: decoded duplicate keys, malformed UTF-8, out-of-range integers, nonfinite floats, trailing bytes and excessive nesting reject.
- [PLAIN-JSON-3] Failed document parsing releases partially allocated DOM, tape and text storage.
- [STANDARD-MODULES-2] Embedded standard source bytes count against the same per-file and total project source budgets as user modules.

## Guest application test tool

- [NATIVE-TEST-13] Successful guest tests with deterministic allocation or mutation confined to their fresh process and keyed inputs may reuse result receipts. Both checked-facts and emitted-code dependency metadata use the same classification. A test process holds no host-granted capability and registers no host provider, so its filesystem, network, clock, randomness and environment effects act only on deterministic stand-ins built from keyed inputs (TEST-CAPS-3) and do not prevent reuse, and neither does output (NATIVE-TEST-OUTPUT-1); a `host` effect or a host call still does. A declared mutation footprint still excludes signature-pure outcome replay.

- [NATIVE-TEST-12] Native guest-test cache identity ignores inherited environment variables the compiler/build does not read and source files outside the resolved import closure, while retaining compiler-affecting environment inputs, disabling durable reuse under dynamic-loader overrides or host calls, and invalidating on transitive dependency, project configuration/policy, provider, tool, or resolution-affecting candidate changes. A run still materializes a broad immutable source snapshot, but successful receipt publication verifies the same resolved identity so an unrelated neighboring edit does not fail with SourceChanged.

- [NATIVE-RUN-MANIFEST-CACHE-1] After a project graph has been loaded, policy-validated, and source-checked, run-project may reuse a checksum-valid emitted native manifest keyed by the ordered resolved source graph, compiler identity, policy identity, and emission controls; a warm hit performs zero lowering/emission, while --no-cache, changed inputs, or corrupt entries force fresh emission.

- [NATIVE-TEST-1] `mylang test` checks the project and discovers `test_`-prefixed declarations: every exported one, and an unexported one only when it already has the defaulting shape — no generic parameters, no value parameters, and a `bool` result — so a private helper named `test_` never aborts a run. A discovered test with data parameters and no cases-file `arguments` is a property test (PROPERTY-TEST-1); one whose shape cannot default (a non-`bool` result without `expected`, or a property test given `expected`) is reported as one failed case rather than a host failure. It validates named typed JSON inputs and compares native results structurally. Filters and reasoned skips never turn an empty or all-skipped selection into success. Each execution uses a fresh bounded native process without implicit host grants.
- [NATIVE-TEST-2] Successful deterministic tests may reuse verified receipts keyed by semantic dependency closure, call signature, relevant layouts, case settings, execution limits, and tool/runtime identity. That identity follows QBE-4: the compiler's embedded build-time digest, and path, size and modification time for external tools, rather than rereading executables on every run. Whitespace and unrelated body edits preserve valid results; affected helper edits invalidate consumers. Corrupt receipts are misses; failed executions and executions that call host-provided code are not reused (NATIVE-TEST-13). `--force` executes selected unskipped tests.
- [NATIVE-TEST-OUTPUT-1] Output is a deterministic effect for result reuse, like process-local mutation and allocation: a test that takes `out: Stream` or `err: Stream` and has no other external effect reuses its receipt under the same identity as any other test (semantic dependency closure, call signature, layouts, case settings, execution limits, collector stress and tool/runtime identity). The runner captures both streams of a test process and discards them when the test passes, and a receipt records only a pass, so a reused pass shows exactly what an executed pass shows; a failure, whose captured output is reported with it, always executes again. A test that also reads the clock, entropy, the environment, a file or the network is cacheable too, because what it reads is a deterministic stand-in (TEST-CAPS-3); only a host call keeps a test uncacheable.
- [NATIVE-TEST-3] Provider fixtures replace only existing dependency sources in private bounded snapshots, preserve the selected test signature and exported types, and never edit originals. A source change observed during one invocation and any compiler change invalidate results before publication, but an unrelated source edit no longer invalidates an unaffected receipt: acceptance is bound to the selected test/provider dependency closure, contract identity and provider identity, with the resolved-source identity retained for during-invocation change detection and diagnostics; provider replacement additionally verifies the full captured inventory. Native reports distinguish passed, cached, failed, and skipped cases; setup failures exit 2 and empty/all-skipped selections exit 3.
- [NATIVE-TEST-4] A directory argument selects every project root beneath it in sorted order — a `.my` file that declares a top-level `main`, plus standalone `*_test.my` files — treats library modules imported by a root as part of that project rather than programs of their own (a library with tests that no root imports is selected too, NATIVE-TEST-14), skips generated trees, and runs one bounded child command per root across the machine's cores. Child reports merge into one case listing, one count summary and the most severe exit status, and children share the object cache and receipt directory so an unchanged program reuses its results instead of rebuilding. A filter carrying no `*`, `?` or `[` matches any substring of a qualified test name; one carrying them keeps shell-style wildcard semantics. A per-case configuration file is rejected for a directory run, because its keys name the tests of one project.
- [NATIVE-TEST-5] Before checking any production implementation body, native test preparation parses every reachable module and validates exported declarations and interfaces, then reads the case configuration and fully checks only the selected replacement provider graph. The declaration manifest carries canonical declaration-level type descriptors that normalize builtin aliases and expand transparent aliases while preserving nominal identities, and discovery and provider comparison consume those descriptors without checking unselected production bodies. Replacements are validated against every public contract they replace — names, defaults, canonical types and nominal identities, data shapes, generic requirements, declared effects, mutation footprints and return-origin bounds — using the directional subset relation, so a narrower implementation is accepted and a stronger one rejects. An unselected production body may contain a typed hole or body type error while its declarations remain valid; a production interface error, a malformed or unknown replacement, or a syntax error still rejects, and a full production build still rejects the unfinished body. Test receipts record the selected dependency-closure key plus the contract and provider identities, so changing a fake value or a relevant contract re-executes its case. Identical provider graphs share one declaration validation, checked project and prepared native library per invocation; distinct provider graphs stay isolated.
- [NATIVE-TEST-6] After a project validity check through the shared query cache, a warm unchanged deterministic native-test run looks up valid result receipts and executes zero tests without lowering, emitting or linking any code; only the validity check runs, with the declaration check when its contracts are not already cached (NATIVE-TEST-INTERFACES-1). Cached execution is admitted only with the closure-bound plan key, contract identity, provider identity and fixture/environment/budget identities, so a helper edit executes only its dependent tests and changed expectations, arguments, providers, environment, budgets or force mode invalidate exactly the affected receipts. A fully cached selection additionally requires durable checked facts (`native-test-graph-v3`) whose key covers compiler/tool stamps, mode, environment hash, source identity and canonical provider identity; a wrong or damaged graph is a miss. When facts or receipts miss, one checked compilation per selected provider graph produces the test facts and is retained: a later selective lowering request consumes that same checked result instead of re-checking the project in a second process, and any fallback recheck is reported as real checked-body work rather than hidden. Only the selected tests' reachable function closure is lowered, emitted and linked; callbacks, function values, codec boundaries and any indirect-call set widen conservatively rather than omitting required functions. Identical provider graphs share one prepared closure and library, while distinct graphs stay isolated.
- [PURE-SKIP-1] `mylang test --pure-skip` may replay a recorded pass-or-fail outcome for an unchanged test that is both effect-cacheable and signature-pure (no capability parameter and no declared `mutates(...)` footprint) without executing it and without depending on the positive-only receipt. The record is keyed by the same semantic dependency-closure key, contract identity and provider identity as the receipt, and additionally binds the expected value; a recorded failure replays as a failure with its message and never as a pass. A changed dependency, provider, contract or flag is a miss and re-runs, and `--force`, `--no-cache` and any non-pure test never take the path. Without the flag the default result is unchanged.

- [NATIVE-TEST-7] `mylang test` runs each case with the guest's stdout redirected off the runner's binary protocol channel: the child captures bounded output and returns it in the response, the parent appends it to a failure's text, a passing case discards it, and a benchmarked function's output goes to the diagnostic stream. `print` inside a guest test therefore never aborts a run with `InvalidNativeTestResponse`.

- [NATIVE-TEST-8] A failed typed expectation reports both sides of the comparison as `expected <value>, got <value>` — a Boolean test shows its actual and expected values — while a trap keeps its located code and any captured guest output.

- [NATIVE-TEST-9] A guest test entry need not declare `main`: a library module or a standalone `*_test.my` file is checked through a private projection that appends a stub entry point, and a matching `module` header when the file has none, while the `main` requirement for ordinary `run` is unchanged. A standalone `*_test.my` beneath a subdirectory resolves its imports and dotted module name from the enclosing project root — the nearest ancestor holding `mylang.json`, else the nearest ancestor holding a non-test `.my` source — so `tests/parser_test.my` is module `tests.parser_test` and `import sexpr.parser` reads from the project root. A child compiler's located source diagnostic is surfaced alone with exit status 1 rather than wrapped in the generic host-failure line.
- [NATIVE-TEST-HEADERLESS-1] `mylang test` accepts every file that `check`, `run` and `build` accept: a headerless entry — with or without a `main` — is tested through a projection whose `module NAME;` header is written in front of the file's first line rather than on a line of its own, so every diagnostic, trap location and test failure reports the line number of the user's file. A headered entry with a `main` is tested unchanged.

- [NATIVE-TEST-10] A failed `assert` reports the message written after its condition: the per-function native metadata carries one message per program counter, `mylang test` renders it as `R_ASSERT: <message>` in the failure text beside the located code and any captured output, and `mylang run` shows the same message on its human diagnostic; an assertion with no message keeps the bare `R_ASSERT`, and a message containing JSON syntax leaves the versioned trap record valid.
- [NATIVE-TEST-11] A directory run's merged summary counts the programs whose child produced no usable report — a build or other host failure — and names each one on stderr as `not run: <program> (exit <status>, no report)`. The count also travels in the JSON report as `dropped`, and a non-zero count keeps the existing setup-failure exit status, so a totals-only reading of `N passed, 0 failed` can never call a run green while whole programs are missing from it.
- [NATIVE-TEST-14] A directory run also selects a `.my` library that declares a test — a `def test_` function, public or not, or an `@test` function — when no selected root's import closure reaches it, and checks it through the stub-`main` projection of NATIVE-TEST-9. Reachability is the compiler's own module-header graph from each root's project root, so a library a root imports keeps running once, as part of that root; of two such libraries where one imports the other, only the importer is selected. A test file therefore needs neither `main` nor the `*_test.my` suffix to run.
- [NATIVE-TEST-15] Every child report lists, as `modules`, the absolute paths of the project sources its check loaded. After merging, a directory run names each test-declaring source beneath the directory that no reporting child loaded on stderr as `not run: <path> (declares tests, but no program loaded it)`, counts them as `unloaded` in the JSON report (a dropped program is already named by NATIVE-TEST-11 and is not counted again) and as `N test sources not loaded` on the count line, and exits with the setup-failure status 2, so a test file can never drop out of a run silently.
- [AFFECTED-TEST-1] `mylang test --affected --against REF` derives its selection from the working-tree diff rather than from the standing tree: it maps the changed lines `git` reports for `REF` onto the declarations they touch ([AFFECTED-TEST-2]) and runs a discovered test only when a changed declaration is in the dependency closure the run already derives for the test's receipt. It is a selection and never a verdict: the selected count and every skipped test are printed, `--filter` still applies first, `lint --gate` and CI run the whole set, and an empty derivation exits 3 exactly as a filter matching nothing does. `--against` without `--affected` (and `--affected` without `--against`) is a usage error, and a `REF` `git` cannot resolve is a host failure (status 2) rather than an empty selection. A project that cannot be checked fails exactly as it does without `--affected`, and a declared skip, an undefaultable case or a test the checked graph cannot name is kept rather than silently skipped.
- [AFFECTED-TEST-2] The `--affected` derivation compiles nothing of its own: one `git diff --unified=0 REF` under the entry's directory plus `git ls-files --others` name every changed or untracked `.my` file, and each changed line maps by indentation onto the top-level declaration that owns it (a column-0 header up to the next column-0 line, an attribute opening its declaration's block). A line owned by a function or constant changes `module::name` and its generic specializations; a line anywhere else (module header, import, record, domain) changes every declaration of the module; a pure deletion changes the lines on both sides of the gap; an untracked file changes on every line. The selection is then one reverse walk of the checked graph's dependency edges from the changed functions.
- [GUEST-TEST-GC-STRESS-1] `mylang test --gc-stress` selects the tracing collector (`RC-DEFAULT-1`) and runs every collected test's copied-value call with the runtime's stress collector, the same collector coverage `native_execute` gets from its own `--gc-stress` argument, and a directory run forwards the flag to every child so one invocation reports one collector condition. The flag adds no expected value of its own, is part of the execution identity a receipt binds, and without it the collector is not stressed and the default result is unchanged.

- [NATIVE-TEST-PARALLEL-1] Selected test cases execute concurrently: `--jobs N`, then `MYLANG_JOBS`, then the cores the run may use capped by how many per-test memory ceilings free memory holds, sets how many worker threads each start one fresh bounded runner process at a time without copying the parent (`clone(CLONE_VM | CLONE_VFORK)`), and each child answers into its own response file (`--response FILE`). Every outcome — a value, a trap, `Timeout` for a child still running at `--timeout-ms`, or an abnormal exit with its stderr — lands in its case's slot, so a report never depends on completion order. A directory run gives each child an equal share of its worker width.
- [NATIVE-TEST-RUNNER-1] The compiled POSIX test harness is published once under `<cache>/runner/<key>`, keyed by every embedded harness source byte, its compile flags and the `zig` tool stamp, and later runs reuse it instead of compiling it again; a `--no-cache` run compiles a private copy.
- [NATIVE-TEST-STABLE-1] A run materializes its source snapshot at a path that is the same on every run of one project from one cache — `$TMPDIR/.pending/snapshot-<key>`, claimed through the scratch owner lock and cleared of whatever a killed run left — so the compiler's path-keyed caches (the check verdict, parsed modules, emitted-fragment packs) answer across runs; a run that cannot claim it stages at a fresh private path as before, and every run removes its snapshot on exit. The `native-test-compile` session records the `check-project` verdict of the project it checked, unless a configured `warnings: "deny"` rejects that project, so the next unchanged run's validity check replays it.
- [NATIVE-TEST-INTERFACES-1] The production declaration check (`list-interfaces --contracts`) is answered from `<cache>/interfaces/` when the same resolved source identity, configuration, compiler and compiler-affecting environment produced a clean answer before: exit status 0 and nothing on stderr but `--report` records. Each test entry keeps one slot, the latest clean answer behind the identity it answers for, so the store does not grow with edits. A warning, a refusal, `--force` and `--no-cache` always run the check, and a damaged entry is a miss.
- [NATIVE-TEST-OVERLAP-1] When no case configuration names a provider fixture, a run begins the production graph's preparation before the declaration check — loading its stored checked facts, or starting the `native-test-compile` session that checks it — so the body check and the declaration pass run side by side. A declaration failure, or a selection that never uses the graph, kills the session without forwarding its output, so the reported failure and exit status are exactly those of the ordered preparation.
- [NATIVE-TEST-PLAN-2] Planning reads each graph into an index once — symbol positions, resolved dependency edges and facts — so planning a selected test costs its own closure, and a module's contract identity is computed once for all of its tests; a malformed unit fails only the plans that reach it. The plan key is BLAKE3 in the `native-test-plan-v2` domain, so receipts written under the SHA-256 key miss once, and a published receipt leaves out the closure's symbol list, which its key already binds.
- [NATIVE-TEST-IDENTITY-1] The resolved source identity is computed through an import memo under `<cache>/imports/`, keyed by each module's content (BLAKE3) and whether it is the entry, and holding exactly the import names the project loader records for those bytes; a module the memo lacks is loaded alone to read its header, and a module or standard import the walk cannot resolve falls back to the full load. The memoized identity equals the full-load identity, and a damaged memo is ignored. End-of-run verification re-reads the captured tree and accepts it byte-for-byte without resolving imports again; only a changed tree is resolved again, so an unrelated neighbouring edit still never fails the run.
- [NATIVE-TEST-MEMO-MEMORY-1] The one-module load that reads a header the import memo has not seen runs on an allocator that frees (the build context's parent), not on the build context's arena, so a cold `mylang test` keeps no per-module 1 MiB header scratch or project arena: a generated million-line project's 2,401 header loads had grown the context arena past the 8 GiB ceiling (`host failure: OutOfMemory` within half a second).

- [TEST-CAPS-1] Deterministic stand-in capabilities are built by effect-free standard functions that need no capability: `time.simulated_clock(start: Instant) -> Clock` reads `start` until `time.advance(clock: Clock, duration: Duration) -> Result[None, str]` moves it (copies share one reading; a host-granted clock is `Err("only a simulated clock can be advanced")`; leaving the signed nanosecond range is `Err` and leaves the reading unchanged); `random.seeded_entropy(seed: int) -> Entropy` yields the SplitMix64 stream the host's `--allow-random=SEED` uses, so `random.from_entropy` on equal seeds is reproducible and each read advances the stream; `filesystem.memory() -> Filesystem` is an empty in-memory tree that serves every whole-path helper, `open` in `r`/`w`/`a` modes with the handle methods, `exists`, `mkdir`, `remove` (a file or an empty directory) and name-sorted `listdir`, with the scoped-root path rules and the host surface's error texts; `environment.fixed(values: dict[str, str]) -> Environment` answers `get`, `names` and `only` from exactly those pairs (a name that is empty, longer than 128 bytes or holds `=` traps `R_ENVIRONMENT`); and `network.denied() -> Network` refuses every acquisition, and every `net.host(...)` narrowing of it, with the `Connected.err` code `network.denied_here()` (6), which `network.describe(code)` spells `network access is denied in this context: this Network is a deterministic stand-in`. `mylang test` accepts a program that imports `network`: the declaration contracts render its `resource Connection: network` family as written instead of resolving it as a type.
- [TEST-CAPS-2] A stand-in never reaches a host resource. The native runtime keeps each one in a registry owned by the execution context: a clock, entropy or environment stand-in is a word in a range no host-granted authority uses, and a filesystem or network stand-in carries its registry index inside its resource authority, so `fs.dir(...)`, `read_only()`, `env.only(...)` and `net.host(...)` derive stand-ins that stay stand-ins, and every read, write, acquisition and listing through them is answered by the registry without consulting a host provider, the process environment or the disk. A narrowed stand-in environment traps `R_CAPABILITY` on a name outside its scope, as a narrowed host one does. The `__stand_in[...]` and `__clock_advance(...)` intrinsics behind these functions are reserved to the standard library and rejected in ordinary source with `E_INTERNAL_NAME`.
- [TEST-CAPS-3] A `test_` function may declare `Clock`, `Entropy`, `Filesystem`, `Environment` and `Network` parameters beside `out: Stream`/`err: Stream`; none needs a cases-file argument. The runner hands each one a stand-in: a simulated clock at 2000-01-01T00:00:00Z (946684800000000000 ns), entropy seeded with the first eight bytes (little-endian) of the SHA-256 of the test's qualified name unless `mylang test --seed N` names one (a directory run forwards it), an empty in-memory filesystem, an empty fixed environment and a denied network. A `Host` parameter is never supplied, so no test is handed real authority: without cases-file arguments the test is a property test whose `Host` parameter cannot be generated, and it fails naming that parameter (PROPERTY-TEST-1). The seed and the start instant are part of a receipt's identity, so an unchanged test reuses its pass while a different seed executes again.
- [TEST-CAPS-4] Using a stand-in where no parameter supplies the effect is not an effect of the function: real authority reaches a body only through its parameters, so a clock, entropy, filesystem, network or environment capability a function holds without a parameter that permits that effect can only be a stand-in, and those effects are contained — they neither appear in the function's inferred effects nor propagate to callers nor exceed its signature-derived ceiling. A function with a `Host` parameter, a `host def` and an `extern def` contain nothing, because they can reach the host by other means; an explicit `effects(...)`, `noalloc` or `noio` clause still bounds what is not contained, and the bytecode validator recomputes the same containment.
- [PROPERTY-TEST-1] A discovered `test_` function with data parameters and no cases-file `arguments` is a property test: `mylang test` generates an input for every data parameter from its checked type, supplies the deterministic stand-ins for its capability parameters as for any test, and passes when every one of `--property-runs N` inputs (default 100, at most 1,000,000) returns `True` or `Ok(True)` without trapping; `False`, `Ok(False)`, `Err(...)` and any trap other than a discard fail it. Inputs are a pure function of the seed — the test's `Entropy` seed, the SHA-256 prefix of its qualified name unless `--seed N` names one — and the run number, drawn by a SplitMix64 stream per run; run sizes grow from 0 on the first run to 100 on the last (a collection holds at most the size at the top level and half of it per level down, and an input stops growing collections past 4096 nodes). Integers draw three in eight from the edges of their width (0, 1, -1, the minimum and maximum, a power of two, one below it and its negation), one in eight uniformly over the width and the rest near zero; `bool`, `float` (both zeros, both infinities, NaN, the extreme finite and subnormal magnitudes, and small binary fractions), `str` (seven scalars in ten printable ASCII, plus controls, well-known non-ASCII scalars and scalars from the whole range), `bytes`, `list[T]`, `dict[K, V]` and `set[T]` (a repeated key or element is dropped), tuples, fixed arrays, enum-indexed tables, `Option[T]`, `Result[T, E]`, records fieldwise and unions casewise all generate. A parameter whose type reaches a `class`, a `domain` (other than `float`), a function value, `Host` or a type the schema does not describe is a configuration error that fails the test naming the parameter, its type and the reason, and so is a property test whose result is not `bool` or `Result[bool, E]`. A cases-file entry with `arguments` still runs the test with exactly those arguments.
- [PROPERTY-TEST-2] A property test's preconditions filter its inputs: `R_REQUIRES` with the test itself as the innermost frame discards the input, while a precondition broken inside a function it calls fails the test. Copied inputs also validate record and domain invariants at the native boundary (`CONTRACT-DECODE-1`); `R_INVARIANT` from a compiler-generated boundary callback discards a generated input. A test retains an entry prologue that checks invariants in parameters, nested fields, collection elements and keys, arrays, tuples and union cases before its own preconditions; a valid guest-constructed value passes it. Discarded inputs are replaced on the same size schedule; after more than ten times the requested runs are discarded, the test fails with `too many discarded inputs` and the most frequent rejecting clause. An explicit cases-file input that breaks an invariant fails with `R_INVARIANT`.
- [PROPERTY-TEST-3] The first failing input is shrunk greedily: each parameter in turn is replaced by the first of its simpler candidates, tried in order, that still fails (for any reason other than a discard), until a pass over every parameter changes nothing or 2,000 candidates have run. Integers shrink toward 0 (0, then the positive of a negative, then halving the distance: 100 offers 0, 50, 75, 88, 94, 97, 99); floats toward `0.0` by their whole part, halved exponents and the integer series; strings, bytes, lists, sets and dictionaries by dropping every element, then halves, quarters and single elements, then (for integer lists) moving an element's value into its neighbour, then every slot shrunk together and one at a time breadth first — never into a set element or dictionary key another slot holds; records and tuples fieldwise; enums and unions toward earlier cases at their simplest; `Option` toward `None`. The failure reports `falsified after N passing runs (seed S)`, the minimal input as one mylang literal per parameter (`xs = [100]`, `Point(x=49, y=1i32)`, `Some("aaaa")`, `0.0 / 0.0`), the original input with its run number, the reason (`returned False`, `returned Err(...)` followed by that input's error return trace (ERROR-TRACE-3), or a trap's code, message and frames), the original input's own reason when it differs, and `reproduce: mylang test ENTRY --filter 'NAME' --seed S`; an input that ran past `--timeout-ms` is reported unshrunk. `--json` adds a `property` object to every property test's result: `seed`, `counts` (`runs`, `passed`, `discarded`, `processes`, `shrinks`, `attempts`) and, for a falsified one, `failure` with the minimal and original inputs as typed JSON `arguments` and `literals`, the run, the reason and the reproduction.
- [PROPERTY-TEST-4] Property inputs run in batches: one native harness process makes one copied-value call per input and flushes each input's answer — its value or trap, captured output and error return trace — as it finishes, so a passing 100-run property costs one process launch; every property's first batch runs concurrently beside the others, and replacements for discards and shrink candidates run in further batches, several properties at a time. A child that dies or times out mid-batch keeps the answers of the inputs before the one it was running; that input is rerun alone in its own process (a failure if that process dies too), and the rest of the batch runs in a new one. A passing property's receipt identity adds its seed, its run count and the generator version to the test's, so an unchanged property reports `cached`, and changing `--seed`, `--property-runs` or the generator runs it again; a failure is never reused. A directory run forwards `--property-runs`.
- [PROPERTY-TEST-5] The copied-value codec boundary carries `float` as one scalar node of its IEEE bits (typed JSON: the shortest decimal with a `.`, or `inf`, `-inf`, `nan`), `bytes` as kind `bytes` with one `u8` node per octet, and a dictionary or a set of any hashable key as kind `dict` naming its key and value (a set's value is unit and carries no node): one node counting the entries, then each entry's key tree and, for a dictionary, its value tree, in iteration order (typed JSON: an array of `[key, value]` pairs, or of elements). A repeated key in a request is refused as `InvalidArgument`. The checked schema names a dictionary's or a set's `key`, and marks a `class` record with `class: true`.

## Guest performance benchmarks

- [NATIVE-BENCH-1] `mylang test --bench PATTERN` discovers exported `bench_` functions beside `test_` functions and reuses the same cases file for typed arguments, expectations, provider substitutions and named input sizes, each size becoming its own stable benchmark ID `name[size]`. Boolean results default to true and other result types require an explicit expectation, exactly as correctness tests do. Benchmark filtering is independent of test filtering; correctness tests run first and must pass before any measurement, and `--bench-only` skips them for local iteration. Malformed settings, unknown case keys and missing expectations reject instead of silently skipping a benchmark.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-2] Each benchmark invokes the complete native copied-value call, including argument and result codec costs, and measures it inside the native subprocess with a monotonic clock after warmup and calibration to a target batch duration. Compilation, process startup, library loading and report serialization are excluded from the declared timing. The harness reports raw batch durations and iteration counts, median nanoseconds per operation, interquartile spread and sample count, validates the returned value outside every timed region, and keeps the workload observable. Every operation is a fresh copied-value call, so no guest state carries between operations. A trap, an incorrect result, a timeout or an invalid output fails the run and is listed in the candidate's failures instead of producing a measurement.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-3] Three distinct versioned artifacts exist: the approved `perf/baseline.json` with measurements, inventory and provenance; `perf/policy.json` with default and per-benchmark limits, the required suite, runner profile and noise budget; and a fresh run report or candidate. Malformed data, unknown versions, duplicate benchmark IDs, invalid numbers and nonpositive timing baselines reject. A baseline is never replaced while it is being checked, candidate writes are atomic, and a candidate clearly states whether every required test and benchmark completed so an incomplete report cannot become an approved baseline. Each measurement carries its benchmark ID, workload version, fixture identity, measurement-boundary version, optimization mode, sampling configuration and runner profile, with source revision, compiler, toolchain, OS, CPU and environment recorded as provenance.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-4] Comparison is lower-is-better. A relative regression is flagged only when `(C - B) / B` exceeds `allowed_growth_percent / 100` and `C - B` exceeds `minimum_meaningful_delta_ns`; an independent `max_ns_per_op` ceiling applies even when relative growth is small; equality with a limit passes and zero percent tolerance is valid. Only runs whose workload version, fixture identity, boundary version, optimization mode, runner profile and sampling configuration match are compared, and the code-under-test implementation hash is deliberately not part of that key. Unbaselined, removed, skipped, incompatible and missing results produce explicit incomplete-coverage or incompatible verdicts rather than a green gate. A noisy or near-limit result earns at most one predefined confirmation run whose samples are evaluated together; unresolved noise is inconclusive and never silently passes.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-5] The approved baseline stays fixed until a reviewed promotion replaces it. Promotion requires a complete, passing candidate and records the approver, reason, revision, runner profile and old and new measurements in retained history, so successive accepted regressions stay visible and a successful build never advances the baseline by itself. A local comparison writes no baseline, a candidate cannot raise its own limits to pass, and the baseline and policy formats serve a later compiler benchmark adapter through distinct benchmark IDs and timing boundaries.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-6] A directory benchmark run discovers and measures each program's benchmarks with a fan-out width of one, so timing batches never compete with each other, and a failing correctness test in any child blocks that program's measurements. Because a directory run has no single aggregate benchmark report, it rejects `--bench-output`, `--bench-baseline` and `--bench-policy`; each child's most severe exit status carries its own performance gate.

## Guest memory ceilings

- [MEMORY-CEILING-1] Every process that runs guest code runs under a byte ceiling. The soft ceiling is the managed heap's own budget: an allocation that would exceed it after a collection traps with `R_MEMORY_BUDGET`, "memory budget exhausted", and exit status 1, rather than growing. The ceiling travels in `MYLANG_MEMORY_LIMIT` as bytes with an optional `K`, `M` or `G` suffix, so no spawner needs a new argument ABI and a program started by hand under an exported variable is bounded the same way; malformed text refuses the run instead of leaving it unbounded, and zero removes the ceiling. `--max-memory=BYTES` on `run`, `build --run` and `test` outranks the variable, which outranks the built-in default: 512 MiB for one test child, 4 GiB for `run` and `build --run`. A resolved test ceiling is part of a receipt key, so changing it re-executes rather than reuses.
- [BUDGET-HINT-1] A native budget trap reports the flag that raises it instead of a generic failure: `R_STEP_LIMIT` answers "instruction budget exhausted; raise it with --max-steps=N" and `R_FRAME_LIMIT` answers "call-depth budget exhausted; raise it with --max-frames=N", with or without a located guest trace, in text and in the message field of `--diagnostics=json`. Every other runtime code keeps the text it had, and `R_MEMORY_BUDGET` still answers "memory budget exhausted".

- [RUNTIME-UNBOUNDED-1] `mylang run`, `mylang build --run` and a binary built by `mylang build` count instructions and call depth without a default ceiling: a program is stopped only by `--max-steps=N`, `--max-frames=N`, the byte ceiling, or `--timeout=SECONDS`. An embedded library run with zeroed `MylangRunOptions` is unbounded the same way. `mylang test` keeps its per-test guard of 1,000,000 instructions and 1,024 frames.

- [RUNTIME-TIMEOUT-1] `--timeout=SECONDS` on `mylang run` and `mylang build --run`, or in a built binary's `MYLANG_OPTIONS` ([NATIVE-ARGV-1]), arms a monotonic wall-clock timer inside the guest; when it fires the process writes `R_TIMEOUT: wall-clock budget exhausted; raise it with --timeout=SECONDS` to standard error and exits 1. There is no default timeout, and a zero, empty or non-numeric value is a usage error.
- [MEMORY-CEILING-2] Behind the soft ceiling the runtime caps its own address space with `RLIMIT_AS` at the budget plus fixed headroom, before its first allocation, so a runtime defect cannot outgrow the budget. It never loosens a tighter ceiling already in force and installs none under a sanitizer, whose shadow mapping reserves more address space than any budget allows. The compiler bounds itself the same way at start-up, at 8 GiB unless `MYLANG_HOST_MEMORY_LIMIT` says otherwise, and the tools a native build spawns complete under the inherited ceiling. Every host that runs a guest in its own process — the byte runner, the board trial and the schematic renderer — installs that same ceiling before it runs anything, because there a runaway guest is a runaway host.
- [MEMORY-CEILING-3] A directory test run fans out over the cores the machine has, further limited by how many per-child ceilings its reported free memory holds, so a cold run on a loaded machine waits instead of oversubscribing. `--jobs N` outranks `MYLANG_JOBS`, which outranks that derived width; zero and non-numeric widths are rejected, the width never exceeds the programs there are to run, and it is never less than one.
- [ARENA-BOUNDED-1] A project's arena (every module's source, syntax and checked program) and the native emission's whole-program arena each hold no more untouched address space than one 64 MiB chunk: shared chunks grow by half up to that size, and an allocation of 4 MiB or more gets its own mapping from the child allocator, which a `remap` grows in place of a copy and a `free` returns. The compiler's address-space ceiling (`MYLANG_HOST_MEMORY_LIMIT`, 8 GiB) therefore bounds what the frontend touches, not a geometric reservation: `std.heap.ArenaAllocator`'s half-again node growth had a cold million-line check fail with 4.2 GB resident when its next node would have crossed the ceiling. A module's tokens, which nothing reads after its parse, are lexed from the child allocator and freed at once rather than kept in the project arena. The arena's state is shared by copies of its owner and is single-threaded.
- [PARSE-EXACT-1] A project module's node and statement lists grow in the project arena's freeing parent while it is parsed and are copied into the arena at their exact length when the parse ends, so the arena keeps no abandoned growth buffers (three times the final lists before; the cold 500k-line frontend's parse went from 1.08 GB to 0.44 GB).
- [LOWER-EXACT-1] Lowering builds each function's work stack, labels, fixups, loop table and instruction list in a scratch arena drawn from a freeing allocator and reset after the function, and copies only the finished instructions into the output; bytecode validation's tables live in the same scratch. The output holds the lowered program and nothing else, and the lowered program is unchanged.
- [SYNTAX-RELEASE-1] A whole-program `emit-project-qbe` (the build frontend) returns the merged syntax's node and statement tables to the project arena once the program is checked, because lowering and emission read only the typed program and bytecode; the tables have mappings of their own, so their memory goes back to the system. An invalidation explanation, a selective test emission and every other command keep them. The emitted program is byte-identical.
- [MANIFEST-STREAM-1] Remembering an emission for replay writes its record piece by piece around the manifest, hashing as it goes, instead of assembling header, warnings, manifest and unit list in one buffer, so the build frontend holds its manifest once rather than twice.

See `docs/experiments/guest-tests.md` and `tests/native_test_cli.py`.

## Installed feature identity

- [FEATURE-REPORT-1] `mylang --features` reports `language-features-v1`, the compiler
identity, pinned Zig version and embedded feature/restriction manifest, without reading
its own executable. `compiler_sha256` is that identity: a build-time SHA-256 over the
compiler's source inputs, Zig version and optimize mode, embedded when the compiler is
built. The same identity appears in every receipt's `implementation`, so a receipt and a
feature report from one compiler always agree. The current reference is
`docs/current-language.md`; historical checkpoint prose does not supersede it. The
manifest records implementation coverage, not a test certificate.
- [CLI-HELP-1] `mylang --help`, `-h` and `help` print to stdout and exit 0: first a pointer to `mylang docs primer`, then the everyday commands (`check`, `run`, `build` and `build --release`, `test`, `fmt`, `fix`, `docs`, `reference --query`), then a "Working in a large project" block naming the navigation commands that exist (`context --symbol`, `context --at`, `tests-for`, `list-interfaces`, `test --affected --against`), then the full command reference. Any other malformed invocation still prints the reference to stderr and exits 2.
