- [CLI-DOCS-1] `mylang docs` answers from the user guides embedded in the executable, so a project outside the compiler repository can read them: with no argument it lists every topic with a one-line summary, `primer` first, and `mylang docs TOPIC` prints that guide's Markdown to stdout and exits 0 from any working directory; `--help` prints its usage, and an unknown topic or a second argument exits 2 naming the topics. The topics are the `docs/` pages one table in `src/user_docs.zig` names (`primer`, `syntax`, `statements`, `records`, `functions`, `errors`, `text`, `bytes`, `io`, `effects`, `modules`, `memory`, `arenas`, `stdlib`, `tests`, `build`, `navigation`, `context`, `api`); the build embeds each one, so an edited page ships with the next compiler and a moved one fails the build.
- [FEATURE-REPORT-2] The manifest's `implemented` section is a JSON object keyed by feature
slug. Every entry carries `kind`, either `language` or `tooling`, a one-line `summary`, and a
`doc` pointer of the form `<page>.md#<heading-anchor>` naming the documentation section that
demonstrates the feature. A slug that stops describing a real form moves to `removed` with a
date and a note rather than disappearing.
- [DOCCOV-1] `mylang doc-coverage [<repo-root>]` resolves every implemented feature's `doc`
pointer against that checkout. A pointer without an anchor, a page that does not exist, a page
reached through a `..` component, a page under `docs/legacy/` whose examples the runner never
executes, and an anchor matching no heading are all reported failures.
A heading's anchor is its text lowercased with punctuation dropped, spaces turned into hyphens,
and hyphens and underscores kept; its section runs to the next heading of the same or higher
level, and headings inside fenced blocks are not headings. A `language` section must contain a
fence tagged `mylang` or `my` that the documentation runner executes — one tagged
`# doc: skip` or `# doc: not-mylang` does not count. A `tooling` section must contain a fence
tagged `bash`, `sh`, `console` or `text`, or an executable program fence.
- [DOCCOV-2] Every `export` of every `src/stdlib/*.my` module must be named as a whole word in
that module's `## <module>` section of `docs/standard-library.md`, every such section must
carry an executed example, and every exported definition must have a `#` comment line directly
above it in the module source.
- [DOCCOV-3] The command prints per-page feature counts, the standard-module and export totals,
and one line per failure, then a failure count. It exits 0 only when nothing failed, 1 when
anything is undocumented, and 2 for an unusable command line. A missing or malformed
`features.json`, an unreadable page and an unlistable `src/stdlib` are reported as failures
rather than host errors. `zig build test-doc-coverage` runs it, and the default `test` step
depends on it.
- [DOCTEST-1] `mylang doctest [<repo-root>] [--filter GLOB] [--report PATH] [--verbose]` walks
`README.md`, `SPEC.md` and every `*.md` under `docs/` recursively, except `docs/legacy/`,
extracts fenced `mylang`/`my` blocks, and applies the leading `# doc:` directives (`expect`,
`expect-error`, `check`, `project`, `file`, `skip`, `not-mylang`, `allow-read`, `allow-write`).
Blocks sharing a `# doc: project <name>` are materialised as one multi-file project under a
private temporary directory; every unit is then checked, run, check-project-ed or run-project-ed
by the running compiler with `--diagnostics=json` and the same `--print-result` and
`--restrict` capability-grant options the documentation gate used before. `expect` compares
trimmed standard output, `expect-error` compares `diagnostic-v1` codes, and the command reports
per-document tallies, mis-tagged prose fences and failures. `--report PATH` writes a
`doc-examples-v1` JSON report, and the command exits 0 only when nothing failed, nothing was
mis-tagged and at least one executable block was found.
- [DOCTEST-2] Units are independent, so `mylang doctest` executes them on a bounded pool of
workers that pull from one shared cursor, each unit keeping its own private directory, child
process and status. The width is `--jobs N`, then `MYLANG_JOBS`, then the machine's cores capped
by how many per-child memory ceilings its free memory holds — the rule and the ceiling
`mylang test <dir>` fans out under; a zero or non-numeric width is a usage error. Statuses are
collected and reported only after every unit has finished, so the per-document tallies, the
failure lines and the summary are byte-identical to a serial run whatever order the units
complete in; `--verbose` progress lines interleave but each is written whole under one lock. A
host failure in one worker leaves the units the other workers hold running.
- [DOCTEST-3] A compiler child that dies in the host — the host exit status, no `diagnostic-v1`
  record, and either the CLI's host-failure marker or no program output at all — fails its
  example with a `host failure` detail even when the example expects no output, so a dropped
  example can no longer be counted as a run. A located source diagnostic, a guest that returns
  the host status after printing its result, and every other diagnosed failure keep their
  existing meaning.

- [DIAGNOSTIC-RECOVERY-1] Semantic checking recovers at ordinary and symbolic function boundaries after a source error, reports independent function faults in source order, and deduplicates repeated errors at the same source range and code. It stops within the failed function and skips whole-program verification when any body is incomplete; syntax, type-catalog, and namespace construction remain prerequisites. A failed check never publishes typed code or permits execution. Repeating a failed compilation check preserves diagnostics rather than appending duplicates.
- [DIAG-ORACLE-1] Type mismatches render the type the way source spells it, unknown fields and variants list the declared members, unknown functions suggest close candidate names, and arity errors report the expected and given argument counts.

## Proven parameter return regions

- [STANDARD-MODULES-7] Standard generic Result values retain the language's must-handle obligation; an ignored fallible result is a source error.

- [BYTE-IO-BULK-1] The byte application host optionally registers io::read_block(i64)->Block and io::write_block(Block)->i64 for a copied plain Block with bytes:[256]u8 followed by count:i64. Reads return up to 256 useful bytes with zero padding, count zero at EOF, and reject offsets outside [0,input length]. Writes reject counts outside [0,256], append up to remaining unpublished output capacity, and return the accepted count. Each call charges one work unit plus transferred bytes before mutation; a work failure never appends a partial block. The block's record and array shapes are validated against the emitted native host metadata before any callback is linked, and declaring only one of the pair rejects. Existing scalar declarations remain compatible. Output is published only after successful guest exit.
- [BYTE-IO-LIMITS-1] mylang-io accepts unique decimal --max-input, --max-output, --max-steps, --max-calls and --max-work values using = syntax after --project DIRECTORY. It also accepts unique --qbe=, --zig= and --cache-dir= paths for the native build, preferring an explicit path, then the environment, then the tools this build produced. Input/output limits may be zero; execution budgets must be positive. Unknown flags, duplicate limits, non-decimal values and overflow reject before invocation. The configured input limit, output publication capacity and guest call/work budgets are enforced without changing default limits.

- [SCOPED-PROVIDERS-1] Packaged resource adapters accept only relative normal path components under an open directory and exact configured numeric TCP endpoints. Read-only acquisition rejects write/create/truncate modes before effects; network adapters do not resolve DNS or follow redirects.
- [SCOPED-PROVIDERS-2] Directory adapters reject symlink path components, create exclusively, truncate only opened regular files, report actual partial I/O and close all provider handles when their stores end. The host keeps directory and endpoint grants alive until provider stores close.
- [SCOPED-PROVIDERS-3] The packaged synchronous TCP adapter connects only after an exact endpoint grant match, transfers bounded byte chunks with reported progress, and closes the connection on completion or cleanup.

- [SCOPED-PROVIDERS-4] mylang-io provisions directory and numeric endpoint capabilities only with explicit --allow-dir=PATH and --allow-endpoint=IP:PORT arguments; directories default to read-only and --allow-write requires a directory grant. Unknown, duplicate directory, invalid endpoint and unscoped write grants reject before invocation.

- [LOOP-EXIT-1] break and continue are terminating statements targeting the nearest enclosing while loop. They reject outside loops, retain must-handle obligations, conservatively preserve ownership of outer locals, and release exited owned values and nested managed arenas before jumping. Generic bodies enforce the same contracts. Loops remain conservatively fallthrough-capable for return checking.

- [CONSTANT-1] Module-level const NAME: TYPE = EXPR declarations define immutable builtin integer or bool values. Initializers use literals, scalar casts/operators and visible constants with acyclic forward dependencies; allocation and external effects are excluded even in untaken operands; SOURCE-EVAL-1 adds bounded scalar source-function calls. Checking evaluates with width/overflow/division validation and bounded depth/work before emitting literal getters. Normal short-circuit evaluation applies after syntax/dependency validation. Exports and imported names use ordinary privacy checks; generic functions may read constants.
- [CONSTANT-2] Module-level str and bytes constants require a matching text or byte literal initializer, including empty literals and ordinary escapes; calls other than `embed_bytes`/`embed_str` ([EMBED-1]), concatenation, casts and dependencies are rejected even when unused. Transparent aliases resolve normally. Their getters retain ordinary checked literals and contribute managed_allocation on every read, including imported and generic reads; noalloc/effects() callers reject. Scalar constant evaluation is unchanged. Checking, run, build --run and installed executables agree, and literal edits invalidate cached semantics and emitted objects.

- [FOR-RANGE-1] for NAME in START..END iterates a half-open ascending range of matching integer types with a scoped binding the body may assign ([PY-FOR-REBIND-1]). Bounds evaluate once left-to-right; empty/reversed ranges execute no body. Continue performs the hidden checked increment before the next condition; break skips it. Generic ranges retain integer requirements and the same ownership cleanup rules as while.


- [TYPE-ALIAS-1] type NAME = TYPE declares a transparent non-parameterized alias for an existing scalar, aggregate, fixed array or concrete nominal application. Alias chains support forward references and reject cycles or declaration conflicts. They preserve target nominal identity, ownership and result obligations; they never unwrap domains. Exported aliases obey interface visibility checks and private aliases cannot be named across modules. Constants accept aliases of supported scalar types.



## Runtime requested-byte accounting

- [RUNTIME-BYTES-1] Embedded allocation budgets support child quotas clamped to the parent limit. Child allocations and resize/remap operations charge both live-byte limits; failed acquisitions do not charge storage; release restores live accounting and retains peak statistics. Accounting objects must outlive every allocation made through them.
- [INTEGER-EXTENDED-1] Named integer bitwise operations, shifts, wrapping and saturating arithmetic use declared widths; invalid shift counts trap identically across optimization modes.

## Explicit implementation obligations

- [TYPED-HOLES-1] `todo[T]()` declares an unresolved implementation obligation with a checked required type and a located `E_TYPED_HOLE` diagnostic plus related type span. Ordinary and uncalled generic function bodies report independent holes at safe recovery boundaries. Formatting retains them. No checked program, bytecode or executable is published until every hole is replaced. This is an explicit declared type, not inferred expected-type completion.

## Bounded host replay

## Bounded VM explanations


- [FLOAT-DOMAIN-1] Builtin float is IEEE binary64: separate arithmetic operations whose NaNs are canonical wherever they are observed ([FLOAT-NAN-OBSERVED-1]), numeric comparisons in which signed zeros compare equal and every NaN comparison is false, and checked conversion to and from signed integers. The fifteen raw `f64_*` source spellings are removed; ordinary `float` values use numeric operators and the prelude `sqrt`, `floor`, `ceil`, `trunc` and `abs` operations. `src/stdlib/math.my` reaches the correctly rounded binary64 square root through the compiler-internal `__float_sqrt` spelling because the module declares its own `sqrt`, which shadows the prelude helper.
- [INTERNAL-NAME-1] The compiler-internal `__float_sqrt` spelling is accepted only in embedded standard-library sources; ordinary source receives `E_INTERNAL_NAME` and is directed to the public `sqrt` operation.
- [CLOCK-INTRINSIC-1] The compiler-internal `clock_now[R](capability)` and `random_u64[R](capability)` provider reads are accepted only in embedded standard-library sources; ordinary source receives `E_INTERNAL_NAME` and is directed to the `time` and `random` standard modules. The standard `time.now` and `random.from_entropy` wrappers lower to the same provider boundary, so no bytecode or native lowering changes.
- [FLOAT-INTRINSIC-FLOAT-1] The fifteen removed `f64_*` source spellings report `E_REMOVED_SPELLING`; ordinary `float` values use numeric operators, conversions and the prelude `sqrt`, `floor`, `ceil`, `trunc` and `abs` operations.

- [STRUCTURAL-COMPOSITE-1] Structural field and operation signatures substitute fixed arrays and nominal applications. Result obligations remain transferred by value. Invalid unused templates still reject.



- [CONST-LENGTH-1] Generic functions and nominal declarations accept explicit const N:u64 parameters, used as array lengths, forwarded constant arguments and u64 values in function bodies. Literal lengths are bounded to 1048576 and participate in specialization identity separately from type arguments. Symbolic checking preserves unknown lengths and rejects invalid unused templates, including type/value confusion and array literals whose length cannot be proved.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-1] Supported changed-source checks reuse independently owned checked function bodies with relocated spans, nodes, statements and locals; failed checks or snapshot allocation failures do not replace the last complete snapshot.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-2] Declaration/type/import identities invalidate body reuse, and complete region/effect/codec passes rerun after every body edit.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-16] Retained-body validation orders its checks by cost and reads the source once: the dependency digest and both stored lengths reject before any source byte is touched, and the stored region — which encloses the stored body as its trailing slice — is then compared against the fresh source in one pass that decides the body too, because equal region bytes and equal body and region lengths imply equal body bytes. No persisted digest covers the body bytes, so the byte comparison stays the authority rather than being replaced by a digest, and a same-length body edit still misses as a declaration-region change while an unchanged neighbour body is still reused; an entry whose stored shape does not place the body at the end of its region keeps its own body comparison, and the reason a rejected request reports is unchanged: a length change stays a region-length change, a dependency change stays a dependency change, and a changed body or region stays a declaration-region change.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-7] Ordinary checked-body eligibility is per function: an unrelated generic, alias, constant or callback declaration no longer disables reuse, and changing one ordinary body rechecks that body while unchanged dependents reuse. Entries are keyed by stable declaration identity with dependency digests over referenced declarations, types, layouts, constants, scope/import/visibility lookups including negative lookups, and effects/mutation/return-origin contracts. Old function and record ordinals remap to the fresh catalog; missing or ambiguous remapping is a miss, never a partial checked body. Global effect, mutation and codec passes still rerun after every body edit so a reused caller never keeps an obsolete callee verdict.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-8] Ordinary checked bodies persist across compiler processes as versioned `semantic-cache-v2` entries. The durable header/checksum and payload carry only stable declaration identities, dependency contexts, source-region/body bytes and owned remappable checked trees; every decoded reference/range/tag is validated and remapped through C1R or treated as a miss. Only successful checks publish atomically, incompatible compiler/effects/runtime-ABI/flags/mutation-contract versions are isolated, damaged/truncated/oversized entries fall back fresh, and `--no-cache` performs no semantic reads or writes while preserving acceptance and diagnostics.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-15] Durable checked-body publication batches a bounded group of entries and issues one shared filesystem synchronization when the batch closes, instead of a synchronous flush per body. Each entry is still written as a complete checksummed image and atomically renamed before that sync, updates stay proportional to the changed entries, a warm request that publishes nothing performs no sync, and `--no-cache` performs none either. A crash or an interrupted write can therefore leave only a torn image, which the checksum, version, contract, identity, context and bounds validation rejects as a miss; no partial or stale image is ever accepted.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-9] A checked-body snapshot larger than the in-memory retention budget keeps a bounded leading prefix of stable ordinary bodies and drops the remainder instead of disabling reuse for every body. Retained bodies reuse across edits; dropped bodies miss and check fresh on demand; a budget that fits no body preserves the previous complete snapshot only while it still fits this request's ordinary share, otherwise releases it so the combined bound holds.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-10] Relocating a retained ordinary body resolves exactly the declarations that body references. The remap is sized by the body's reference set, not the program catalog, and an old declaration ordinal the body did not carry is an `InvalidRelocation` miss.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-13] `check-session clear` releases every in-memory retention kind — ordinary checked bodies, symbolic templates and concrete specializations — together with their keys, indexes and arenas. Cumulative work counters stay monotonic and `functions.retained_bytes` reports the now-empty storage. Programs already returned from a cleared cache remain valid and deinitialization after clear is safe.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-14] Ordinary checked bodies, symbolic templates and concrete specializations share one `reuse.max_bytes` in-memory budget that accounts for owned entry rows, exact arena backing allocations including per-chunk bookkeeping, stable keys, and exact hash-index backing capacity including the stdlib minimum capacity, header and alignment. Newly checked publication and durable-hit promotion converge on the bound by releasing the globally oldest generic entry or truncating the ordinary snapshot to its fitting leading prefix; evicted entries miss and check fresh, oversized entries bypass retention without changing acceptance, a zero budget disables reuse, and `functions.retained_bytes` reports the total with a per-kind breakdown and eviction count.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-11] Checked symbolic generic templates persist their direct abstract results (effect/mutation masks, body-local diagnostics and ordered abstract call edges) across compiler processes under an explicit `symbolic-template-v1` payload kind and a stable key namespaced away from ordinary checked bodies. Replay requires byte-identical declaration region/body, a matching dependency context, name-resolution mode and abstract-checker version; scoped-bound resolution and structural validation still run on every request, and the whole-graph effect propagation always reruns from the combined fresh and replayed masks. A changed template body invalidates its own entries while an unrelated ordinary body edit preserves template reuse; malformed unused templates are always rechecked; `work-report-v1` reports real `symbolic_templates` checked/reused counts.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-12] Concrete specialization keys are canonical over the template identity, ordered positional type/length descriptors, transitive name-ordered provider contracts, template constraint dependencies, declared effect/mutation/return-origin contracts and the build context, with every boundary length-prefixed. Checked concrete specializations persist under an explicit `specialization-v1` payload kind in the shared `semantic-cache-v2` store and are replayed only when the canonical key, defining declaration region/body, dependency context and build contract match and every stored typed `FunctionRef` and record reference remaps to exactly one fresh identity. Missing, ambiguous, interned-catalog and synthesized-lambda references, and nested specialization references whose instance the fresh run has not yet materialized, are conservative misses; a self-recursive specialization remaps to its own fresh instance. Whole-graph effect and mutation propagation always reruns from the combined fresh and replayed trees. `--no-cache` performs no specialization reads or writes.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-3] Cached source spans and node/statement identities must belong to unchanged mapped intervals and their function's tables; invalid relocation falls back instead of trusting stale references.

- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-4] Reusing a caller body never reuses transitive effect verdicts; a callee's new effect rejects an unchanged effect-bounded caller.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-5] Failed cache publication releases only its pending snapshot; the previous successful function queries remain reusable after OutOfMemory.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-6] Project queries include module paths, import/export text, signatures and type catalogs, preserving fresh/cached diagnostics and execution through source relocation and changed module interfaces.
- [CALLBACK-1] Noncapturing function values have exact parameter/result signatures and effect ceilings, defaulting to unbounded when omitted, including parameter-dependent signatures in generic helpers. An implementation callable is assignable to a destination callable when its parameter count and types and its result type are exactly equal and its declared effect ceiling is a subset of the destination's; the reverse direction rejects. A call through a stored callback uses the destination ceiling. Creation stores an immediate function identity; invocation validates the target and preserves ordinary ownership/result obligations. Callback parameters and results are ordinary copied values; captured environments are rejected.

- [CALLBACK-EFFECTS-1] A callback type with no `effects(...)` ceiling is unbounded, and an unannotated free function is assignable to any callback slot; an explicit narrow ceiling still rejects a function value whose declared ceiling is wider, and a call through a stored callback uses the destination ceiling.
- [CALLBACK-SLOT-1] An unannotated function used as a value takes the effect ceiling of the callback slot it fills: a parameter known at the call, and equally a generic callee's callback parameter once its type arguments are inferred from the checked arguments (`sort.sort_by_key(xs, key)` with `key` unannotated). The function's inferred transitive body effects must fit that ceiling: a function value whose target has no clause of its own and whose body reaches an effect the slot forbids — `managed_allocation` included, so an allocating helper no longer fills an `effects()` slot a `noalloc` caller invokes — is `E_EFFECT_BOUND` at the value, naming the target, the slot type, the effect and its witness path, so no effect can reach a bounded caller through a callback. `mutation` is the mutation analysis's, an unbounded slot accepts any body, and a sort key that builds a `str` still fits because the key slot admits allocation (LAMBDA-KEY-INFER-1).

- [DEAD-RECORD-WRITE-1] Writing a field of a plain-`record` local that was copied from a collection read, a loop iteration variable, or a by-value parameter is rejected with `E_DEAD_RECORD_WRITE` when the copy is never read afterwards, because the write cannot reach the original storage; a field write to a definitely fresh local, or to a copy that is read again after the write, is accepted, and the diagnostic names `class` as the reference-semantics alternative.

- [SEMANTIC-CALLBACK-1] Function-name values may infer callback schemas while checking bodies, so semantic body reuse stays disabled for these programs until snapshots can safely remap their inferred types. Fresh and changed-source callback execution retain identical signatures and outcomes.

- [STANDARD-TIME-RANDOM-1] Standard durations and Unix nanosecond instants use checked signed arithmetic that reports overflow, reversal and unrepresentable spans as Result failures instead of wrapping or trapping; seeded SplitMix64 has stable word sequences, independent snapshots when a generator is copied under value semantics (a shared stream only under legacy sharing), caller-state advancement through `mut` parameters, and inclusive integer sampling that covers the whole signed range without modulo bias.
- [STANDARD-TIME-RANDOM-2] Standard clock and entropy calls require explicit capabilities and keep the unavailable, denied and failed provider categories apart in their reported messages; a later failed read leaves earlier results intact and host allocation failure aborts normally.
- [STANDARD-TIME-RANDOM-3] Standard provider wrappers preserve source effect ceilings, so a caller declaring a narrower ceiling than clock or random plus managed allocation is rejected while checking; one entropy-seeded generator performs exactly one provider read and replays identically from its state word.

- [TYPE-ALIAS-SCHEMA-1] Exported transparent aliases retain their checked canonical target in public schemas and native interfaces, including scalar, array, chained and concrete generic targets.



- [SCOPED-DIRECTORY-1] Read-authorized `m:path` metadata and `l:path` directory listing acquisitions publish bounded immutable byte snapshots under the existing directory grant. Empty paths denote that root only for these modes; intermediate/final directory traversal never follows symlinks, while final metadata reports a symlink itself.
- [SCOPED-DIRECTORY-2] Failed directory snapshot construction releases partial bytes and open directories before returning; allocation failures never publish a live snapshot handle.


## Standard library over managed types

- [STANDARD-MATH-1] Standard math classifies, rounds and roots binary64 values with IEEE behaviour preserved at zeros, infinities and not-a-number; sqrt reduces by exact powers of four and refines by Newton iteration, pow takes an integer exponent whose magnitude saturates by parity, and abs, gcd, lcm and isqrt report the values the signed range cannot hold as Result failures.
- [STANDARD-MATH-TRIG-1] Standard `math.sin` and `math.cos` accept one binary64 angle in radians and use native sine/cosine with range reduction across finite inputs. Sine preserves signed zero and subnormal inputs, cosine of either zero is one, and nonfinite inputs produce not-a-number under the ordinary float observation rules. The implementation uses compiler-owned intrinsics: raw internal spellings and forged stdlib paths are rejected with E_INTERNAL_NAME. Existing unary opcode values stay fixed; the appended trigonometric operations never enter the checked integer-conversion helper.
- [STANDARD-STRINGS-1] Standard strings add the classification, search, trimming, padding, case mapping and numeric conversion that builtin str lacks. `to_int` delegates to `int(text)` and `to_float` delegates to `float(text)`, so both wrappers use the pinned numeric grammar and parser; the float wrapper accepts surrounding whitespace, digit underscores, infinities and NaNs that its former guest parser rejected. `format_int` covers bases 2 to 36 including the most negative integer, and `format_float` rounds halves away from zero and rejects magnitudes it cannot render.
- [STANDARD-FORMAT-FLOAT-EXACT-1] `strings.format_float` rounds the exact binary value of its argument: when the scaled product `|value| * 10**decimals` rounds onto an exact half (for example `2.675 * 100.0 == 267.5`), the exact product error decides, so `format_float(2.675, 2)` is `"2.67"` as in C and Python. Only a value whose scaled product is exactly a half rounds away from zero (`format_float(0.125, 2)` is `"0.13"`), and a result that rounds to zero carries no sign (`format_float(-0.001, 2)` is `"0.00"`).
- [STANDARD-STRINGS-2] `strings.repeat(text, times)` doubles its copies instead of appending one copy at a time: the answer so far is appended to itself while its copy count stays within `times`, and one prefix of it tops up the rest, so building an answer of `n` octets copies at most about `3n` octets and takes a logarithmic number of concatenations, where the old loop copied the growing answer once per copy and was quadratic in `times`. An empty `text` or a zero count answers the empty text at once; a negative count is still `Error.Invalid`. Every answer is the same text.
- [STANDARD-SORT-1] Standard ordering is a stable merge over a copy that leaves its input unchanged, for integers, binary64 values, Unicode code point order on text, and any element type through an integer key callback. The concrete integer, float and text wrappers delegate to the same one-work-buffer engine as `sorted`, and the keyed form to the keyed sort (STANDARD-SORT-KEY-1). Text comparison reads stored Unicode scalars directly without allocating a byte encoding. Binary search, extrema and duplicate removal report absence through Option and keep first-occurrence order.
- [STANDARD-PATH-1] Standard path operations are pure text algebra with no filesystem authority: joining honours an absolute right side, basename and dirname agree with the trailing-separator convention, splitext ignores a leading dot, and normalize removes duplicate separators, single dots and resolvable parents while keeping unresolvable ones.
- [STANDARD-CODECS-1] Standard base64 emits canonical padding for both RFC 4648 alphabets and decodes only canonical input, rejecting a length that is not a multiple of four, a symbol outside the alphabet, padding before the final group and a final group with nonzero unused bits. Hexadecimal transcoding accepts either letter case and rejects odd lengths.
- [STANDARD-JSON-1] Standard JSON parses strict RFC 8259 text into a recursive union, rejecting comments, trailing commas, leading zeros, bare fractions and exponents, unescaped control characters, lone surrogates, duplicate object keys, trailing text and nesting past a fixed limit, and naming the byte offset in every message. Objects keep parsed key order, a number without fraction or exponent that fits the signed range stays exact, and rendering round-trips a parsed document without added whitespace.
- [STDLIB-ERROR-UNION-1] A standard module whose operations can fail exports an error union and returns `Result[T, Module.Error]` from every exported fallible operation instead of `Result[T, str]`; each case carries a `str` payload reachable through an exported `message(error)` helper, so callers match on category instead of the human text and adding a case is a breaking interface change. `json` and `strings` are converted; `math`, `time`, `random`, `base64` and `hex` still return `Result[T, str]`.
- [STANDARD-POOL-1] A standard pool hands out copyable generational handles into a reusable slot store. Removing a slot advances its generation so every earlier handle stops matching; a stale or out-of-range handle reports absence and no handle traps, released slots are reused before the store grows, and copies of a pool value refer to one shared store. A handle names a slot and a generation rather than a pool, so it must be used with the pool that issued it.
- [STANDARD-SORT-KEY-1] `sort.sort_by_key(values, key)` is a thin forwarder to `sorted(values, key=key)` (LIST-SORT-KEY-1), so it returns a new stable list and leaves its input unchanged. Each key is computed exactly once, in input order — earlier versions computed them in the order a recursive merge first asked for each one, so a key function that traps on several elements now raises the trap of the first such element in input order, as Python does. The extra memory is the result, one key word per element and the native sort's scratch, for lists of any length.

## Application library parity

- [STANDARD-INTEGER-EXTREMA-1] Standard math integer min, max and clamp compare exact values at every supported integer width without arithmetic overflow, including both ends of each signed and unsigned range.

## Ordinary JSON schema helpers




## Bytecode execution


- [BYTECODE-INLINE-1] Lowering inlines a direct call to a non-entry leaf function whose every instruction is on a no-fault, no-allocation allow-list, whose locals need no owned-resource cleanup, and whose owner scope (when any) matches the caller's. Splicing remaps the callee's locals and branch targets to fresh caller slots and the continuation pc, preserving results, trap traces and GC roots; a call to a fallible or allocating callee is left intact.

- [BYTECODE-INLINE-2] Lowering also inlines a direct call to a non-entry leaf function that can trap but cannot allocate: the allow-list admits bounds-checked scalar `get_index`, integer `binary_integer` and the checked add/sub/mul/div/rem, `cast_integer`, `neg_*`, the float conversions and `sqrt`-style operations of `unary_float` (the pure `myl_qbe_float_unary`), `array_length`, `get_field`, `update_record`, `switch_variant` and predicates, while `str` indexing and every call, managed construction, buffer, arena, print and file/resource/provider operation stay out. Every spliced instruction records its callee, callee-local pc and call-site pc; the emitter groups those into an inline-frame table linked into the function metadata, and the runtime walks it to emit the callee frames followed by the enclosing frame at the call site, innermost first, under the same sixteen-frame cap and `omitted_frames` accounting. Trap text, `MYLANG-TRAP` JSON and source locations stay byte-identical to the pre-inlining compiler, including nested inlines; the step budget still counts the rewritten instructions rather than the removed call.
- [BYTECODE-INLINE-3] A callee larger than the flat inline budget is spliced only where the call site runs inside a loop of the caller: the inliner marks every instruction in a loop body — the contiguous pc range that ends at a backward branch, which the structured lowering guarantees — and a larger callee is inlined there while the same callee's single-shot call sites keep their call, so a hot path loses its call while a cold path keeps its code size. A spliced larger callee gets the same provenance, inline-frame replay and trap text as a smaller one.
- [BYTECODE-INLINE-4] Eligible leaf calls may also be inlined into the entry function. Each removed call retains a call-site marker outside its callee trace range. Builds with frame accounting check the physical depth plus all active inline ancestors at that marker, after argument evaluation and before entering the callee; `--max-frames` refusals report only frames already entered, including for zero-argument and nested calls. Sibling calls reuse logical depth. Builds without frame accounting omit the check.
- [BYTECODE-INLINE-5] The no-allocation inline allow-list admits constructors whose complete type contains only plain scalar, fixed-array, domain, record or variant values, excluding managed edges, recursive boxes, callbacks and resources. Plain `Result` callees preserve error construction, propagation and return sites from the original callee, including nested splices. A callee using `?` is spliced only when its call has no unfinished caller operands below the arguments; other sites keep the call so an error cannot unwind the caller's operands.

- [NATIVE-TIMING-1] `run --timings` reports native execution nanoseconds on stderr, covering the guest process and the native build it needed but excluding source compilation. Timing preserves guest results, quotas and runtime diagnostics; duplicate options and unsupported commands reject it.

- [PHASE-TIMING-1] One-shot compiler commands and the raw byte I/O host accept opt-in `--timings=json`, emitting versioned phase-timings-v1 JSON Lines on stderr. Records use nonnegative duration_ns, explicit inclusive scope, available work counts and the command exit_code; total includes cleanup and excludes timing serialization. Attempted failing phases remain observable, stdout and default behavior are preserved, and legacy `--timings` remains compatible.

- [PHASE-TIMING-2] Opt-in project timing may additionally separate source/header load, syntax replay and parse, declarations/interfaces, source merge, dependency-key preparation, ordinary-body replay, symbolic templates, concrete-specialization replay, fresh body checking, effect/origin passes, warning derivation and semantic retention/durable-store I/O. These records are bounded, disabled by default, never affect cache keys, acceptance, diagnostics or work-report counts, and use `inclusive=false` for disjoint phases while only `total` is inclusive.

- [ERGONOMIC-INTEGRATION-1] Late exports, inferred generic calls and unit procedures compose across module boundaries, preserving cross-module effect and result obligations.
- [GENERIC-INFERENCE-1] Generic function calls may omit their entire type-argument list when checked call arguments and the expected result type uniquely determine every type and const length parameter. Matching follows direct variables, fixed arrays, nominal applications, and callback signatures; repeated occurrences must agree. Abstract callers retain distinct symbolic identities and declared requirements. Argument expressions are checked and evaluated once in their original order; ordinary type, effect, ownership, and structural checks remain in force. Constraints alone, defaults, and partial explicit lists do not infer missing arguments; ambiguity and conflicting inference report E_GENERIC_INFERENCE. Unmaterialized phantom type arguments require explicit specialization.
## Unit procedures

- [UNIT-1] The builtin `unit` is a distinct no-value type. Unit functions accept `return;` and implicit fallthrough; `main` still returns `i64`.
- [UNIT-2] Ordinary standalone expressions must have type `unit`; all other values require `_ =` or result handling, including generic bodies. VM calls preserve unit identity and ownership cleanup.
- [UNIT-3] Unit is described explicitly in public schemas and is rejected by data/wire and host ABI boundaries.
- [UNIT-4] Unit fallthrough and early return release owned parameters through the ordinary cleanup paths.
- [UNIT-5] Fallible values with unit success payloads retain all result obligations; a successful `try` yielding unit may appear as a standalone statement.
- [UNIT-6] Public unit-returning functions remain callable within language modules. Their schema uses `kind: unit`; copied VM/host/native ABI invocation rejects unit-containing signatures explicitly instead of interpreting unit as a scalar or capability.

## Managed tracing heap

- [GC-ROOTS-1] Managed collections and aggregate snapshots retain independent values across later mutations. Retained values, including intermediate call arguments and aggregate snapshots, root managed objects across allocation and stress collection. Stable shared identity is available only under `--legacy-sharing`.
- [GC-PACING-1] The tracing collector's pause interval grows with the live set and keeps a floor: after a collection the next one runs once the young generation has grown by half the surviving bytes or four mebibytes, whichever is larger (the first runs at one mebibyte), and that collection is a full one once the old generation — the bytes the last collection kept — reaches twice the bytes the last full collection kept plus four mebibytes, so a steady allocation workload re-marks its long-lived set a few times instead of at every collection. Collection timing is not observable — the language has no destructors and no program-visible collection counter, and `--gc-stress` still collects at every allocation — and the byte budget is still enforced on every allocation, so peak memory stays within the configured ceiling.
- [GC-OBJECT-1] A tracing-heap object carries a two-word header immediately before its payload: a mark word holding bit 0, the immortal-literal flag, the element count and the large-object flag (bit 63), then the element descriptor. The count stays in the header because precise tracing needs it for any payload address, including one first reached through a root. A large object — one in a size class above 8 KiB, or an oversized one with a segment of its own — keeps the collector's mark in bit 0; every other object's mark is a bit of its segment's side bitmap, and its bit 0 only records that it is old, for the store barrier. The collector's marking worklist is a heap-side array grown on demand; a refused reservation abandons the collection unmarked and refuses the allocation that ran it, so a partly marked graph is never swept. The payload stays aligned to `max_align_t` behind the two words. An arena payload carries the same two words, with the arena flag and its depth in the mark word.
- [GC-SEGMENT-1] Tracing-heap storage is reserved in size-classed segments instead of one host allocation per object. An object's charge is its header plus payload rounded to the payload's 16-byte alignment, then up to a class stride. A class of at most 8 KiB lives in 64 KiB segments aligned to their size and carved from larger host reservations, so an object finds its segment's mark bits by masking its own address; a larger class uses 64 KiB slabs, and an oversized object a one-slot segment of its own. A class hands out storage from holes — runs of free slots a sweep found — and from the never-used, zero-filled tail of its newest segment; a reused slot's payload is zeroed before the program sees it, except a coallocated text block's (GC-NOZERO-1). The sweep reads a segment's mark bits, not its slots: the runs between consecutive live objects become holes, and only the first slot of each receives a hole header. A one-slot oversized segment whose object died returns whole to the host. The byte budget charges the class stride rather than the exact request, refuses an allocation that would pass the limit after a full collection, and spends retained arena chunks before reporting host failure; a segment's unused tail is uncharged runtime slack.
- [GC-GENERATIONS-1] The tracing collector is generational without moving anything: every object that survives a collection stays marked and is old, and an allocation is young. A minor collection marks only young objects — a reference to an old object is not followed — and reclaims only unreachable young objects, sweeping only the segments storage was handed out from since the last sweep; its extra roots are every old object a later store may have given a young reference: each old object whose descriptor is traced and is not a `class` body (list and dictionary headers, element storage, byte views, which change without a barrier), and each old `class` body the store barrier reported. A full collection clears every mark first and reclaims old garbage too; it runs when the old generation reaches the full threshold, whenever an allocation would otherwise cross the byte budget (so an allocation is refused only after a full collection), after a remembered-set or worklist reservation failed, and for `myl_native_heap_collect`. Under `--gc-stress` every allocation still collects, and every third collection is full, so two minor collections run between full ones. Values, traps, traces and exit status are unchanged.
- [GC-AGING-1] A minor collection promotes a young object only the second time it finds it reachable. The first time the object stays young: it is kept (not reclaimed), its mark is cleared again after the sweep and a per-granule age bit records that it survived once; the next minor collection promotes it if it is still reachable and reclaims it otherwise, so a structure that is live across one minor collection and dies before the next — a tree under construction — is never promoted into the old generation. A young object first reached through an old object (an old list, dictionary or remembered `class` body) is promoted at once, since the old container keeps it. A promoted `class` body that references an object the same collection keeps young is listed as remembered, exactly as if the store barrier had reported it, so the next minor collection still traces that edge; a remembered body stays listed while it references a young object. A full collection promotes every survivor. Only promotion counts toward the full-collection threshold. Values, traps, traces and exit status are unchanged.
- [GC-SPARE-1] A size-classed segment a sweep leaves with no object in it (other than its class's current bump segment) is unlinked from its class and becomes a spare, which the next class of any stride that needs a new segment takes before a new host mapping is made. A spare keeps its pages, so its bytes are stale and every slot handed out from it is cleared the way a reused hole's slot is. Size-classed segments are carved from 2 MiB-aligned private anonymous mappings, which read as zero until written. Values, traps and exit status are unchanged.
- [GC-NOZERO-1] A coallocated block (`myl_native_heap_alloc_block`, the storage of every `str` built at run time) does not clear a reused slot's payload: its caller writes every octet it reads before the next safepoint, and its payload is plain bytes the collector never interprets. An arena block is still carved from zero-filled storage, and every other allocation is still zeroed. Values, traps and exit status are unchanged.
- [GC-POOLSET-1] The collector tells a size-classed object from its address alone — whether it lies in one of the heap's pool mappings — so marking a reference to an object that is already marked, which is most of what a minor collection's rescan of old lists and dictionaries does, reads the object's mark and age bits and not its header. Objects outside the pools (large and oversized objects, arena allocations and frozen literals) are still classified by their header. Values, traps and exit status are unchanged.
- [QBE-GEN-BARRIER-1] A field update of a `class` body that stores a value the collector traces first runs the generational barrier: one load and test of the body's mark bit, and for an old body a call to `myl_native_heap_remember`, which clears the bit and lists the body for the next minor collection, so each old body is listed at most once per collection. A field update that stores only scalars runs no barrier. A generated `class` body descriptor is the only one marked `.cell`; every store into an existing class body is a field update (`update_record`), which is what makes the barrier complete.
- [QBE-INLINE-ALLOC-1] Constructing a `class` whose body plus its two-word header fits a size class of at most 256 bytes allocates inline: the generated code bumps the cursor of the heap's per-class inline window over one prepaid slot, writes the header, zeroes the payload and runs the constructor, with no runtime call and no root record. Only an exhausted window calls `myl_native_heap_alloc_small`, the one safepoint and the only branch that publishes roots; it collects exactly when `myl_native_heap_alloc` would, refuses at the budget exactly where it would, and then opens the class's next window on a hole or fresh storage, charging in advance only as many slots as the budget and the collection trigger both still admit. Every prepaid slot nobody took is discharged before any collection decision, when an arena opens, and by the recount after every collection, so the trigger and the `R_MEMORY_BUDGET` refusal happen exactly where charging one object at a time would put them. The windows stay empty while an arena is open, under `--gc-stress` and during an `escape` copy. In a program that opens arenas, a construction inside one bumps the innermost arena's current chunk instead — the free tail the runtime last allocated from at that depth — writing that depth's arena header; the chunk is zero-filled, and every runtime operation on arena chunks first folds that progress back into the chunk. A larger class body keeps the runtime call.
- [GC-BOUNDARY-1] Managed list operations enforce element types, and an out-of-range read or write raises a located list-bounds trap.
- [GC-SOURCE-1] Managed local copies can be mutated through a `mut` helper without explicit allocator authority. Updating their source requires an explicit store-back; the original remains unchanged until that store.
- [GC-SOURCE-3] Managed results held as intermediate call arguments or inside ordinary record snapshots survive collection triggered by later arguments and callee allocations.
- [GC-SOURCE-4] Out-of-bounds managed reads and writes produce located R_LIST_BOUNDS traps and release execution roots and backing.
- [GC-SOURCE-6] `noalloc` rejects transitive managed allocation and list growth; fixed-slot mutation and reads remain permitted.
- [GC-SOURCE-7] Managed element declarations reject owned resources and unchecked results, including such values nested in ordinary aggregates.
- [GC-SOURCE-9] Bytecode validation rejects managed metadata on record shapes that are not managed containers.
- [GC-GENERIC-3] Directly recursive value declarations without a finite construction remain rejected. Recursive records and unions with finite cases use the immutable boxes defined by RECURSIVE-VALUES-1.
- [GC-GENERIC-4] Source formatting preserves generic list declarations and contextual uses of list as a variant name.

## Managed standard library

- [GC-GENERIC-5] Managed list elements cannot contain allocator or provider capabilities, including nested records, arrays, and instantiated generic elements; declarations reject with E_LIST_CAPABILITY.
- [GC-CAPABILITY-1] Managed element declarations reject capability values and capabilities nested in aggregates, preserving the source authority boundary.


## Managed arenas


- [SURFACE-STMT-1] A statement ends at its newline, at the dedent that closes its suite, or at EOF; two statements written on one line are separated by `;`.
- [SURFACE-STMT-2] Postfix `?` propagates result errors and unwraps success with postfix precedence.
- [SURFACE-STMT-3] `None` and `()` both spell the unit type and unit value; a bare `return` before a newline or a dedent returns unit.
- [SURFACE-STMT-4] Calls and function parameters permit trailing commas; a delimiter continues an expression across newlines.
- [SURFACE-STMT-5] Parse-only declaration listing and module formatting preserve newline-terminated module, import, export, and type directives.
- [SURFACE-STMT-6] Project discovery recognizes newline-terminated directives and declarations while allowing multiline function signatures.

## Command-line authority

- [CAPABILITY-1] `Filesystem`, `Network`, `Clock`, `Entropy` and `Environment` are the canonical capability types. The removed `*_cap` spellings report `E_REMOVED_SPELLING` with the corresponding type; declared or imported types of the canonical name still win. Authority stays explicit: an entry point declares what it needs by parameter type, helpers receive grants as ordinary arguments, and no numeric cast or copy mints one.
- [CAPABILITY-2] `mylang run` and `run-project` grant every capability by default: filesystem reading and writing with no directory restriction, the clock, entropy and the whole process environment. `--restrict` switches the command to deny-by-default, and only then do `--allow-read=DIR` and `--allow-write=DIR` (each repeatable, up to eight directories), `--allow-clock`, `--allow-random`, `--allow-env` and `--allow-all` narrow anything; without `--restrict` they are accepted and narrow nothing. `--allow-random=SEED` pins the entropy seed in either mode, because it selects a stream rather than withholding one; an unseeded stream is seeded from the wall clock. `--allow-all` grants the clock, entropy, every environment name and read/write access to the working directory, and cannot be combined with another grant. A repeated flag, an empty-valued flag, and any of these options on a command that does not execute a program are usage errors.
- [CAPABILITY-3] Under `--restrict`, an entry point that names a capability the command line did not grant is rejected after checking and lowering but before the first guest instruction, with a message that says `--restrict` is active, names the flag that would grant the capability, and offers to drop `--restrict`; the status is 2. Without `--restrict` that refusal cannot happen. Grants for capabilities the entry point does not request provision providers that are never read.
- [CAPABILITY-4] The default filesystem grant is one direct root over the process working directory: a relative guest path resolves against it and an absolute path names itself, each operation applies the whole path at once and follows symlinks, and write authority is always present, so the default can never be narrower than a read grant. Under `--restrict`, directory grants are an ordered search path of scoped roots instead: a guest path must be relative with no empty, `.`, `..`, NUL or backslash component, it is tried against each granted root in order and the first success wins, traversal opens each component with symlink following disabled and final opens reject symlinks. Modes that change the tree — write, exclusive create, truncate, create directory and remove — consider only roots granted with `--allow-write`, which also grants reading that root, and a read-only grant rejects them before opening anything. Neither mode is a sandbox: the providers run inside the compiler process, the host filesystem tree is trusted, directory handles retain authority across renames, and directory scoping does not distinguish hard links to shared inodes.
- [CAPABILITY-5] The clock reports Unix-epoch nanoseconds from the host wall clock, with no monotonicity guarantee. Entropy is a reproducible pseudorandom stream, seeded by `--allow-random=SEED` when given and otherwise from the wall clock; it is not a cryptographic entropy source, and a program needing one must be embedded with its own provider. The environment exposes the host process environment, restricted to the `--allow-env=NAME,...` allowlist when one is given, reporting absent names as absent and denying names outside the list.
- [CAPABILITY-6] The default filesystem grant carries the process's own authority, so like C's `fopen` it opens whatever the process can open other than a directory: a FIFO, a character device such as `/dev/null`, or the `/dev/fd/N` pipe a shell's process substitution `<(...)` names, and a read waits for the FIFO's writer and reads to end of file; truncation still applies only to a regular file. Under `--restrict`, a granted directory serves regular files only, exactly as before: a FIFO or device inside it is refused with the ordinary denial, opened without waiting for a writer, and absolute paths such as `/dev/fd/N` remain outside every grant.

## Ordinary output

- [IO-1] `stream.print(values...)` writes each argument's Python rendering to the receiver stream's destination, separated by single spaces and followed by one newline. `int` and the fixed-width integers render in decimal, `bool` as `True`/`False`, `None` as `None`, `float` with a `.0` suffix when it has no fractional digits and `nan`/`inf`/`-inf` for the nonfinite values, `str` raw at the top level and `repr`-quoted inside a container, and `bytes` as a Python `b'...'` literal with `\n`, `\r`, `\t`, `\'`, `\\` and `\xNN` escapes. Lists render as `[a, b]`, insertion-ordered dictionaries as `{k: v}` and fixed arrays as `[a, b]`. Nesting deeper than 24 levels renders as `...`, so a cyclic managed graph still terminates. `stream.print()` writes only the newline.
- [IO-2] Records render as `Name(field=value, ...)` in declaration order, enum cases as `Name.case`, union cases as `Name.case(payload)` or `Name.case`, checked results as `Ok(value)`/`Err(value)` and optionals as `Some(value)`/`None`. Module qualification is stripped from the printed name. A record with a moved field prints `<moved>` for that field rather than reading it.
- [IO-3] `stream.print` accepts the keyword arguments `sep=` and `end=`, each an ordinary `str` expression that replaces the separator or the trailing newline. Positional arguments must precede both keywords, neither may be repeated, and any other keyword is rejected with `E_IO_ARGUMENT`. Printed values evaluate in source order, then `sep=`, then `end=`. Both keywords materialize the prelude `str` type on demand, so a program whose only text is a separator still checks, and a `stream.print` call without keywords needs no text schema at all.
- [IO-4] `stream.print` and `stream.write` contribute the `output` effect. `noio` withholds `filesystem`, `network` and `output` together, so a direct or transitive `stream.print` under `noio` is rejected with `E_EFFECT_BOUND` and a call witness; `effects(output)` permits it and `noalloc` does not constrain it. Bytecode validation independently recomputes the effect from the emitted instruction.
- [IO-5] `stream.print` is checked only on a receiver whose type is `Stream`; a bare `print`/`eprint` reports `E_OUTPUT_CAPABILITY` with the fix to add `out: Stream` and pass one from main, while a local binding or declared function named `print` remains an ordinary name. Capabilities, files, owned resources and callback values have no rendering and are rejected with `E_IO_ARGUMENT`. A host that supplies no output writer discards program output rather than failing, and a writer that refuses bytes traps with `R_OUTPUT`.
- [IO-6] The QBE native backend lowers `stream.print`. The compiler emits one C renderer per type a program actually prints, so every value shape renders byte-identically to `src/io_render.zig`, including `sep=`/`end=` raw text, the 24-level depth cap and the float spelling. A call buffers its whole line before any byte reaches the stream and flushes once; a refused write traps `R_OUTPUT`. Generic function bodies reject `stream.print` and the whole-path file builtins with `E_GENERIC_BODY_UNSUPPORTED` rather than reporting them as unknown functions.
- [IO-7] `with` is one statement form with two resource kinds. `with arena() as name:` opens a managed region; any other expression must produce an open file handle and must name its binding. `open(fs, path, mode)` takes a `Filesystem` capability, a relative `str` path and a literal mode — `"r"`, `"w"`, `"a"` with an optional `"b"` or `"t"` — and returns `Result[File, str]` for binary modes or `Result[TextFile, str]` for text modes. Directly as the head of a `with` statement the call yields the handle itself and a failure traps with a located `R_FILE`, the way Python's `open` raises; `with open(...)? as f:` propagates the result instead, and every other position yields the ordinary result. A binary handle's `read`, `readline` and `readlines` produce `bytes`; a text handle's produce `str`, decoded as UTF-8. `f.read()` reads to end of file, `f.read(n)` reads at most `n` bytes, `f.readline()` keeps its terminator, and `f.readlines()` returns the lines with their terminators kept.
- [IO-8] `f.write(data)` accepts the handle's own element type, writes every byte by looping over the provider's partial progress, and returns the count. `f.flush()` exists for familiarity and is a no-op because writes are unbuffered. Mode `"w"` creates the file or truncates an existing one, `"a"` appends at the current end, and `"r"` neither creates nor truncates. Whole-file reads and writes are capped at 64 MiB.
- [IO-9] `read_file`, `read_text`, `write_file`, `write_text`, `listdir`, `mkdir` and `remove` each take the capability first and return `Result[T, str]`, whose error text names the operation, the path and a readable reason. `exists(fs, path)` returns `bool` rather than a result. A helper opens, uses and closes its resource within the call, so a failure leaves no handle behind.
- [IO-10] A directory granted without write authority rejects every writing mode — `w`, `a`, create, truncate, `mkdir` and `remove` — before opening anything, and the returned error says the mode needs a write grant.
- [IO-11] A file `with` scope closes its handle exactly once at every exit: falling off the end, `return`, `?` propagation, `break`, `continue`, an explicit `f.close()`, and a trap or host allocation failure unwinding through it. A method on a closed or stale handle traps with `R_FILE`; so does any provider failure during a method, since `f.read()` yields bytes rather than a result. The trap message carries the same readable reason as the `Result` error text.
- [IO-12] File operations contribute the `filesystem` effect, so `noio` rejects them with `E_EFFECT_BOUND`. `with` on a value that is not an arena or an open file is rejected with `E_WITH_RESOURCE`, which tells a `Result` operand to propagate with `open(...)?` or match first. A non-literal mode, an unsupported mode and an unknown handle method are rejected with `E_FILE_ARGUMENT`. Generic function bodies reject file operations. The QBE native backend runs the whole file surface too, over a native scoped-directory provider with the same request grammar, `Result` error text and located `R_FILE` traps; an open handle is one owned resource word that the ordinary local drop closes at every scope exit. A native executable grants every capability by default and `--restrict` switches it to deny-by-default with the same `--allow-*` flags, which `mylang build --run` forwards after `--`.
- [IO-13] A `class` value renders its own fields wherever it is nested — a union or option payload, a record or class field, a tuple or fixed-array element, a list or dictionary element — exactly as it renders at the top level. Every generated renderer receives the address of its value's slot, and a class slot holds the body pointer, so the class renderer loads that pointer before reading any field; no renderer reads a field at the slot itself.
- [IO-14] `stream.print` renders every finite `float` in its whole positional spelling, however long: the renderer's frame holds the longest finite double (the smallest subnormal's `0.` and 323 zeros, or the largest double's 309 integer digits), so `1.2345e300`, `-1.7976931348623157e308` and `5e-324` print digit for digit as `str(x)` spells them, where a value longer than 64 characters used to leave the line unprintable and trap `InternalError`.
- [IO-15] `print` and `write` are methods of any `Stream`-typed receiver, not only of a bare name: a `Stream` held in a class or record field prints and writes through the field expression (`sink.stream.write(data)`, `pair.first.stream.print(...)`) exactly as a bound local does, with the same `output` effect, return value and lowering. Such a receiver takes positional arguments only (`sep=`/`end=` still need the stream bound to a name), and any other method on it is `E_COLLECTION_METHOD` with a message naming `print` and `write` instead of the collection methods.
- [CLI-CACHE-DIR-1] `--cache-dir=DIR` (or, for the frontend caches, `MYLANG_FRONTEND_CACHE=DIR`) keeps every persistent cache a command writes under `DIR`, including the ones a command's own compiler children open: `mylang test` gives its `list-interfaces --contracts` child the same `--cache-dir` as its facts, check and build children, and the checked-body store and the `--explain-invalidation` baseline follow `MYLANG_FRONTEND_CACHE` exactly as the syntax cache does. With no directory named the caches keep their per-project `.mylang-cache` defaults. `--no-cache` leaves native objects in the default object cache, as before.
- [RUN-FLAGS-1] `mylang run` and `run-project` take every execution budget in both spellings, `--flag N` and `--flag=N`, for `--max-steps`, `--max-frames`, `--timeout` and `--max-memory`, and check the value exactly as the `=` spelling is checked (a missing, zero or non-numeric value is a usage failure). A rejected `run` or `run-project` command line, including `mylang run` with no file, prints the usage of those two commands, which lists each budget in both spellings, not the `mylang test` usage the general text opens with; a bare `mylang` still prints the general usage and exits 2.
- [RUN-FRAME-BUDGET-1] A `--max-frames` budget reserves 32 KiB of native stack per frame plus 8 MiB inside the guest's address space, which is its `--max-memory` ceiling plus 256 MiB and never more than the limit the process inherited (the compiler's own 8 GiB ceiling, `MYLANG_HOST_MEMORY_LIMIT`, or `ulimit -v`). `mylang run`, `run-project` and `test` refuse a budget that cannot fit before any build or guest starts, with exit 2 and `mylang <command>: a call-depth budget of N frames (--max-frames) reserves X of native stack (32 KiB per frame), but <guest> may map only Y: ...`, naming either the `--max-memory` that would hold it and the largest `--max-frames` that fits, or, when the inherited process limit is the cause, that limit and `MYLANG_HOST_MEMORY_LIMIT=0`. The runtime's own stack mapping reports the same facts instead of a bare `OutOfMemory` for a budget that slips past the check (for example a built program run with `MYLANG_OPTIONS`). The former "hard cap near 269,622 frames" was this inherited 8 GiB limit, not a size overflow. `R_FRAME_LIMIT` advises `--max-frames=N` together with a `--max-memory` that covers N x 32 KiB.
- [PROJECT-LIMITS-1] `mylang.json` may carry a `limits` object with a `run` and a `test` entry, each any of `max_steps` and `max_frames` (positive integers up to 4294967295) and `max_memory` (a byte count or a K/M/G size; zero removes the ceiling). `mylang run`, `run-project` and `test` read the configuration nearest the entry file and use these as defaults for the matching flag: a flag on the command line always wins, `MYLANG_MEMORY_LIMIT` outranks the file's `max_memory`, and the built-in defaults apply to whatever the file leaves out. The values go through the same checks as the flags, so a configured `max_frames` the memory cannot back is refused exactly as the flag is. A directory run leaves each program to its own configuration, and a missing or invalid configuration changes nothing here because it is reported where configuration errors already are. An unknown or duplicate key, an entry that is not an object, and a zero, non-integer or out-of-range budget are configuration failures.
- [CLI-INPUT-REFUSED-1] Every command that takes a source entry (`check`, `check-module`, `check-project`, `run`, `run-project`, `build`, `test`, `fmt`, `fmt-module`, `fix`, `lint`, `api`, `invoke`, `list-*`, `emit-*`, `dump-*` and the rest) probes it before any cache opens or any work starts. A missing path, a path the process may not read and a directory where a file is required are refused with `mylang <command>: cannot read '<path>': <reason>` on stderr (or the `diagnostic-v1` message form under `--diagnostics=json`), where the reason is `no such file or directory`, `permission denied` or `is a directory, not a file`, and exit 2 like every usage failure; `test` and `fix` accept a directory. `invoke` answers with its `native-invocation-v1` `setup-failure` whose `failure.diagnostics` carries the reason. A refused input creates no cache directory, and no command prints a Zig error-return trace for a host failure.
- [CAPABILITY-FIELD-METHOD-1] A capability held in a record field takes method syntax through the field expression exactly as a bound local does: `ctx.fs.read_text(path)`, `ctx.fs.open(path, "rb")` (also as a `with` head, where a direct `open` traps `R_FILE`), `ctx.fs.dir("data")`, `ctx.fs.read_only()`, `ctx.env.get`, `ctx.env.only`, `ctx.env.names` and `ctx.net.host(name)` check and lower to the nodes the local spelling builds, so effects, permissions, literal-argument rules, arity errors and `noio` bounds are unchanged. The same holds for any receiver expression, such as `make_fs().dir("x")` or `pair.first.fs`. `Clock`, `Entropy` and `Host` have no methods, and an unknown method on a capability receiver is `E_COLLECTION_METHOD` whose message names that receiver's own methods rather than the collection methods.
- [IO-FILE-WRITE-LINEAR-1] `fs.write_file`, `fs.write_text` and `File.write` hand the filesystem provider at most 64 KiB per call and loop over its reported progress, so writing one large `bytes` or `str` costs time linear in its length. The resource layer copies whatever it is offered before each provider call and the scoped provider consumes at most 64 KiB of it, so offering the whole remaining tail copied O(n) bytes per 64 KiB written: 64 MiB took 14.6 s and now takes well under a second.
- [REQUIRED-PROJECT-IMPORT-POLICY-1] A `mylang.json` discovered by walking up from the entry source file to the filesystem root makes its `import_policy` required for ordinary `check`, `check-project`, `build`, `run`, `run-project`, project variants and native test preparation, evaluated before any cached success and never selected from the shell working directory. Missing or malformed configuration is a host failure (status 2); a policy violation is status 3 and language or import-cycle errors stay status 1. Policy and configuration identity participates in verdict caches and receipts so a stricter policy invalidates an earlier pass, import-cycle checking cannot be disabled, and projects without a configuration keep their prior behavior.
- [POLICY-IDENTITY-OWNED-1] The configured-policy identity that the successful-verdict caches key on is copied into memory each cache owns when it is published, and freed when the cache is dropped, so a warm `check-project` or `check-session` request under a `mylang.json` replays its stored verdict exactly as an unconfigured project does instead of keying on stale stack bytes. Every request first clears the identity, so a long-lived session whose project stops configuring one never keys on the previous request's configuration.
- [TESTS-FOR-1] `mylang tests-for <entry.my> <qualified-name>` loads and checks the project, then prints the discovered tests that reach that declaration: a `direct` list of tests whose body calls it directly, and a `reached-by` list of every test that reaches it transitively. It is a selection aid, not a verdict: an unknown qualified name prints a message and exits 2, and a declaration no test reaches prints empty lists and exits 0.
- [TESTS-FOR-SYNTAX-1] `mylang tests-for` on a project whose bodies do not type-check still answers, exit 0 with the diagnostics on stderr, from a call graph read from the merged syntax: each call resolves by name through the calling module's own declarations and its import aliases, following re-exports, and the tests are the checked discovery rule's. The answer is the checked answer's three lines followed by a `basis: syntax` line; a project whose syntax does not parse says so and exits 1.
- [REF-DIFF-1] The commit-time rules identify changed declarations from `git diff --unified=0 <reference>` run in the entry file's own repository: the changed working-tree line ranges come from the `+` side of each hunk header, and a declaration is changed when its source line range intersects one. A failed git invocation is a host failure, and the reference revision is never recompiled.
- [CHANGE-WITHOUT-TEST-1] Under `mylang lint --gate --against REF` only, every non-test function whose declaration changed since `REF` must be the direct callee of a test whose own body changed or was added in the same diff, be exempt under `test-coverage.exempt`, or carry a `change-without-test` waiver; otherwise it reports `L_CHANGE_WITHOUT_TEST` at error severity with a related note naming the tests that currently call it directly. A plain `lint --gate` without `--against`, and a baseline row under `.mylang/lint/`, never suppress it, because the rule judges a diff rather than the standing tree.
- [API-DIFF-1] `mylang api-diff <entry.my> --write=FILE` records an `api-diff-baseline-v1` snapshot of every declaration's stable identity and its interface and body fingerprints; `--against=FILE` classifies the current project against one snapshot as `additive` (a new identity), `compatible` (only the body fingerprint changed), `breaking` (the identity disappeared or its interface fingerprint changed) and `unchanged`, printing a one-line summary and the changed declarations by default or one `api-diff-v1` JSON object with `--json`. It is a report and never blocks: exactly one of `--write`/`--against` is required, and a malformed or unreadable baseline exits 2.
- [API-FILE-1] `mylang api` renders one interface file per project module (every loaded module except the embedded standard library), named like its source with `.api` for `.my`: the line `# generated by mylang api; do not edit`, `module NAME`, the first paragraph of the module docstring, then every public declaration in source order as a blank-line separated block in mylang declaration syntax without bodies: records, classes, unions, enums and results with their fields and cases, domains with their codec and their public members' signatures nested beneath them, resources, type aliases, named constraints, `const` declarations with their type and value, re-exports as `export module.Name`, and functions with their whole signature (type parameters and requirements, `mut` markers, the keyword-only `*`, defaults, the return type, any `noalloc`/`noio`/`effects(...)` clause as written) and the `ceiling(...)` `list-interfaces` publishes, preceded by their `@deprecated` and `@requires`/`@ensures`/`@invariant` clauses with whitespace folded to single spaces. Types are canonical: aliases expanded, capabilities in their Python spelling, this module's own declarations unqualified and every other one qualified by its full module name. The text reads only the declaration index, so an edit to a body, a private declaration, a comment, a declaration docstring or the layout leaves it byte-identical, and the same sources render the same bytes whatever the cache state.
- [API-FILE-2] `mylang api <entry.my> --write` writes every missing or stale interface file, leaves current ones untouched, and deletes an orphaned one: a `.api` file under the entry's root, outside hidden directories and `zig-out`, that carries the generated header, belongs to no loaded module and whose `.my` source no longer exists (a hand-written file, or one whose source exists but another entry loads, is never touched). `--check` writes nothing and exits 1 when any file is missing, stale or orphaned, printing each such module with a short unified diff from the checked-in file to the regenerated one (at most 40 lines per module) and the `--write` command to run, or exits 0 with a one-line summary. `--json` prints one `interface-files-v1` object whose `modules` list each out-of-date file with its `state` and the `added`, `removed` and `changed` declaration names (`Domain.member` for a domain member, `__doc__` for the module docstring). Exactly one of `--write`/`--check` is required (exit 2 otherwise), a declaration error exits 1 with its diagnostics, and a file that cannot be read, written or deleted exits 2.
- [API-FILE-3] A `mylang.json` carrying `"interface_files": true` makes the interface files part of checking the project: once its bodies check, `check`, `check-project`, `run`, `run-project` and a whole-program `emit-project-qbe` (the `build` frontend) report each missing or stale file as `E_INTERFACE_FILE_STALE` at `PATH:1:1`, naming the module and the `mylang api <entry> --write` to run, and exit 1. Every checked-in interface file's bytes are folded into the configuration identity the successful-verdict caches key on, so editing or deleting one never replays an earlier success. The value must be a JSON boolean; without the key, or with `false`, nothing changes. Navigation, listing, lint and native-test preparation do not enforce it.
- [LINT-2] A `mylang.json` version 2 may carry a `lint` object: `profile` (`agent` default, `strict`, `safety`), `on_build` (`report` default, `block`), `warnings` (`report` default, `deny`), positive integer `min_chars` (the `undocumented-export` docstring floor, 12 by default), `block`/`ratchet`/`report` arrays of rule names, and a `rules` object whose per-rule settings carry positive integer `max`, `hard_max` and `recover_pct`, reasoned `exempt` and `allow` entries, and `modules` globs. Unknown or duplicate keys, malformed values, a non-object `lint`, and an exemption without a name or a non-empty reason are located configuration failures (status 2); `lint` requires `version: 2`, and a version 1 document keeps parsing without it. A configured `lint` object participates in verdict cache identity exactly as `import_policy` does.
- [LINT-1] `mylang lint <entry.my|dir>` loads the project through the shared frontend, runs every enabled rule over the checked program, prints each finding as an `L_*` diagnostic on stderr and one summary line on stdout. It exits 0 when the language is valid and no finding blocks, 1 for a source, type, effect or ownership error with no rule evaluated, 2 for a usage or configuration failure, and 3 when a blocking finding exists. Without `--gate` a `block` rule is reported at warning severity and never changes the exit unless the project sets `on_build: "block"`; `--gate` raises each block finding to error severity and refuses. `--json` (or `--diagnostics=json`) renders findings as `diagnostic-v1` JSON Lines on stderr, and `--profile=agent|strict|safety` overrides the configured profile for one run. A `# lint: allow <rule> reason="..."` comment on the line above a declaration waives that rule for it; a waiver without a reason is `L_WAIVER_REASON` and always blocks. The rules are `untested-export` (report under `agent`, block under `strict` and `safety`), `undocumented-export` (report under `agent`, block under `strict` and `safety`; a missing, too-short or name-restating docstring on an exported function, record, union or domain), `dead-private` (a private function with no call site), and the test-hygiene rules `test-no-assertion` (a test body whose every `return` is the literal `true` and which never asserts), `test-conditional` (a branch or match, or more than one loop, at a test body top level) and `test-empty` (an empty test body), all blocking under every profile.
