- [RC-PUBLISH-FAST-1] Outside counting builds, the direct publication (semantic copy or take) of one list or dictionary pointer runs inline exactly as its generated glue does: a null pointer copies through; a payload that is neither pinned nor in an arena is marked shared unless it already is or is immortal, its pointer is stored, and then either the source is cleared (a take) or one owner is acquired (an immortal payload counts none). Its destination is always private work or aggregate scratch distinct from the source. A child-history mode, a pinned or arena payload and every other type still call the glue.
- [RC-NATIVE-VARIANT-1] Outside RC counting/site builds, take and drop/unloan of a counted, copyable tagged variant of at most 32 bytes with at most eight declared cases/physical fields may inline at most four declared active cases whose physical payload has no counted edge or unique-drop obligation. Take admits only disjoint complete source/destination ranges, established by its dynamic overlap guard or the unchanged RC-DISJOINT-TAKE-1 completed-publication certificate, copies every value byte (including inactive payload and padding), then clears the complete source; drop/unloan clears the complete value without acquiring/releasing tokens or draining, exactly as that active case of generated glue. Managed, unknown or malformed tags and overlapping takes retain the complete generated helper and its existing status/failure mapping before caller ownership guards change. Niche/outer-domain/unique-resource/unsupported layouts and GC remain unchanged; publications and their history protocol are not changed. Before any take store, the requested unsigned-word region is compared with the current unsigned-word region depth through the published native-heap ABI; an inadmissible region reaches the original generated helper, including its checked invariant failure. The guard uses the helper argument width, and no inactive managed payload is inspected.

- [RC-FLAT-DROP-1] Outside counting builds, dropping a local's previous value at a store or its value at an explicit release, when it is a plain inline record of at most 64 bytes (nested plain records allowed; no variant, array, live view, class, Cell or unique resource) whose counted leaves are at most four one-word owner pointers emits one inline owner release per leaf, whose last-token slow path calls that leaf type's own drop glue (which queues the death and drains), and then clears the whole value, instead of calling the record's glue. Operand consumption and return exits, whose drops rarely run or run once, keep the glue call. Releases, deaths and counts are the glue's, leaf by leaf.
- [RC-INLINE-ZERO-1] RC ownership lowering clears a small fixed-size range — a moved-from value after an inline take or a closed field transfer, and a fresh owning aggregate before construction — with inline stores instead of a `memset` call when at most eight stores cover it; each store is no wider than the range's alignment (at most 8 bytes), so it is aligned for both backends. Larger ranges keep the `memset` call. The cleared bytes are the same.
- [RC-ALLOC-CACHE-1] The production RC allocator keeps one singly linked list of free slab slots per size class in the heap (`myl_native_heap.rc_cache`, at a frozen offset). Retiring a slab-sized object marks its slot free and pushes it on its class's list, with no slab bookkeeping; the drain and every other runtime retirement of the production allocator push inline. Every runtime allocation of a slab-sized extent pops its class's list, and an empty list refills from one slab at a time: the slab's whole free list, or a run of at most 4 KiB of bump slots threaded in address order and marked free. Generated code constructing a class body in a production (non-counting) RC artifact pops the list inline, writes the counted prefix (one owner, no hold, a one-element mark and the body descriptor), zeroes the payload and counts the object in `objects_live`; only an empty list, or an open arena in a program that opens one, calls `myl_native_heap_alloc`, which refills the list or allocates in the arena. Counting builds keep the runtime call, so their allocate and retire events stay exact. Listed slots stay charged to the slabs that hold them: a budget refusal, a fresh pool reservation while at least 1 MiB and a quarter of the reservation was retired since the last return, and heap teardown or release give every listed slot back to its slab and release empty slabs, so empty slabs are still reused across classes and budget, `objects_live`, visits and teardown are unchanged. `heap->allocations` counts runtime allocations only.
- [RC-LARGE-MAP-1] The production RC allocator gives every large extent of at least 128 KiB (its record included) an anonymous mapping of its own, zero on arrival, and unmaps it as soon as the extent is released, so a freed large block never stays resident; smaller large extents keep coming from `calloc`. A growing large backing moves from a `calloc` block into a mapping when it reaches 128 KiB and a mapping grows in place or moves with `mremap`; a mapping's new tail is already zero, so only a `calloc` block's growth is cleared. The budget charges, `objects_live`, visits and teardown are unchanged.
- [RC-DRAIN-FLAT-1] The RC drain handles a dying object whose admitted flat plan has no leaf (at any element count), or a single element with at most 16 leaves, without a callback or a walk: each live leaf's owner count is released and a last token queues its object directly on the drain's own list, and the dying object's edge words are left as they are because its extent is retired at once and never read again. A call-free loop handles every dying slab-sized object of the production allocator whose type's plan is unconditional (or has no leaf): one mark test admits it (dying, counted, not large, and a single element unless the plan has no leaf), its leaves are released and its slot is pushed on its class's free list inline; the previous death's type answers a repeated type without a probe. Every other object is handled on its own with the drain's list and counters published first. The drain's `objects_live`, reuse-epoch and listed-byte updates are applied before any finalizer, edge hook or generic walk runs and when the drain ends, so a callback that allocates always sees a bumped reuse epoch. Invariants, statistics and the order of deaths are unchanged.
- [RC-DRAIN-PAIR-1] In the drain's call-free loop, a dying object whose type's admitted plan has exactly two unconditional leaves (a binary node, say) starts a run of that type's consecutive deaths in a function of its own, which keeps the run's few values in registers: each death's two leaves are released at the plan's two offsets, in the plan's order, without walking its live set, and its slot is pushed on the one class list the whole run shares (a one-element slab object's class follows from its type's size alone), whose head is written back once and whose listed bytes are counted once. The run ends at an empty list, a death of another type or one the constant one-element fast test refuses, which stays queued. Deaths of every other admitted type also run per type, so a run's type and fast test are compared once per death. Counts, statistics, the class lists, listed bytes and the order of deaths are unchanged.
- [RC-DRAIN-TYPES-1] The RC drain remembers its two most recent death types with their flat plans in the heap's RC state, across drains, most recent first: a death whose type is either one needs no binding probe (bindings are never replaced), in the call-free loop or in the one-object path, and a type found second moves first; any other type is probed once and becomes the most recent. The one-object path probes again only after a close hook ran. A dying list header whose elements own nothing (RC-PLAIN-TYPE-1) releases only its backing, with no element descriptor lookup. Deaths, counts and statistics are unchanged.
- [RC-LIST-FLAT-RELEASE-1] When an RC list dies or is cleared, elements the shape walker would visit one by one (a nested shape that is neither a bare pointer nor a shallow record) and whose type admits a flat leaf plan (only owner pointers, at most 16 leaves, no hook; `RC-SEMANTIC-FLAT-1`) release their live leaves through that plan, each edge cleared and its owner count dropped, exactly as the walker releases them, without walking the element's shape once per element; pointer and shallow elements keep their direct loops, and any other element type keeps the walker. Counts, retirement order within an element and statistics are unchanged.
- [RC-PLAIN-TYPE-1] The RC runtime keeps the last type it proved owns nothing (an admitted flat plan with no leaf and no native unique obligation; bindings never change) and answers a repeated query without a metadata probe. A list append that grows the list, a clone of a list, a keyed sorted copy (which gathers straight into its result as the collector's does), and an edge-free death outside a drain copy, clone, gather and retire such elements as bytes: the clone copies the element storage at once and the append copies the value, exactly what retaining a value that owns nothing does, with no count or statistics event.
- [RC-LIST-HEADER-1] The RC runtime registers its list header module once per heap and remembers it, allocates a list or storage header through the production storage adapter's cached single-type path (the generic request path for an arena region or another allocator), and skips the element type's registration probe when the type is the one last proved plain (RC-PLAIN-TYPE-1), which is registered. A list of plain elements whose growth moves its backing leaves the new capacity past the copied live prefix unwritten, as RC-OPAQUE-EXTEND-1's in-place growth does. Counts, statistics, budget and failures are unchanged.
- [RC-DICT-FAST-1] RC dictionaries take common ownership steps without the generic transfer walker: inserting an ordinary pointer key (a `str`, list or other counted pointer) with a value that owns nothing acquires one owner for the key and copies the value's bytes; a key built from a described slice carries the hash the lookup walk computed; tearing a dictionary down looks the key and value bindings up once and releases each live entry's leaves through their flat plans (the walker only for a plan the flat form cannot admit); and releasing a run of ordinary pointers clears and releases each in turn. An arena key, a value that owns anything and every refusal keep the generic path. Counts, statistics, order of deaths and failures are unchanged.
- [RC-RANGE-KEY-1] A counted (RC) artifact lowers DICT-RANGE-KEY-1's allocation-free dictionary keys as the collector does: a `str` slice whose value is only a `str`-keyed dictionary lookup, `get`, `contains`, index or write key (directly, or through a range local) describes the slice's span beside its operand instead of building a text. Its operand holds the sliced text itself as a borrowed value: the slice's result takes one hold on that text (an RC loan) instead of owning a fresh copy, so the text outlives every read of the key and the hold is returned when the operand or range local is dropped. The dictionary reads, compares and hashes the octets in place; a write of an absent key stores one owned copy of the octets (`myl_native_dictionary_write_range`), and a write of a present key replaces only the value. Answers, traps, stored keys and the program's output are unchanged; a program that opens an arena keeps building the slices.
- [RC-ANCHORED-RANGE-KEY-1] A described `str` slice (RC-RANGE-KEY-1) whose receiver is anchored by RC-READONLY-ANCHOR-1 is itself anchored: the slice publishes no hold, and a range local or compiler temporary every definition of which stores such a slice (or the receiver itself, for the slice's receiver temporary, which a slice only reads) is bound as borrowed from the parameter's storage, so `key = seq[i:i + k]` over a never-written read-only `seq` stores the text's bits with no retain, and its replacement drops nothing. A dictionary write tail keyed by such a local keeps its certificate, and the write still stores one owned copy of an absent key's octets. A parameter the function writes, a slice built into a real text, and every other consumer keep their ordinary ownership.
- [RC-NAMED-RELEASE-1] A named local of a collection type that the program only reads and rebinds whole (its own owning slot: no move alias shares it, no loop iterates it, and no copy-on-write site, place, alias or loop entry names it) is released on the first straight-line store edge with an empty operand stack after its last use, as a finished collection temporary is, instead of when its next value is stored or the frame exits: `counts = frequencies(seq, k)` in a loop frees the previous dictionary before the next one is built. A local some borrowed or anchored value still reads, or a receiver chain still needs, stays owned until those uses end; answers, counts and traps are unchanged.
- [RC-BUFFER-APPEND-1] A counted (RC) artifact lowers a `byte_buffer` `append_range` or `append_byte` with the same inline capacity-sufficient path as QBE-BUFFER-APPEND-1: the three argument refusals, one room compare, one octet copy or store and one length bump, with only a refused request, an out-of-range octet or a full receiver reaching the runtime helper. The receiver load follows the operation's uniqueness check, buffer storage is never shared between two buffer headers (a counted clone copies the octets), and a snapshot leaves its buffer no spare capacity, so the room test alone keeps every inline write private to the receiver. No root is published on a counted heap, and results, refusals and the allocation trap are unchanged.
- [RC-INERT-RETURN-1] A bounded pure entry condition over readonly integer/boolean parameters may return an inline value whose bytes and managed edges are statically zero before initializing its RC journal. Native stack/frame admission remains; step-accounted builds, heap frames, entry adapters, incoming ownership, calls, scopes, allocation, sharing/history, loans, error provenance and unsupported instructions retain ordinary lowering. The nonempty branch keeps the complete original ownership protocol.

- [RC-MUT-UNIQUE-1] A scalar-element list parameter with the mutable-payload ABI may use the caller's established uniqueness throughout its activation when no bytecode instruction rebinds, moves, drops or exposes the parameter slot by reference. The caller pins the payload, so snapshot publication copies instead of adding another owner. Rebound parameters and other layouts retain ordinary runtime uniqueness checks.

- [RC-PHYSICAL-HOIST-1] RC uniqueness may move to a semantic loop entry only after finalized physical loans prove a closed scalar-only body with no lasting managed publication; root storage and existing hidden liveness remain unchanged, and nested list elements detach only after their owning outer list.
- [RC-BUFFER-HOIST-1] The same physical loop proof admits a named `byte_buffer` root whose loop body writes it only through its in-place methods (`append_byte`, `append_int`, `append_float`, `reserve`, and `append_range`/`append_utf8` whose data or text operand is exactly one `load_local` of another local carried unconsumed to the call): its uniqueness check runs once at the loop entry, as the collector's does, instead of before every append. Any other use of the buffer, a store of a counted value, a call, a snapshot or a branch into the operand window keeps the check at every append. Appended octets, refusals, the allocation trap and every snapshot taken before the loop are unchanged.

- [RC-PRODUCED-OWNER-1] An immediately preceding unconditional ordinary-owner producer may skip its operand initialization test. Branch entries, held mappings, borrowed entries and other producers keep guarded transfers; pin, arena and failure behavior stays on the existing typed TAKE path.
- [RC-DISJOINT-TAKE-1] The completed ordinary-owner publication proof also establishes that scalar operand bits or stable aggregate operand storage occupy a distinct range from pending work storage. Its bounded inline TAKE may omit a dynamic overlap test; all pointer/header checks and the fallible pin/arena fallback remain. Transfers without this physical-storage proof retain their overlap test.

- [MUT-REBIND-CALLER-1] A whole-variable rebinding of a `mut` list, dictionary or set parameter reaches the caller, including a rebinding in a loop, on some paths only, or through recursive forwarding. Writes before and after the rebinding reach that variable; read-only sources and named snapshots remain independent. The old core selects caller-storage ABI per parameter when the body can rebind it, transitively through mut calls, while never-rebound direct parameters keep the mutable-payload fast path (RC-MUT-UNIQUE-1). Mut collection callback calls use caller storage because their type records writable positions without a body-specific rebinding layout; adapters preserve each target's canonical direct-call ABI, including separately compiled imports. The ABI flag survives lowered signature serialization and module interfaces and participates in signature conflict detection and native fact identity. The backend conservatively selects storage beyond its compact mask; the existing origin analysis supports only sixteen tracked parameter positions. Fixed-array rebinding remains E_ASSIGNMENT_TARGET (PY-ARRAY-MUT-1).

- [RC-STABLE-JOURNAL-1] Operand and pending RC slots keep their frame storage and guard words across typed publications. Only the role word changes before initialization, so reused stack positions and held/ordinary transitions drop their current values by the static type of the stack position at the drop.


- [RC-PLAIN-LIST-WRITE-1] RC writes of nonzero scalar list elements with no counted or unique edges use the native typed store after unchanged index normalization and bounds checks; managed and unique elements retain fallible ownership publication helpers.

- [RC-INERT-PROJECTION-1] An immediate list-element read into a proved inert mutation receiver borrows from the live physical parent until fill detaches and refreshes it; named snapshots, callbacks, alternate fill entries and unproved paths retain ordinary ownership or scoped protection.
- [RC-INERT-FIELD-RECEIVER-1] A list receiver filled straight from one field of a named plain record (`record.items[i] = value`, `record.items[i].field = value`) is an inert receiver path whose root is that record: the field read lends the list without a loan, the fill keeps its uniqueness check, and the proved interval needs no receiver scope, pin or backing hold. The interval may also read plain fields of any plain record in place (`load_local record; get_field` with a plain result). The record root must be a named local or parameter that is not a class, collection, place or alias, is live through the mutation and is not stored in the interval; element types, stored values and every other interval rule of `RC-INERT-RECEIVER-1` stay unchanged, and calls, callbacks, managed results and unproved paths keep the scoped protocol.
- [RC-INERT-BYTES-RECEIVER-1] A `byte_buffer` receiver filled straight from one field of a named plain record (`record.buffer.append_byte(b)`, `record.buffer.append_range(data, start, end)`, `append_utf8`, `append_int`) is an inert receiver path under the rules of `RC-INERT-FIELD-RECEIVER-1`: the field read lends the buffer without a loan, the fill keeps its uniqueness check, and the interval needs no receiver scope, pin or backing hold. A byte buffer is only ever the path's own leaf. Its interval may also load an immutable byte string or text parameter the function never writes and read its length, and the append may consume such a value: the caller keeps it alive, nothing in the interval can release it, and the append only reads it. Any other managed operand, a call, a callback or a byte buffer inside a list keeps the scoped protocol.

- [RC-FRAME-REGION-1] RC functions without arena boundary instructions reuse their entry region depth for typed transfers. Functions that open or close arenas read the current depth at each transfer; callees restore their own scopes before returning.

- [RC-LOOP-VERSION-1] RC loop versions cache element storage only inside a proved physical no-publication interval, after entry uniqueness and with no residual COW checks or allocating lowered helpers. Failed range guards execute the original loop and preserve its traps.

- [RC-SCALAR-LOOP-VERSION-1] RC integer-only loops may reuse the existing guarded arithmetic version when bounded inspection proves that every touched value is an integer, Boolean or unit and no explicit or implicit ownership action occurs in the interval. Managed values, callbacks, region transitions, by-reference locals and uncertain paths retain their previous admission rules. The original checked loop remains the fallback, and step-accounted builds keep one original copy.

- [RC-FILE-OPEN-TRAP-1] A trapping file open releases the provider's unpublished owned error text before reporting R_FILE, including inside an arena. Fallible open publishes that text in Result.Err; successful handles retain their unique resource cleanup.

- [RC-NO-TRACING-ROOTS-1] Counted native functions retain guarded ownership slots and arena/callback scope cleanup without publishing tracing-GC roots; GC artifacts retain their existing traced root chains.


- [RC-SEMANTIC-HISTORY-1] Experimental RC separates semantic sharing history from physical lifetime. A frame-private loan copies inline bytes and holds every managed edge without cloning pinned payloads, changing ordinary owner counts or setting sharing marks. Failed acquisition clears its output and rolls back all holds; cleanup clears the loan before allocation-free release. Raw unique resources and uniquely finalized boxed bodies cannot become loans. Loans never enter ordinary heap or provider ownership.

- [RC-COMPACT-JOURNAL-1] RC frames omit guard and role slots only for locals with no counted or unique obligation and operand positions whose complete reachable physical type union is plain. Retained positions keep their original reverse cleanup order, both construction scratch slots, dynamic held roles and exact arena local ranges. Omitted guard requests fail internally; plain aggregate staging copies bits without inspecting an absent ownership guard.

- [MUT-EXPRESSION-STACK-1] A transparent `mut` argument marker consumes exactly one checked target expression, regardless of whether that target lowers to an indexed read, a field or a synthesized block. Nested dictionary mutation arguments compose with later call arguments without checker stack underflow, while missing-marker and nonassignable-target diagnostics remain unchanged.

- [RECURSIVE-FILE-CONTRACT-1] Recursive immutable box admissibility detects builtin `File` and `TextFile` resource leaves through records, variants and nonempty fixed arrays, including declaration-order and generic wrappers, and rejects them with the existing `E_RECURSIVE_RECORD` rule before execution. Zero-length resource arrays contain no obligation; ordinary scoped file handling and acyclic resource/Result transfers keep their existing contracts.

- [COW-BOX-REBOX-ROOTS-1] Reboxing a narrowed recursive value after a nested collection write is a GC safepoint independent of the preceding child-copy safepoint. The compiler republishes every live frame root around that allocation, including older snapshots no longer reachable through the latest box owner; repeated standalone writes preserve those snapshots under collection stress.

- [RC-HISTORY-PUBLISH-1] Experimental RC semantic COPY and TAKE apply sharing history at explicit publication events. Reused ordinary mutable leaves commit SHARED only after the entire transfer succeeds; fresh pinned snapshot shells stay unmarked and preserve the older source region. Semantic detachment shares reused children, while lifetime-only detachment does not. Children and source-children modes preserve the source and mark its direct mutable children, replacing protected paths only in the output. Count, metadata and allocation refusal leave source values, tokens and history unchanged. Separate pinned mutable occurrences produce independent snapshot identities; whole-view H edges preserve identity without semantic marks.


- [RC-SEMANTIC-LOWERING-1] RC keeps copyable borrowed projections and compiler captures in private lifetime loans, then commits semantic copy history at the GC copy decision before later evaluation. Ordinary ABI and heap publications never expose a loan token.

- [RC-PHYSICAL-RETAIN-1] Physical helper ownership publication preserves pointer identity and acquires direct O/H edges without semantic marks or pin snapshots; count and region refusal rolls back the whole batch.

- [RC-BOX-WRITEBACK-1] A counted mutation of a collection field materialized from a boxed Option payload republishes the updated body into the owning Option slot before the receiver is used. The replacement preserves the original allocation region, retains child identity without adding semantic sharing or pin clones, retires only a locally owned prior box, and keeps earlier snapshots unchanged.



- [RC-FLAT-SINGLE-DESTRUCTION-1] A dying heap allocation with one element may traverse its prevalidated bounded ordinary-edge plan without the repeated-element extent loop. It preserves descriptor edge order, clears each edge before release, defers callbacks until parent traversal completes, retains O/H death and arena/immortal checks, and leaves custom finalizers, H shapes and zero/multiple elements on their existing paths.
- [RC-DRAIN-CACHE-1] An iterative RC drain may reuse the previous death's binding for the same native type. A published binding is never replaced, so the reuse needs no batch check; generic callback paths still reacquire descriptors after finalizers. Metadata registration from retirement, mixed layouts, O/H death, edge order and nonallocating bounded-stack destruction preserve their existing behavior.
- [RC-FLAT-VARIANT-1] An RC flat plan admits variant arms as conditional leaves: a leaf beneath a variant is live only while the variant's u32 tag equals its arm, exactly as the generic walker visits one arm. Plans hold up to 16 leaves in walker order; simultaneously live leaves never overlap one another or any tag word, and a live variant nested inside another arm keeps the generic walker. When every conditional leaf tests one tag word with a tag below 32, a per-arm live set is precomputed. Loans, unloans, retains, copies, takes, replacements, drops and drain read the tag before changing any edge and touch only live leaves, preserving the byte copy of inactive arms and whole-value clearing.
- [RC-METADATA-MAP-1] Registered RC metadata is found through three budget-charged pointer maps (modules, shape identities including persistent sources, and native-type bindings, which RC-TYPE-ID-1 indexes by type id where a type carries one), each at most half full with a multiplicative home slot and a shared empty sentinel, so a lookup is one inline probe with no kind comparison. The maps grow in a staged transaction that commits with the batch and reserved frames; refusal leaves every map, charge and binding unchanged.
- [RC-TYPE-ID-1] Every native type descriptor the compiler generates carries a small dense `rc_id`: the runtime's own types (raw bytes, list header and storage, bytes and text views, dictionary, and the one shared pointer, byte and word leaf descriptors every runtime source uses for list elements and byte or slot storage) hold the fixed ids 1 through 9, and a linked program's generated types are numbered from 16 in the order of the program-wide descriptor catalog, so a lookup indexes a table by id and confirms the slot by the descriptor's address instead of hashing that address. The table is sized by the ids its registrations claim and never by a stray id: a binding takes its type's id slot only when the id is nonzero, greater than the previous id claimed in the same registration, within eight plus four times the descriptors registered so far, and unclaimed; every other binding (a type with no id, a repeated or out-of-order id, an id a separately compiled module numbered the same, an outlier) lives in the hashed map exactly as before, and a lookup that misses its slot falls back to the hashed map, so no type ever finds another type's binding and a type nobody registered finds none. The inlined probe is the indexed load and one compare; every other case (a pending slot, a hashed binding, a miss) is one out-of-line call that preserves the caller's registers under clang, so inlining the probe never gives its caller a frame. Registration stages the id table with the other maps, so a refusal leaves table, charge and bindings unchanged. Deaths, counts and statistics are unchanged.
- [RC-STARTUP-3] A binding placed in an id slot (RC-TYPE-ID-1) waits with its flat plan unbuilt and builds it on the first lookup of that type, which also marks the slot active; a program registers every descriptor it links but pays the plan walk only for the types it reaches. A hashed binding builds its plan when registered. Plans, deaths, counts and statistics are the ones an eager build gives.
- [RC-CLASS-TABLE-1] The production RC allocator maps a payload size to its slab class through a constant table, entry n the least class whose stride holds n * 16 bytes, so heap start builds nothing and no heap carries a copy. A checked runtime verifies the table against the class search once per process.
- [RC-LEAF-RETIRE-1] A dying RC heap object whose admitted flat plan has no leaf (no edge, hook or finalizer) retires immediately when no drain is active; inside an active drain or finalizer reentry it is queued behind its siblings as before and the drain retires it without a walk. Retirement statistics count each such object once.
- [RC-LOCAL-LIST-1] A bounded closed scalar activation may keep its one nonconstant fixed list of at most sixteen integer or boolean elements in counted native frame scratch. It has exactly one constructor, no incoming managed edges, result edges, callbacks, mutation, arena transitions, receiver captures or backedges. Admission requires registered edge-free element metadata, an idle ordinary production RC heap with no unrelated live objects, scopes, resources or registered cleanup callbacks, and already charged header/backing cache slots for that simultaneous peak. Refusal changes no state and executes the original located constructor. Admission settles the same cached allocation/retirement tickets, preserving allocation totals, reuse epoch, trim accumulation and cache order, before publishing the private stack value; no later operation in the activation can allocate, and scalar reads retain original bounds, overflow, instruction and frame checks. Unit element, counting/site, GC, custom allocator and heap-frame paths keep ordinary construction.
- [RC-STORAGE-TEARDOWN-1] The production RC allocator marks every retired slab slot, so `myl_rc_storage_visit` enumerates each allocated, unretired heap payload (live or queued for death) exactly once without a side table, and `myl_rc_storage_release_all` frees every slab and large extent at once without walking edges or running finalizers, discharges their budget, drops the dead queue and subtracts them from the live count, leaving registered metadata and a usable empty heap.
- [RC-SLAB-POOLS-1] The production RC allocator carves its 64 KiB slabs from 2 MiB anonymous mappings instead of the C heap, so peak residency depends only on slab use and not on malloc's layout. A freed slab's range waits on a spare list for reuse; beyond sixteen spares its pages are returned to the host. Slab budget charges, retained-empty-slab trimming and refusal are unchanged, the mapping registry lives in the charged storage record (a charged overflow array past 64 mappings), and heap destruction unmaps every pool.
- [RC-SCOPE-SPARE-1] RC scope journal nodes released by a scope wait on a bounded 32-node spare list that is not charged to the heap, and the next pin or hold takes one from there before the metadata allocator. Reusing a spare node repeats the metadata budget admission and charge of a fresh allocation and releasing one discharges it, so the charge follows live entries and accounting and refusal match allocation exactly; heap destruction frees the spare nodes, and a trap's abandoned scopes (RC-TRAP-TEARDOWN-1) return their nodes to the same list. A short receiver path prepares in local frames instead of charged metadata.
- [RC-SCOPE-FAST-1] The receiver protocol's public runtime entries — scope begin, pin (and hold) with collection backing, and scope end — are forced inline, with only a fresh journal node, a full spare list, a death, a refusal and a journal of more than one entry out of line, so a full-LTO build carries the straight-line fast path into guest code and a QBE build pays one call per entry. The runtime's constant-time checks and count tests they use are forced inline too. A pinned or held list records its storage block's hold in the same journal entry as the list, acquired right after the list's own and released right before it, instead of in an entry of its own: the counts, pin bits, statistics events and failure rollback are those of two entries, one node is charged instead of two, and a dictionary keeps one entry per backing block.
- [RC-CHECKED-1] RC runtime invariants that need a metadata lookup or a value walk (a loaned or retained payload's own finalizer, the native unique-resource walk on semantic publication, registration of a uniqueness slot shape, the module registration checks that scan or look up earlier descriptors (each one quadratic in the module), a registered allocation element and transfer frame shapes) run in every build except a production `--rc-runtime` artifact: the counting `--rc-runtime=stats` runtime, the GC artifact's copy of the RC library and the standalone C runtime tests keep them. Every build keeps the constant-time invariants.
- [RC-SITES-1] `--rc-runtime=sites` builds a counting RC artifact (`rc-v3-sites`) that also attributes every physical ownership event to the emitted call that caused it. Before each direct call into the runtime or into generated glue, a lowered function bumps that site's call count and makes the site current; every counted event (owner and hold acquire and release, count limits, allocation, retire, clone, uniqueness check) and the sites-only journal events (receiver scope begin, end, hold, pin and prepare; frame enter and leave; slot drop; a uniqueness check that is not answered in place) land in the current site as well as the program totals, so a site's counts sum to `MYLANG_RC_COUNTS`. A site is its function, instruction pc (or the prologue `entry` / shared `exit`), source position, callee (a type's glue operation reads `rcg_OP`) and the static type its descriptor names. At exit the runtime prints a `MYLANG_RC_SITES` summary line and the top `MYLANG_RC_SITES_TOP` (default 20) sites by events to stderr and writes every site that ran as JSON to the file `MYLANG_RC_SITES` names. Guest code, `--rc-runtime` and `--rc-runtime=stats` artifacts are unchanged.
- [RC-UNIQUE-IN-PLACE-1] The native uniqueness bridges return before building a slot, region or edit when the addressed payload is already unique, which both the owned and the borrowed protocol leave in place; every call still records exactly one uniqueness check.
- [RC-SHALLOW-WALK-1] A registered RC shape of ownership depth at most two (a pointer, or a record, variant or array whose direct children are pointers or pointer-free) is seeded, released, unloaned, region-scanned and visited by transfers without walker frames, in the walker's leaf order and reading a variant tag when its field is reached. Releases through this path only enqueue; callers drain as before. Every clone shell and the generic body copy seed their values, so iterative cloning visits each yielded pair without seeding it again.
- [RC-LIST-DIRECT-APPEND-1] An RC list append with an empty death queue publishes its retained value straight into the reserved slot past the live prefix: a borrowed input inside the live prefix is re-addressed after growth, an input inside the spare capacity keeps the staged path, and a refused retain leaves the length unchanged. A pending death keeps the staged path, whose publication may drain callbacks.
- [RC-RAW-BLOCK-1] Slab-sized RC raw byte storage bypasses the generic allocation hook with identical accounting and statistics. Raw storage stays zero-filled, while a GC-style block (text payloads) leaves its payload for the caller to write before any read, as GC-NOZERO-1; arena and large requests keep their existing paths.
- [RC-SEMANTIC-POINTER-1] Direct (policy 1) semantic publication of one ordinary pointer marks a reused mutable payload SHARED only after its owner acquisition succeeds, without a clone transaction or metadata, so it neither allocates nor refuses at the memory budget; pinned and younger-region payloads keep the general transaction, and a refused acquisition commits no history.
- [RC-CLONE-SPAN-1] An RC clone descriptor may provide a contiguous `span` form of its iterative `next` protocol: every remaining seeded pair as one run of a single shape at that shape's size, advancing the same cursor. Lists provide it, so a clone fills all elements in one loop (frame-free for shallow element shapes) before the children that pinned elements scheduled; results, memoized snapshots, history marks, statistics and refusal rollback match the per-pair protocol.
- [RC-SEMANTIC-FLAT-1] Direct (policy 1) semantic publication of an admitted flat aggregate preflights every live leaf (pins and younger-region payloads keep the general transaction; owner limits refuse before any change), then acquires ordinary owners and marks each reused mutable payload SHARED, without a clone transaction or metadata. Arena and immortal leaves follow the general transaction's rules, TAKE clears the source, and a refusal commits no history and leaves the output empty.
- [RC-FLAT-TAKE-1] A physical RC TAKE of an admitted flat value into a distinct output publishes straight into that output when no death is queued: the flat transfer refuses before writing anything or completes without draining. A queued death, an aliased slot or a refused plan keeps the staged path.

- [RC-PLACE-ROLE-1] RC receiver preparation preserves a private inline capture's loan role: its managed leaves are held references, while fields reached after dereferencing a heap body resume ordinary ownership. Scoped and immediate detachment replace the held leaf only after the replacement hold succeeds; immutable boxes retain transactional child sharing. Unshared mutable identity, snapshot independence, iteration checks and refusal rollback remain unchanged.

- [RC-FRESH-CONSTRUCTOR-1] A bounded scalar-input constructor proof may relocate a complete ordinary-owned operand directly into its already guarded partial body only when every managed producer is physically linear and fresh. Aliases, copies including immutable-box copies, loans, holds, unique resources, captures, uncertified calls, mixed managed branch provenance and arena transitions retain ordinary typed preparation. Dynamic owner-role refusal, allocation failure cleanup and native frame admission remain intact.

- [RC-PLAIN-AGGREGATE-1] A nonzero inline aggregate with no counted or unique edges may copy directly into a private update scratch and use the existing checked list-element store. This preserves source snapshots, overlap rules, callback evaluation order, bounds details and COW scopes; counted, held, unique and zero-sized values retain their ownership preparation paths.

- [RC-EMPTY-LOCAL-JOURNAL-1] A bounded emission proof may omit a compiler local's RC journal only when its anchored read or inert mutation capture never installs an owning, held, loan or unique token. Rebinding, reference parameters, pattern payloads, uncertain clone destinations and H-capable receivers retain entries. Only certified locals synthesize empty role reads; unexpected publication remains an internal error. Operand and transactional entries, trap linkage, physical anchors and arena cleanup order remain intact.

- [RC-FRESH-RETURN-1] A bounded closed scalar-input constructor may hand its sole ordinary-owned result to the unchanged return ABI and omit only the provably empty normal journal cleanup. Return bytes are materialized before source guard handoff; aliasing, managed locals, loans, unique resources, unknown calls and arena transitions retain the generic return path. Trap journals, frame and step accounting, physical stack admission and caller arena lifetime remain intact.

- [RC-EMPTY-RECEIVER-STORAGE-1] A physically empty compiler-local certificate may also omit that receiver's lifetime-scope scratch when its anchored read bypasses scope setup or its inert mutation bypasses both setup and cleanup. Omitted scopes never link into runtime cleanup; any later request for their address remains an internal error. Uncertain aliases, callbacks and mutable arguments retain scope storage, while frame sizing counts only actual allocations and preserves admission and trap linkage.

- [RC-SCALAR-REPLACE-1] Replacing a nonzero scalar field with no counted or unique obligation may load its complete canonical value before storing directly, leaving pending ownership clear. Managed, held, unique, aggregate and zero-sized values retain their transactional preparation; receiver COW, bounds, callback ordering and scalar width/domain representation are unchanged.

- [RC-EMPTY-FRAME-1] A bounded physical certificate may omit only ownership-frame linkage for a stable caller-pinned plain-element list body with no local publication, scope, callback, arena transition or hidden owner; private storage, native/logical/step admission and caller scopes remain. Unexpected publication or scope creation is an internal compiler error.
- [QBE-POSITIVE-INDEX-1] Without step accounting, an exact canonical negative-index normalizer followed by a plain list read may receive a bounds-tested positive fast path. The captured receiver and index have already been evaluated; a successful unsigned index-below-length check reaches the same payload read, while failure executes the unchanged normalization, casts, overflow checks and original bounds trap. The certificate refuses interior control-flow entries from jumps, conditional jumps, Result error propagation and variant switches; only the normalizer's own conditional edge may enter its final index-load join. It also refuses receiver scopes, COW work, hidden releases, managed or live-view elements and element strides below two bytes. Native list capacity times stride must fit size_t, so admitted lengths are below 2^63 and the unsigned guard proves a nonnegative signed index. Step-counted builds preserve every original instruction.

- [RC-FRESH-SPAN-1] A bounded closed fresh constructor may preflight all ordinary source roles before allocation, keep those source guards live across refusal, and then perform only infallible byte moves and exact guard handoffs until complete result publication. Every failed proof or role check keeps the original partial-construction journal path; unique, held, loaned, aliased, callback and unusual-ABI cases retain their existing cleanup and transfer behavior.

- [RC-CLOSED-FIELD-TRANSFER-1] RC may physically move a list field from writable caller-owned inline record storage only with an explicit closed extraction/mutation/storeback certificate: the source cannot be observed while empty, the ordinary journal owns the token through traps, successful storeback restores the field, and existing snapshot, pin, semantic sharing, arena and unique-resource obligations remain authoritative.

- [RC-CLOSED-FIELD-TRANSFER-2] The closed field transfer also moves a counted field of any inline record, list or dictionary type (sealed capabilities allowed) out of a frame-owned local's own slot as well as writable caller storage, across a closed interval that may branch, loop and rejoin at the store-back and may call guest functions with any operands independent of the source. The certificate refuses a second writable parameter, a live view anywhere in the frame, a pending receiver scope, a value that may hold, borrow or name the source (other than plain bits) live at extraction or used in the interval, a read of the moved field or of the whole source (a distinct sibling field may be read), a return, `?` exit, arena transition, indirect or host call before the store-back, and a target that survives it. The emptied field is zero bytes, every RC value's empty cleanup form. When the target has its own frame guard the extraction relocates the field straight into the target's slot (releasing an initialized previous value first) and the store-back is a plain relocation back into the still-empty field that clears the target's guard; the ordinary copy, target store, store-back temporaries and record update emit nothing, and no copy-on-write site, fill, share or loop entry may be planned on those instructions. GC lowering is unchanged.
- [RC-DEAD-FIELD-MOVE-1] `t = s.f`, where value semantics already proved the copy a move (`VALUE-SEMANTICS-2`) and `s` is a frame-owned inline record local (not a parameter, reference, caller storage, element borrow or copy-on-write place) that no later instruction reads before overwriting it, moves the counted field's tokens into `t` instead of copying them, so a later mutation of `t` finds one owner instead of cloning. The source keeps its other fields, and its later drop finds the moved field's zero bytes. The certificate refuses a live view anywhere in the frame, a pending receiver scope, a `mut` reference to the source anywhere in the function, and any other local that may hold, borrow or name the source and is live at the extraction. The move happens only when the source's guard and role word show an ordinary owner when it runs (a match binding may hold a loan); otherwise the target retains a copy as before. The target must have its own frame guard. GC lowering is unchanged.
- [RC-ELEMENT-TAKE-1] A match arm that `COW-INDEX-RETURN-1` proves a same-slot return (`match objects[i]: case K(p): f = p.field; change(mut f); objects[i] = K(..., f, ...)` on the first use of a never-rebound `mut` list parameter, at an unchanged index, with nothing between the arm's entry and the element write reading the list, the binding or the index other than the binding's scalar fields and the one extraction, every continuing path ending in that write and neither temporary surviving it) binds its payload by moving it out of the element instead of retaining a copy, so the extracted field (RC-DEAD-FIELD-MOVE-1) can hold the only owner and its mutation needs no clone. The element keeps its tag with zero payload bytes, every RC value's empty cleanup form, until the write replaces it: a trap in between tears the heap down (RC-TRAP-TEARDOWN-1) and the write's release of the old element releases nothing. The move happens only when, as the arm runs, the scrutinee is the element's own storage (not a private loan copy) and the list has one owner and no shared, arena or immortal mark; otherwise the arm retains a copy as before. A borrowed dispatch, an in-place anchored binding, a narrowed Option binding, a binding held by reference and a payload without counted edges or with a unique resource keep the ordinary binding. GC lowering is unchanged.
- [RC-FIELD-INOUT-1] Unaccounted RC native lowering may refine an existing closed inline-record field transfer into a direct inout field pointer when the target has one definition, one mut reference and no other reads except the unconditional store-back. The straight closed interval contains exactly one direct guest call with that record as its sole declared mut parameter, plain other operands and a plain result; callbacks, reachable indirect/host calls, parent reads or rebinds, branches, escapes and nonplain argument evaluation retain the original transfer. The original parent field stays owning and rooted, the ghost target never owns, and all normal callee COW, rebinding, allocation and trap lowering remains authoritative. Step-counted execution, tracing GC and VM bytecode are unchanged.

- [RC-ITERATION-HOLD-TRANSFER-1] An entry-snapshot iteration source remains an observer when a named field transfer would change its stored value. A D1 live place reads its root on each step, so field transfers preserve independent values while record-field write-back stays visible to the loop.

- [RC-PLAIN-FIELD-LOAN-1] A checked list read through a certified stable receiver may project at most eight immediately adjacent inline-record fields to a plain scalar without acquiring owners of unused sibling fields. Every intermediate operand remains physically borrowed; branch entry, escaped aggregates, callbacks, unique resources and live-view records refuse this optimization.
- [RC-ADJACENT-VARIANT-READ-1] Experimental RC may let a checked list read lend its inline variant directly to an immediately adjacent ordinary match when a concrete retained list token protects the source through complete selected-payload retention. Bounds, tag and step checks remain; payload ownership commits before old arm-local cleanup. Unknown storage, intervening ownership effects and transitively unique, opaque, callable or live-view payloads keep ordinary lowering.
- [RC-SCALAR-LENGTH-BODY-1] The canonical scalar-result read-only body proof may read list lengths as well as list elements. It retains bounds and step/frame admission checks while borrowing managed local bits from caller inputs for the activation; mutation, allocation, callbacks and owning results remain excluded.

- [RC-FRESH-ROOT-HOIST-1] A bounded fresh-allocation and physical-release proof may extend RC loop-entry uniqueness to plain-element append and plain terminal construction, retaining real owner journals and fallible growth. Shared roots, retained aliases, prior enclosing-loop publications, callbacks, rebinding and region transitions retain their original checks; zero-iteration entries cannot introduce allocation through these new hoists.

- [RC-PLAIN-APPEND-1] An RC append of a plain one-word scalar may write directly only with spare capacity, incrementable generation and a registered zero-edge native binding with no pending finalizer. Capture the scalar before mutation. Cold metadata, pending callbacks, growth, overflow and nonplain values keep the original publication and failure path; ownership, generation and region semantics are unchanged. A bounded closed scalar-loop certificate may reuse a successful readiness answer in separately accounted storage, reset on every external entry and invalidated before every slow append; capacity, generation, bounds and failure checks remain per operation.
- [RC-PLAIN-APPEND-2] A plain inline aggregate of at most 64 bytes may use the existing generated-glue append room path directly in native IL. The same spare-capacity, nonfrozen-generation and source-outside-live-backing guards select an exact byte copy followed by length and generation increments. Every other case calls the original generated glue, and counted or unique-drop aggregates remain on their ownership path. This does not eliminate allocation or alter publication behavior.
- [RC-PLAIN-POP-1] RC tail removal of a plain value of 1 through 64 bytes may copy and clear the last slot directly only when the list is nonempty, its generation can increment, its native binding is already registered and owns no edge or unique obligation, and no finalizer is pending. Cold metadata, pending callbacks, managed values, empty lists and frozen generations keep the original runtime path. Receiver uniqueness, arena lifetimes, failure reporting and iterator invalidation remain unchanged.
- [RC-OPAQUE-EXTEND-1] Growing a large RC storage block in place leaves the new capacity unwritten when the element's registered binding has no owning leaf and no unique drop (its flat plan is empty), exactly as the collector's opaque extension: no release or drop ever walks those bytes and every element is written before it can be read. Any other element, or one without a registered binding, still receives zero bytes, the empty form its cleanup walk expects.
- [RC-PRESIZED-APPEND-1] Inside a versioned loop whose facts presize its one plain scalar append (`QBE-LOOP-VERSION-1`), an RC build uses the same check-free room write as the collector when the loop also carries the quiet scalar append-loop certificate of `RC-PLAIN-APPEND-1`. The guard, after its capacity step, asks the runtime once whether the list may take direct room writes and requires the list generation below 2^62, so no append in the loop can reach the frozen generation; either refusal runs the original loop with its per-append admission. This already-certified guard selects the identical hot readiness alias to force LLVM inlining, without changing the predicate or proof. The copy then stores each element and bumps the length and generation with no call, flag or per-append check. An uncertified loop keeps its ordinary RC append and so its single original copy.
- [RC-APPEND-RANGE-1] An already certified quiet scalar append loop whose root traces through at most eight exact handoffs to an untouched empty constructor may use its two planned copies for scalar-range specialization instead of a presized copy whose room guard cannot hold. Its guard retains the existing ordered scalar range conditions and refuses uncertain, reference, caller-storage, collection-dependent or effectful values; uncertainty selects the complete original checked loop without trapping or allocating. Both copies retain ordinary per-append capacity checks, readiness admission and invalidation, growth, allocation refusal, cleanup, accounting and publication in the original order, with no receiver or backing pointer cached across allocation. Only scalar checks proved by the existing facts are omitted. Existing instruction/node/work limits still apply, and a bounded lowered-call audit allows only the original scalar append, its readiness query and cold trap helpers; the general allocating-copy and operand-isolation vetoes remain unchanged for all other loops. Nonempty, used, aliased, incoming and presized roots retain their existing policy.
- [RC-FRESH-APPEND-GENERATION-1] Inside a certified quiet scalar append loop (`RC-PLAIN-APPEND-1`) the per-append room test omits the frozen-generation comparison: the certificate's root is a freshly allocated list, which never carries the frozen generation and cannot count up to it. Capacity, admission and every other append keep their checks.

- [RC-NONHELD-CAPTURE-1] A bounded exact named-source load/store proof may refine the function-wide possibility of terminal held list tokens only when canonical publication, all writers and the independent whole-local empty-token lifetime are established. Parameters, repeated source definitions, pattern/ref writers, borrowed entries and uncertain inputs retain normal handling. Actual iterator/view holds, source owners, clone checks, semantic history, callback/trap behavior and physical counters remain unchanged.

- [RC-UNIQUE-CAPTURE-1] Ordinary RC may omit only the second uniqueness check at an adjacent scalar-list mutation receiver capture when the exact first check prepares the same named ordinary root and finalized independent input and whole-local proofs establish a raw load and physically empty capture. Both instructions retain their positions and accounting; the first check retains its failure provenance. Alternate entries, aliases, descriptor or storage mismatches, publication, hidden physical effects, arena programs and speculative paths keep ordinary lowering. GC, RC counting and RC site instrumentation retain all original checks and events.
- [RC-INDEX-UPDATE-1] Ordinary unaccounted RC may reuse a successful canonical i64-list read's normalized private integer index for one same-root scalar write, and forward that exact stored i64 value to a following same-root read. The first read's normalization, bounds checks and error details and the original COW detach and failure position remain. Scalar detachment preserves list length and element bits; scalar writes preserve length, storage and generation. No interior pointer crosses detachment. A bounded closed interval fixes the physical root, descriptor and stable source index, excludes outside entries after the first receiver initialization, and retains receiver cleanup and every instruction's ownership result protocol. Only the following receiver capture may have an unchanged i64/u64/bool/unit operand prefix; its full token protocol remains. Rebinding, resizing, references, caller storage, callbacks, publication, other physical ownership work, managed elements, unsupported widths and speculative emission refuse. Accounted, GC, counting, site and arena builds keep original lowering.

- [RC-PLAIN-RECORD-WRITE-1] Experimental RC may publish a direct plain scalar field into an initialized caller-owned inline record when the complete RHS precedes an exact effect-free capture/update/store-back tail. Every use and definition of the compiler capture must prove its token stays empty, including across backedges. Only the original final store publishes the scalar; managed siblings retain their existing owners and marks, and any intervening step trap leaves the record unchanged. Unknown aliases, planned ownership effects, managed replacements, unique contents and live views retain general lowering.
- [RC-NAMED-RECORD-WRITE-1] The same exact scalar tail may address a private named inline record only after a bounded whole-root CFG proof converges to initialized ordinary ownership at its entry. Canonical definitions, rebindings, planned cleanup and arena watermarks participate in the proof. Every mutable reference must have one traced direct typed caller-storage consumer; unknown publishers, escaping references, moves, held or borrowed homes and unsupported effects retain general lowering. The original final store remains the only scalar publication.

- [RC-DICTIONARY-TAIL-1] Experimental RC may omit a duplicate receiver uniqueness check and temporary pin scope only for an exact fully evaluated dictionary write tail with independently proved empty capture and input guards across all backedges. Preserve the original first uniqueness, ordinary root/key/value journals, semantic publication, runtime write/drain/failure behavior, frame and step admission. Uncertain roles, hidden writers, callbacks inside the tail, borrowed entries, managed values and held receivers retain general lowering.

- [OWNERSHIP-FIELD-WRITE-1] Borrow and semantic-copy elision must account for mutations through compiler-created record-field receivers even when those receivers own their temporary values. Follow bounded known temporary origins without changing their ownership roles; unknown or cyclic mutation ancestry cannot prove a borrow safe. Named independent copied homes stop this ancestry, and read-only field access retains borrow opportunities in heap and arena code.

- [OWNERSHIP-MUT-RECORD-WRITE-1] A `mut` record argument (`f(mut r)`, the caller's variable passed by reference) counts as a write of the whole tree rooted at that variable for borrow and semantic-copy elision, because the callee may write any field or collection inside it. A local that borrowed the record or any part of it (`before = r`, `items = r.items`) and is still read after the call keeps a real copy, so the call's writes never show through it under either runtime. Path locals keep their existing receiver rules.

- [OWNERSHIP-MUT-RECORD-WRITE-2] The OWNERSHIP-MUT-RECORD-WRITE-1 write takes effect when the direct call receiving the `mut` record runs its callee, not where the reference is pushed. A borrow (a borrowed local or an elided match binding) live at the reference only because a later argument of the same call reads it (`match rt.objects[i]: case Obj.instance(instance): bind(mut rt, instance.klass)`) stays a borrow when it is dead on entry to that call, the code from the reference to the call is straight-line, and every such read projects the borrow at once, through field reads, domain wraps and value copies, to a plain scalar: the scalar is read before the callee runs, so no value sharing the borrow's storage reaches the callee or crosses the call. A read that passes the borrow itself, a collection or any other aggregate part of it (`keep(mut r, before.items)`, `hold(mut r, before)`), any other use of it, a branch between the reference and the call, an arena boundary, a consumer that is not a direct call, or a borrow still read after the call keeps the real copy. Conversely a borrow whose collection-holding value (itself, a field or an element of it) is already on the operand stack when the reference is pushed (`hold(box.names, mut state)` with `box = state.boxes[0]`) is a real copy too, although the borrow itself is dead there: the callee would receive, or the caller keep across the call, storage the callee writes.
- [OWNERSHIP-MUT-RECORD-WRITE-3] The OWNERSHIP-MUT-RECORD-WRITE-2 rule that a borrow whose collection-holding value is already on the operand stack at a write is a real copy holds for every write of the borrow's root tree, not only a `mut` record reference: a `mut` collection argument (`hold(box.names, mut state.boxes)` with `box = state.boxes[0]`), a mutating method through a path (`lst(box.names, state.boxes[0].names.pop())`) and a `mut` argument of the root itself (`lst(ys, push(mut xs))` with `ys = xs`). The value on the stack is read after the write by its consumer, so the borrow must be a value of its own although it is dead there; the operands the writing instruction itself consumes are read at the write and do not count. Both runtimes print the value the borrow had (`1`, not the collector's former `2`).

- [RC-DICTIONARY-KEY-1] A bounded physical certificate may lend an immutable builtin string from a stable named list owner through a closed dictionary get/write interval. It proves the complete capture and key local lifetimes remain token-free, tags the final tail with explicit anchor authority, preserves real iterator holds and all owner journals, and retains first uniqueness, terminal write, step/trap and region failures. Uncertain writers, callbacks, alias mutation, alternate entry, scope transitions or middle CFG entries keep ordinary lowering.

- [RC-DICTIONARY-LOOP-1] A direct fresh empty dictionary may reuse one real loop-entry uniqueness check only with a bounded whole-root lifetime and CFG proof composed with an anchored immutable key tail. Preserve owner journals, original body site metadata, terminal write/growth/refusal behavior and loop-version eligibility. Shared or uncertain entries, snapshots, rebinding, calls, retained views, arena transitions and interior entries retain per-write uniqueness.
- [RC-DICTIONARY-TEXT-LOOP-1] The fresh scalar-dictionary loop proof also admits ordinary owning immutable text keys and bounded text slicing, length, shares and drops whose physical types cannot contain its root or run a destructor. Preserve every key owner/hold journal and terminal runtime write; this certificate grants no cached-storage loop or empty-drop authority. Root publication, callbacks, references, arena transitions, unknown shapes and exhausted proofs retain per-write uniqueness.
- [RC-DICTIONARY-EMPTY-DROPS-1] A completed fresh-dictionary loop certificate may omit only exact zero-token cleanup consumers after proving its canonical constructor-store reset and every physical publication through the backedge. Keep all journal storage, original native calls, scope/frame/trap handling and setup/later owning uses. Uncertain guard users retain ordinary emission; unexpected publication after completed proof is an invariant failure.

- [RC-READ-LIST-VERSION-1] A bounded read-only scalar-list loop may reuse the existing guarded range/storage version only with initialized ordinary O or terminal H journal anchors and no mutation, callback, ownership cleanup, capture or arena transition; refusal retains the checked original loop, step accounting remains unversioned, and trap cleanup retains the original tokens.
- [RC-READ-LIST-VERSION-2] The read-only list loop version also admits a list the caller anchors — a read-only list parameter the function never rebinds, which the caller owns for the whole activation — with no runtime token test, and a compiler temporary every store of which is an adjacent capture `load_local P; store_local T` of one such list or journal-anchored local that the loop never writes: its reads resolve to P's anchor. Plain one-word values that own nothing (integers, `bool`, unit and plain domains such as `float`) count as scalars, inlined instructions are admitted under the same rules, and the copy may call the heap-free scalar float and integer helpers besides cold traps. Everything else keeps the RC-READ-LIST-VERSION-1 refusals and the original checked loop.
- [RC-READ-BYTE-ITERATION-1] Immutable byte-string iteration may use the existing guarded read-loop copy under the same initialized ordinary O or terminal H journal anchor, caller anchor, capture, physical quietness and range conditions as read-only lists. The byte storage and length remain stable throughout a nonallocating read-only loop; mutations of byte buffers, callbacks, ownership transitions and step accounting retain the original loop. The copy removes only guards established by its range facts, so an unbounded XOR/add reduction keeps its arithmetic overflow check and exact original trap behavior.
- [QBE-BYTE-REDUCTION-1] A complete canonical immutable-byte addition or XOR/add iteration, already admitted by the RC read/storage/range certificate, may call a heap-free unsigned byte reduction when its mask is in 0..255, its positive trip count times 255 fits signed i64, and its initial sum has enough upper headroom. Nonnegative increments cannot underflow; the guards prove every prefix addition succeeds. The bulk result preserves the final sum, counter and iteration value. Any guard or certificate refusal keeps the checked original loop, including exact arithmetic trap positions, details and prior effects. Mutable buffers, callbacks, extra body operations, aliased scalar storage, interior entry edges and step/frame accounting refuse bulk reduction.
- [QBE-LOOP-UNSWITCH-1] A checked single-entry counted loop may specialize its complete set of at most 16 source-certified equality literals of one unwritten, unaliased i64 selector under pure pre-loop equality guards. Each folded comparison must have no incoming edge to its literal, comparison or conditional-jump instruction: joined short-circuit and conditional operands retain their original decisions. The native pass clones the original control-flow graph, folds only source-certified conditional decisions and prunes unreachable blocks; it retains all executed arithmetic, bounds, cold failure, ownership, callback, allocation, cleanup and original-position operations. Other selector values enter the original checked loop. Zero-trip loops still execute no body effects. Step/frame accounting, escaping selectors, interior native/source entries, unsupported CFGs, excessive literals/conditions or analysis work retain the original whole loop. Aggregate copied text must fit all three general budgets: 256KiB absolute, twice the original loop region, and half the whole function input (with a 4KiB allowance for small functions); any excess refuses the complete specialization.
- [RC-READONLY-LOOP-VERSION-1] A raw readonly RC body (`RC-READONLY-BODY-1`) emits no token, journal or count traffic, so its planned loops keep the collector's guarded check-free versions under the same emitted-call audit, with no RC admission of their own. Every other RC activation keeps its RC admissions.
- [RC-READ-RECORD-VERSION-1] The read-only list loop version also admits lists of plain inline values — records, tuples and variants that own nothing and name no live view — and lets such values sit on the stack and in locals, be stored, loaded and projected with `get_field` inside the interval: they are bytes copied into frame storage or read in place, with no token, journal or count. Counted, unique, managed and view-bearing values keep the refusal.

- [RC-READONLY-MANAGED-CALLS-1] A scalar readonly body may call a managed-return helper only through an exact saved per-call public borrowed-entry certificate. Every result edge remains anchored by original activation inputs or immortals. One bounded root proof shares all managed-query limits; final physical publication/scope/history effects veto elision. Raw callers select the alternate before frame lookup and reject missing or inconsistent routes internally; canonical and indirect managed-result ABIs remain owned. Cache identity includes transitive public certificates and refusal. Complete returned aggregate bits must remain in caller storage across later calls and loop reuse.

- [RC-READ-BYTES-VERSION-1] An RC guarded fast loop may omit an immutable-byte GET_INDEX receiver drop only for an exact raw-load/index-load/read triple with a live ordinary concrete owner and an actually empty operand guard checked at every external loop entry. The scalar-only quiet body preserves that empty guard across backedges. Keep the original drop, normalization, bounds/trap and cleanup path as fallback; mutable or held sources, aliases, suffix entries and unknown effects refuse the optimization.

- [RC-RECORD-FIELD-READ-1] A bounded closed scalar-list field read may borrow from a proved initialized private named record home. Prove every explicit and implicit capture writer, incoming CFG edge and physical ownership effect; retain frame storage and mandatory step, bounds, arithmetic and arena failures. Unknown homes, aliases, callbacks, managed results and non-failure emitted calls retain complete ordinary publication. Temporary loan counts and their implementation-only hold-limit admission may disappear; all retained owners and their limits remain unchanged.
- [RC-CALLER-FIELD-READ-1] A canonical inline-record inout parameter may lend an ordinary scalar-list field through a proved closed checked-read interval. Its suspended caller provides lifetime, not ordinary ownership or uniqueness. Keep bounds/frame/step traps and all storage; refuse callbacks, mutations, independent cleanup, arena changes and unproved parameter authority. Removed temporary holds need not retain their former implementation count-limit admission.

- [RC-BORROWED-READONLY-ENTRY-1] Only an exact public borrowed-entry certificate plus bounded physical readonly composition may omit an alternate entry's empty ownership journal. Keep canonical owned ABI, exact managed call routes, independent query limits, return-storage materialization before heap-frame release, trap and frame checks, and versioned proof/refusal cache identity. Unknown effects, recursive managed roots and error-trace return materialization refuse the optional optimization.

- [RC-IMMORTAL-ENTRY-1] Independently bounded constant-result certificates select distinct immortal entries only through exact saved readonly call routes; canonical results remain owned and physical preflight, typed literal authority, source/cache identity, step/frame failure and complete result storage remain authoritative.

## Compile-time file embedding

- [EMBED-1] `embed_bytes("path")` answers `bytes` and `embed_str("path")` answers `str`, both read from a project file while the program loads, so the data is immutable static content of the binary. They are prelude builtins that a binding, declaration or module name may shadow. Each takes exactly one plain string literal (a variable, `b"..."`, a second argument or a computed path is `E_EMBED_ARGUMENT`), and `embed_str` requires valid UTF-8 (`E_EMBED_UTF8` names the offending byte offset). A call needs no capability parameter and contributes no effect, not even `managed_allocation`, so it is legal under `noio`, `noalloc` and `effects()`, in generic bodies, and as the whole initializer of a module `const` whose type is `bytes` for `embed_bytes` and `str` for `embed_str`; reading that constant, locally or through an import, loads static data and allocates nothing. Every byte is kept, including NUL, and an empty file is an empty value. Native embedded objects are ordered by semantic symbol in both whole-program emission and module linking. Behavior is identical under reference counting, `--gc-runtime` and `--release`.
- [EMBED-2] An embedded path is relative to the directory of the source file spelling the call and uses `/`. Absolute paths are `E_EMBED_ABSOLUTE`, an empty path or one holding NUL or a backslash is `E_EMBED_PATH`, and a path that leaves the project root through `..` or through a symbolic link is `E_EMBED_ESCAPE`, decided before the target is probed. The project root is the directory of the nearest `mylang.json` above the entry, else the entry's own import directory. A missing file is `E_EMBED_MISSING`, a directory or other non-regular file `E_EMBED_DIRECTORY`, an unreadable file `E_EMBED_UNREADABLE`, a file over 16 MiB `E_EMBED_TOO_LARGE`, and a source that was not loaded from a directory `E_EMBED_UNAVAILABLE`; each is located at the string literal. A symbolic link that stays inside the root is followed.
- [EMBED-3] The loader finds every `embed_bytes("...")` and `embed_str("...")` call while it reads a module's header, reads each file once with the project provider, and records its outcome and BLAKE3 content digest on the module's source, so the checker only looks results up and a failed read is an ordinary diagnostic. That digest enters every key that covers the module: the configured identity the frontend verdict, in-memory verdict, run-manifest and emit-replay caches key on; the dependency context of each declaration whose region holds an embed, which keys checked bodies, check summaries, stand-in units and stored fragments; the implementation hash of a native test; and the test snapshot's resolved identity and materialized copy, where captured files sit at the paths their relative references need. Editing only an embedded file therefore rechecks and re-emits exactly the declarations that embed it, an unchanged file reuses everything, and deleting or replacing a file with a directory changes the keys as well.
- [EMBED-4] An embedded file is lowered to a `make_record` operation naming its digest and length, not to one node or instruction per byte, and to one static object in the native build: the emitted manifest carries an `embeds` table with each distinct file's payload once, keyed by kind and content digest, the metadata C unit defines each as a frozen `bytes` or `str` object exactly as a literal of the same content is laid out (the payload as one C string literal), and a unit loads it with one `loadl` of the object's symbol, so the payload appears in no unit text and is not scanned or hashed by the text passes, the fragment store or the object keys of the units. Both native backends use that table. Work is linear in the file's size.
- [EMBED-5] A `project-dependencies-v1` receipt lists every embedded file as `{module, path, status, blake3, bytes}` beside the module inputs and `--explain-invalidation` reports an edit to one at the `load_source` stage. `mylang fmt` leaves an embed call as written, and `mylang check` and `check-session` report embed diagnostics and never replay a verdict across an edit, deletion or replacement of an embedded file. An interface file (`mylang api`) prints an embedded constant as its initializer text, `embed_bytes("path")`, never the file's bytes, so editing the file leaves the interface unchanged.

## Typing papercuts

- [DISCARD-NAME-1] `_` is the discard name and never shadows anything. A `for` target (`for _ in ...`, `for _, x in ...`, `for _, _ in ...`, the second name of `enumerate`, `zip` and dictionary items included), a comprehension element name and a `with ... as _` scope may bind `_` again inside another scope that binds it, so `for _ in range(2):` around `for _ in range(3):` is legal, and a loop that discards both of its names binds nothing. The inner `_` hides the outer one for its own scope and the outer one is visible again after it. `_` does not become an error to read where it is bound today, and a `match` payload of `_` still binds nothing. Every other name keeps the rule: a `for` variable, comprehension element or payload binding may not reuse an active local or top-level name (`E_SHADOWING`), because loop variables are block-scoped.
- [OPTION-EQ-1] In `left == right` and `left != right`, a `Some(...)`, `None`, `Ok(...)` or `Err(...)` operand that has no expected type takes the type of the other operand, in either order and whatever that operand is (a call, a method call, a conversion such as `int(text)`, a field or a local), in ordinary and generic function bodies. The other operand is checked first; the typed comparison keeps the source order and operands still evaluate left to right. When both operands wait for a type (`Some(1) == Some(1)`) nothing is inferred and `E_OPTION_INFERENCE` or `E_RESULT_INFERENCE` still reports it; a payload of the wrong type (`half(4) == Some("a")`) is `E_TYPE`. `x is None` and `x == None` agree on a known `Option`. Each equality link in a comparison chain uses the same peer inference, checking a shared operand once and retaining left-to-right runtime evaluation and short-circuiting, in ordinary and generic bodies.
- [CONST-METHOD-1] `NAME.method(args)` on a module constant declared `str` or `bytes` is checked as the same method on a local bound to the constant: the constant is read once through its getter and the method applies to that value, in ordinary and generic function bodies, for an `embed_bytes`/`embed_str` constant, for a private constant of the same module and for a public one reached through an import (`alias.NAME.method(args)`). The method vocabulary, arities, diagnostics and effects are the local receiver's: a literal constant contributes `managed_allocation`, an embedded one none. A constant of another type has no methods.
- [CONST-FLOAT-1] A module `const` may be declared `float` (or an alias of it), with an initializer that is a float literal, an integer literal (which takes the float type from the declaration), another visible constant, a parenthesized expression, unary `-`, or arithmetic and comparison over those. The constant folds at check time and every read is the same wrapped binary64 literal a float literal checks to, in ordinary and generic bodies, through an import and under `noalloc`. `-0.0` keeps its sign, an overflowing literal is `E_FLOAT_RANGE`, a non-float initializer for a `float` constant (or a float one for an `int` constant) is `E_TYPE`, and the interface listing prints the initializer as written.
- [CONST-FLOAT-2] A constant initializer folds float operations exactly as the checked program computes them, so a constant and the same expression evaluated at run time are the same bits (a NaN is the canonical NaN).
